# Dashboard spec — `dashboard.html`

## Behaviour added in v0.17

- **Auto-apply** (Settings, default on): every save — inline, dialog, New, flags, Pending review decisions — is applied immediately through the live server, with Undo in the snackbar. Off: Apply buttons (toolbar and app-bar chip) return.
- **Owner picker** (combobox) on actions and RAID: existing names (you first, with item counts), type to filter, "Add … as a new owner", × removes a name from the list (items keep it). The list lives in the people register (see "People (BL-55, BL-56)" at the end).
- **Filters:** Owner is multi-select (search, Only me, Select all, Clear); Status adds **Due soon** (`review.due_soon_days`, default 7) for actions and RAID, and Delayed for actions.
- **Client messaging:** single field, always the three headings; "client msg to do" shown only on client-visible items; clicking the client flag on incomplete messaging opens the dialog with client-visible ticked and sets the flag only when saved complete (each line ≥ 5 letters or digits).
- **Pending review:** every proposal is editable (all fields) with Save or Save and approve; no copy-command buttons.
- **Lists:** frames grow to 50 rows before scrolling inside; headers pin under the app bar; column widths draggable and remembered per list (Settings › General › Appearance › Column widths and order › Reset).
- **Footer:** "Data as of <last apply or rebuild>" · live status · unapplied count, small and muted, on every page; nothing in the app bar.
- **Help:** Settings › Help and documentation, `?` (on the tab used last), or ⌘K; three tabs, Start here, How do I… and Reference, all from `system/templates/help.md` (BL-84).
- **Shortcuts:** `/` also opens ask creaite.
- **Excel:** `tracker-view.xlsx` is re-exported immediately on every apply (so on every auto-applied save), pausing only while unimported workbook edits exist.

## Added in v0.18

- **Settings** (one page with six categories; see Settings page below): **You** (name, short forms, own email domains); **Opening views** (new action priority, Actions and RAID status filters used on open and Reset, Actions sort: overdue first / priority / due date); **People** and **Workstreams** managers — counts per item type, Rename and Merge into (applied to every item in one apply, with Undo and a change-log entry per item), Remove from list / Retire (hidden from pickers; items keep the value), Restore, Add.
- **IDs:** RAID items are `<CODE>-NNN`; actions `<CODE>-A-NNN`; decisions `<CODE>-D-NNN`.
- **Decisions:** a Source column (short label, full text on hover); Record section in the edit dialog; a source is required for new decisions.
- **Layout:** content up to 1680px wide; minimum widths for description and owner columns, compact ID, type, impact, likelihood and edit columns; "due soon" and "late" labels under the date. If a table is still wider than its frame, it scrolls sideways **inside** the frame — headers and rows never overhang the border.
- **Files:** project nodes in the tree collapse and expand on click (▸/▾), collapsed by default, remembered per browser; opening a project's files from elsewhere expands it.

## Added in v0.19

- **Reminders and notices** (weekly report, client reports, sample/notice banners) appear **on Portfolio only**. Each has × with **Dismiss for this session** (until the dashboard is next opened) or **Dismiss forever** (that occurrence only; stored in `settings.json` `reminders.dismissed_forever`). Undo in the snackbar; Settings › Reminders › Restore brings them back. Turning a reminder type off stays a Settings switch.
- The **live-server alert** is the exception: it stays on every page, because edits cannot be applied while it is showing.
- **Files:** Copy path removed; each file keeps its name link and Open folder.

## Added in v0.23

- **Dates:** shown and typed as DD-MM-YYYY everywhere in the dashboard (lists, panel, calendar picker, Today, cockpits, Files, Activity, sources, reminders, ask creaite); stored YYYY-MM-DD; file names keep YYYY-MM-DD. Typing YYYY-MM-DD is rejected with "Dates must be DD-MM-YYYY."
- **Columns:** the Columns menu offers ID and Source on every list (plus Type and Workstream on RAID); off by default; remembered.
- **Decisions:** plain client messaging (no headings), required when making a decision client-visible or editing it; flagged in lists and the cockpit when missing.
- **Click outside** closes ask creaite and the detail panel; never while the panel is in edit mode (message and a small shake instead). Clicking another row switches the panel.
- **Start command:** `cd <vault> && python3 system/scripts/live_server.py` with a Copy button in Help (Live server and backups), Settings and the red alert; the build fills in the real vault path.

## v0.22 redesign (adopted) — supersedes earlier sections where they conflict

**Reference:** `dev/reference/dashboard-reference.html` is the **single source of design truth** — one working file with every view and component: Today, Projects and cockpits, Work (saved views, owner filter, column chooser, resizing, 50-row frames), the detail panel (view/edit, owner picker, calendar, client messaging, sources), Review triage, Files, Activity, Settings (Preferences and Data admin with People and Workstreams managers), Help, ask creaite, the command palette, live-server alert and held saves, and auto-apply with Undo. Where this spec and the reference differ, the reference wins on look and behaviour.

**Information architecture:** a fixed compact rail with labels — **Today · Projects · Work · Review · Files**, with **Activity** and **Settings** at the bottom (Help via `?` and Settings). Replaces the 10-item collapsible rail, the pin and the separate Actions / My actions / RAID / Decisions / Project / Pending review views.

**Today (home):** a brief, in one centred column: see "Today as a brief (BL-72)" at the end of this file. (Before BL-72: a date headline, reminders as notes, and three columns for Needs your attention, Your commitments and Changed since your last visit.)

**Projects → cockpit:** one screen per project — health with reason, evidence figures and trend; milestone timeline with today and slips marked; actions due or overdue; top 3 risks with their responses; recent decisions; current state (editable); Draft weekly report / Draft client report.

**Work:** tabs **Actions · RAID · Decisions**; saved views (Actions: All open, Mine, Due soon, Chase, Delayed, Overdue, Closed; All open, Mine, Due soon and Chase are status Open only, Delayed is its own view, Overdue holds Open and Delayed; RAID: All open, Urgent and high, No response yet, Mine; Decisions: All, Last 14 days) plus the multi-select owner filter and a Columns menu (show, hide, reorder). Lists are **title-first** (project code and workstream beneath), with few columns; IDs and secondary text live in the detail panel.

**Editing (replaces click-to-edit cells and the centred dialog; for actions, RAID items and decisions itself replaced by the peek, BL-176, below):** a single click selects a row and opens a **non-modal detail panel** on the right; the list stays visible. **E**, the Edit button, or a **double-click on a cell** switches the panel into a **visibly different edit mode** (half the window wide, an "Editing" banner, form fields, Save and Cancel; the ground stays the panel's normal surface, BL-80), focusing that field. **Save** applies immediately (auto-apply) with Undo in the snackbar. Every field from the previous edit dialog is in the panel, including client messaging, owner picker and source.

**Review (replaces Pending review):** grouped by project; keyboard triage — **J/K** move, **A** approve, **R** reject, **E** edit (opens the proposal in the detail panel), **X** select; batch Approve/Reject selected; Approve all routine; items needing individual approval are labelled and excluded from batch and routine approval.

**Activity (new):** the change log, newest first — when, what changed, by whom (you in the dashboard, an approval, Claude Code, the assistant) — with Undo per change (reverses that apply).

**Visual rules:** calm, dense desktop layout (36–48px controls, 14px body); colour only for exceptions — red for Urgent, overdue and off track, amber for at risk; priority otherwise neutral text; one quiet tag style; no floating action buttons ("New" in the toolbar; ask creaite in the top bar and `/`). Material 3 colour and type tokens retained with M3 density guidance applied.

**Status:** the live status sits at the bottom of the rail and the sweep and data times in its tooltip (BL-71; they were in the top bar). The red live-server alert is unchanged.

**Settings:** one page, with the categories on the left and the settings on the right (General, Work and views, Health and warnings, Capture and alerts, People and projects, System), and a search box. See Settings page (BL-115) below; it replaced the Preferences and Data admin tabs.

**Shortcuts added:** J/K, E, A, R, X (list and Review); existing ⌘K/Ctrl+K, `/`, ⌘J/Ctrl+J, `?`, Esc unchanged.

## Added in v0.20 — sources

Wherever a source is shown (Pending review dialogs and "Needs a project", Record sections of action, RAID and decision dialogs, Decisions list):
- **File sources** (minutes, transcripts, documents): the file name is a link that opens the file from the vault through the live server; section and folder shown beneath.
- **Email sources:** Email from, Subject, Date and time (DD-MM-YYYY HH:MM), plus **Open in Outlook** (stored Microsoft 365 message link).
- **Teams sources:** channel or chat, From, Date and time, plus **Open in Teams**.
- Short labels in lists: the file name, "Email · <subject>" or "Teams · <channel>".

## Design system: Material 3

The dashboard follows **Google Material 3** (m3.material.io). The reference design (`dev/reference/dashboard-reference.html`) is the source of truth for look and behaviour.

- **Colour:** M3 colour roles generated from creaite's deep-teal seed (primary, secondary, tertiary and error, each with containers; five surface-container levels), in light and dark. Body = surface, navigation = surface container. Health uses custom success and warning roles harmonised to the seed, always shown as **icon plus text**, never colour alone.
- **Type and shape:** the M3 type scale in Roboto (bundled locally in `system/templates/vendor/`, Apache-2.0; system fonts as fallback). M3 corner tokens: 4px for fields, 8px for chips, 12px for cards, 28px for dialogs and search, fully round buttons. Elevation is tonal; shadows only on floating elements.
- **Navigation:** a **collapsible navigation rail** (icons only, tooltips, a badge on Pending review). The ☰ button expands it temporarily over the page (Esc, clicking outside or choosing a view collapses it). The **pin** keeps it open as a drawer and is remembered per browser. Under 900px wide, the pinned drawer floats.
- **App bar:** an M3 search bar opening the **command palette (⌘K / Ctrl+K)** for views, projects, item IDs and commands; **ask creaite**; status chips (data as of, pending review, awaiting apply, live); theme.
- **Components:** filled, tonal, outlined and text buttons; extended FAB for New (Actions, RAID, Decisions); filter chips (client); a "You" chip on owner cells you own; snackbar with **Undo**; **centred M3 dialog** for edit and new forms; M3 switches and outlined fields in Settings; the calendar styled as an M3 docked date picker (DD-MM-YYYY).
- **Tables:** always compact; sticky headers; sortable columns (click or Enter); a "Filtered" chip with Reset whenever filters are active.
- **Portfolio:** "Needs attention" cards (my overdue actions, due in the next 7 days, Urgent risks, waiting for review), then the health ledger.
- **Accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA):** targets of 32px or more (24px minimum); 3px focus ring; focus never hidden by sticky headers; keyboard access throughout; screen-reader names on icon buttons.
- **Shortcuts:** shown as ⌘ on Mac and Ctrl elsewhere.

## Edit dialog: all fields

- **Actions:**
  - **Details (editable):** description, project, workstream, owner (owner picker), priority, due date, status (Open, Delayed, Closed, Cancelled), raised, closed, latest update; client-visible and client messaging.
  - **Record (read-only):** ID, source, last updated.
  - **Rules:** Closed or Cancelled fills Closed with today; Open or Delayed clears it; Closed can't be before Raised.
- **RAID:**
  - **Details (editable):** description, type, project, workstream, owner, mitigation/resolution action (label follows type), impact, likelihood, due date (action target), status, raised, date closed; client-visible and client messaging.
  - **Priority** is shown and recalculated live; it is never typed.
  - **Record (read-only):** ID, source, last updated.
- **Moving projects:** an item moved to another project keeps its ID; the move is logged.

## Assistant ("ask creaite")

- **Opening it:** ⌘J / Ctrl+J, the app-bar button, the floating button (bottom right; it hides while the panel is open, and shows a dot when a task finished while the panel was closed), or the command palette. **Esc closes it**, unless an inline edit, the calendar, a dialog, the palette or the nav overlay takes Esc first.
- **The panel:**
  - a side panel with quick actions (Sweep, Process minutes, Weekly report, Review, Apply, What's overdue?);
  - streaming replies with step-by-step progress;
  - links back into dashboard views;
  - **confirmation cards before anything that writes**.
  - **the message box grows with what is typed (BL-145):** `#chIn` starts at one row and grows with its content (typing, Shift+Enter, a paste, wrapped lines) up to about half the panel's height (never more than half the window), then scrolls inside with the cursor kept in view; it shrinks as text is deleted and returns to one row after sending or clearing. `chFit()` sets the height on input, whenever code sets the value, when the panel opens or is resized, and on a theme or font change; the height changes at once (BL-136 never animates height). Attach and Send/Stop stay on the box's bottom edge; the `@` and `/` suggestions stay above it and are never taller than the room above (`--acmax`). Enter sends, Shift+Enter makes a new line; the drag-to-resize handle stays off. Code: `/* ===== BL-145 (fix-chat-box) ===== */`; tests: `dev/tests/test_chat_box.py`.
- **How it runs:** each message goes from the dashboard to `live_server.py` to Claude Code headless in the vault. The session ID is kept, so conversations continue.
- **If the live server is down:** the panel offers "Copy command" and "Check again" instead.

## Reference design

`dev/reference/dashboard-reference.html` is the approved look and behaviour, built with fictional sample data. Open it in Edge or Chrome. Where this spec and the reference differ, follow the reference for visual design and this spec for content and rules.

Key elements of the reference:
- **The Portfolio tab is a health ledger:** one row per project, showing a health bar, the reason, a countdown to the milestone, an 8-week health strip (from `updates`), and open, overdue and high-risk counts.
- **Header:** the data-as-of time, a pending count, and reminders.
- **Tags:** You (owner chip), TBC, client-visible, "client msg to do" (client-visible items only), due soon, stale.
- **Light and dark themes, with system fonts.** No external resources.

## Editing (option B; live mode required)

- **Vault access:** always connected. On first open, the browser asks once for access to the vault folder and remembers it (verified in Chrome on 2026-10-03, `dev/tests/folder-access-test.html`). If access is ever lost, show a one-time "Reconnect vault" prompt; there is no permanent connect button.
- **Inline editing** (Actions, RAID, Decisions, wherever those tables appear):
  - click a cell to edit;
  - descriptions, owners and "made by" are auto-growing multiline boxes;
  - dates use the creaite calendar picker (DD-MM-YYYY; never the browser's locale picker);
  - status, impact and likelihood are drop-downs that save on change, and priority recalculates;
  - the client toggle is in a Flags column (making an item client-visible opens the dialog until its client messaging is complete);
  - Enter or clicking away saves, Ctrl+Enter saves a description, Esc cancels;
  - validation happens as you leave a cell;
  - changed cells are highlighted, rows are tagged "awaiting apply", and there's a row-level undo before apply.
- **The Edit form** (per row) and **New action, New RAID item, New decision** forms use the same fields and validation.
- **Project:** "Edit project" covers health (a new reason is required when it changes), sponsor, milestone and milestone date. "Current state" is editable and written as a change to the project's `CLAUDE.md`. Other memory sections and the register stay with cx-edit.
- **Pending review:** Approve and Reject per item and draft (with undo before apply); a project picker for "Needs a project"; "Approve routine <CODE>" and "Approve all routine" follow the bulk rule; "Apply N changes" through the live server (if it is down, the red alert shows and saves wait).
- **Header:** pending count, an "Awaiting apply" count, and the live mode status.

## Settings tab

Every value in `system/config/settings.json` that the page shows (sweep, reminders, review, health criteria, dashboard, backups, live mode, and the Advanced ones) plus the client-report rhythm per project (written to each project's `CLAUDE.md`). **Saved instantly**, with a backup and a log entry. Times are HH:MM (24-hour). Not editable here: Microsoft 365, plugins, Outlook rules, Claude permissions and scheduled tasks (use "setup").

**Live mode (required):** shows status and port, plus Check now. The dashboard checks `/health` every 30 seconds. If the server is unreachable, a red alert appears at the top with the restart command, and the live chip turns red. Alan starts the server himself (`python3 system/scripts/live_server.py`).

## Principles

- **Served by the local live server** (127.0.0.1). It is also a self-contained file (CSS, JS, data and fonts inline or bundled; no CDN), so it still opens read-only, with an alert, if the server is down. Chrome or Edge, not Safari.
- **Never writes to the database.** Edits and decisions become proposals in staging; settings go to `settings.json`.
- **Generated.** `system/scripts/build_dashboard.py` queries `system/tracker.db`, embeds the data as `<script type="application/json" id="data">`, and writes `dashboard.html` plus `system/history/dashboard-YYYY-MM-DD.html`.
- **Clean and professional.** Neutral palette, one accent colour, system font stack, light and dark modes, works down to 1280px wide.
- **Escaping by default (BL-228 D).** New code builds markup with the `html` tagged template, never by concatenating strings around `esc()`: every `${…}` is escaped (`& < > " '`), arrays are joined with each element escaped the same way, `null`, `undefined` and `false` print nothing, another `html` result passes through unchanged, and `raw()` is only for markup the page builds itself (an `ic()` icon, a fragment already built with `html`), never for text from the tracker, a file, mail, Teams or Claude. The result goes straight into `innerHTML`. Existing `esc()` sinks move to `html` when touched (BL-228 D's first batch, then BL-231); `dev/tests/test_hostile_text.py` proves hostile text stays text on every screen.

## Header

- Title "Portfolio", plus "Data as of <last apply>" and "Built <timestamp>".
- A pending-review count badge.
- A warning banner if no apply has run for more than 7 days.

## Views (tabs)

1. **Portfolio**
   - One card per active project: code, name, health pill, health reason, next milestone with its date.
   - Counts on each card: open actions, overdue actions, open risks rated Urgent or High.
2. **Project**
   - Pick a project to see its card, open actions, open RAID, decisions from the last 30 days, the last 4 updates, the "Current state" paragraph from the project's `CLAUDE.md`, and links to the reports in that project's `reports/` folder.
3. **Actions**
   - All open actions, overdue first, then by due date.
   - Filters: project, owner, status.
   - Overdue rows are highlighted. A missing owner or due date shows a "TBC" tag.
4. **RAID**
   - Filters: type, project and priority.
   - A 3×3 impact × likelihood grid of open risks, with a count in each cell.
5. **My actions**
   - Open actions whose owner is Alan (settings `identity`), overdue first, across all projects (since 11-10-2026 Open only, as All open; Delayed actions are in the Delayed view).
6. **Decisions**
   - Log, newest first, with a text search.
7. **Files**
   - **What it is:** a file index built into the page by `build_dashboard.py` at every build. There's no live folder access and no content search (backlog BL-07).
   - **Tree:** All files, `inbox/`, `projects/_portfolio/` (`minutes`, `transcripts`), and each project's `minutes`, `transcripts`, `documents` (including Alan's own subfolders) and `reports`, each with a count. `system/` and `dev/` are excluded.
   - **Quick views:** All files, Latest reports (the newest weekly and client report per project), This week's meetings, Intake.
   - **Filters:** name, type, and dates typed as `DD-MM-YYYY` (never the browser's date picker, which uses the local format). Newest first.
   - **Each file has:** a type badge, location, date and size; a link to open it (relative path); and **Open folder**.
   - **Links in:** the Project view has "Browse all <CODE> files"; source references link to their files.
8. **Pending review** (read-only)
   - Pending batches and their items: source run, project, type, proposed values, match strength, flags, and age in days.
   - Draft minutes awaiting review, with links. Possible matches from cx-sweep are shown in their own section.
   - Unresolved projects, conflicts and stale items are listed first.
   - **Copy-command buttons** (no permissions needed):
     - beside each project, **"Approve all <CODE> (copy)"** copies `approve <CODE>, then apply`;
     - at the bottom, **"Approve all (copy)"** copies `approve all, then apply`;
     - each button shows what it covers ("n routine items covered · m need individual approval"), and is disabled when nothing is routine.
   - **The dashboard never approves anything itself.** Pasting the command into Claude Code runs cx-review under the normal bulk rule.
   - Draft rows note: "Edit in Word if needed, close it, then approve individually".
   - In-place approvals and edits are backlog item BL-01, pending an IT check.

Every row shows its source. File paths are clickable `file:///` links.

## Health criteria (used by cx-weekly-report; thresholds from `settings.json` `health`; shown in a footer legend)

- **On track:** no overdue actions older than 7 days, no open Urgent risks, next milestone date unchanged.
- **At risk:** any one of:
  - an overdue action older than 7 days;
  - an open Urgent risk;
  - the next milestone moved by 2 weeks or less.
- **Off track:** any one of:
  - the next milestone moved by more than 2 weeks;
  - two or more Urgent risks;
  - an issue blocking delivery.

These are proposed defaults. Alan has final say: cx-weekly-report proposes a health rating with reasons, and never sets health silently.

## Trends (FR-15, later)

Built from `change_log` and `updates`: actions opened versus closed per week, risk priority movement, and health history per project.

## Reports

Reports are **Word documents** drafted by skills from templates in `system/templates/`, using the docx skill. They're filed per project in `reports/` on approval. The dashboard's Project view links to them. Portfolio-level reports are backlog BL-02.

- **Weekly report** (cx-weekly-report): `projects/<CODE> <Name>/reports/YYYY-MM-DD Weekly report - <Project name>.docx`, dated by the week-ending Friday.
- **Client report** (cx-client-report): `projects/<CODE> <Name>/reports/YYYY-MM-DD Client report - <Project name>.docx`. Client-safe content only: internal health is shown, and only `client_visible` risks, issues and decisions appear.
- Export to PDF from Word when needed.


## Step 4 build notes (2026-10-05)

- **Template:** `system/templates/dashboard.html`, derived from the reference (v0.23); `build_dashboard.py` embeds the data as JSON. The page is self-contained: no network (Roboto from Google Fonts removed; system fonts until Roboto files are bundled). `dashboard.html` is git-ignored; a dated copy goes to `system/history/`.
- **Today's date** is the viewer's date, so an older build still counts "overdue" correctly. **Vault path** is worked out from where the page is opened (the live server will inject it); never stored.
- **Live status** is off until the live server exists (Step 4c), so the red alert shows and saves are held, as specified.
- **Deep links:** `dashboard.html#review`, `#work`, `#files`, `#cockpit=ABC` open on that view.
- **Project page (BL-11, 12, 13, 14, 15):** Current state first, full width, with paragraphs and images (`projects/<CODE> <Name>/images/…`, referenced as `![caption](images/x.png)` in the project's `CLAUDE.md` Current state, embedded at build, up to 2 MB each); then health and evidence, milestones (timeline plus a list with forecast, baseline when moved, and progress from linked actions), actions due; on the right "Top risks and issues" (open risks and issues by priority, with their type) and recent decisions; Draft weekly report, Draft client report and Browse files in a bar at the bottom.
- **Icons (BL-13):** neutral line icons for action, risk, issue, assumption, dependency, decision and milestone, always beside text, in Work lists, Review, Today and the project page. Dashboard only.
- **Health not set** shows as neutral "Not set"; a week with no approved update shows as a flat bar in the 8-week trend.

## Step 4b build notes (2026-10-05)

- **Vault access:** File System Access API (Chrome or Edge). "Connect vault" once; the folder is checked (it must hold `CLAUDE.md` and `system/scripts/paths.py`) and remembered in IndexedDB; "Reconnect vault" appears only if Chrome drops the permission. Safari shows "Read-only: use Chrome".
- **Writes:** see "Dashboard proposals (Step 4b)" in `dev/staging-format.md`. All writes run through one queue, so bulk approvals can't overwrite each other.
- **Panel:** an optional Milestone field on actions and RAID (that project's milestones); updates to fields other than status edit as text.
- **Help (BL-84)** is the approved first-day and how-to guides as the dashboard's Help page, with the Reference after them. One source, `system/templates/help.md`, in three parts (H1 headings **Start here**, **How do I…**, **Reference**) with a few conventions defined once in a comment at its top. `build_dashboard.help_parse()` reads it, `help_sections()` returns `{tabs: [{id, title, html}], tasks, sections, start}` for the page, and `help_part()` renders one part for a guide, so the tabs and the guides use the same functions.
  - **Start here:** the creaite icon, a hero, **The loop** (five stages in a row, the "You · Review" stage highlighted as the only gate, arrows between them, a return line, stacking under 860px), **Your first hour** (numbered steps), **Every day** (the rhythm strip), **Where things are** (views grid and keys line), **Say this** (the phrasebook table, phrases as mono chips) and **Four rules** (green cards).
  - **How do I…:** a grouped index (Every day, Meetings, Keeping it right, Reporting, Admin), then one card per task: a mono "where" line, the title, numbered steps and a dashed "Or say" row of chips. Linking to a task highlights its card (`.hp-hl`).
  - **Reference:** every section of the old single-page Help, in three groups, with a side index (a grid on narrow widths) and a search box; the vault's server command replaces `[start command]`.
  - **Addresses:** `#help-start`, `#help-howto`, `#help-howto-<task>` (the task's title in lower case with hyphens), `#help-reference` and `#help-reference-<section>`; each tab switch is a history entry, so Back and Forward work. `?` and Settings open the tab used last (kept in this browser).
  - **⌘K:** from two letters, "Help: <task>" and "Help: <section>" entries (and the three tabs); "help" and "how do I…" list the tasks first.
  - **First visit:** `help.start_seen` (false in `settings-defaults.json`, so a new or updated vault sees it once; true in this repo's own settings). While it is false the dashboard opens on Help › Start here and sets it true with a logged settings write; a link with an address is never overridden, and the write needs no prompt (live server or a connected vault).
  - **Look:** the guides' type scale and components, in Schibsted Grotesk (display), Source Sans 3 (body) and JetBrains Mono (mono), embedded from `system/templates/vendor/` (Latin WOFF2; system fonts if a file is missing). Colours are `--hp-*` tokens (`/* ===== Help tokens (BL-84) ===== */`): with no theme set (the base) the artifacts' own colours, light and dark, which creaite uses as it is; in the other themes the same roles map to the theme's accent, warn (the "you" stage), ok (the rules), surface, line and muted. The component CSS is the marked `/* ===== Help (BL-84) ===== */` section, which `dev/tools/build_guides.py` copies into the guides.
  - **The standalone guides** (`system/templates/guide-first-day.html`, `guide-how-to.html`) are generated by `dev/tools/build_guides.py` from Start here and How do I…: self-contained, light and dark, linking to each other. Run it whenever `help.md` changes; a test fails while they are out of date.
- **Removed from the reference:** the "Simulate server stopped" button; settings no longer come from browser storage.

## Step 4c build notes (2026-10-05)

- **Start:** `cd <vault> && python3 system/scripts/live_server.py`, then open `http://127.0.0.1:8765/` (port from `live_mode.port`). Leave the Terminal window open; Ctrl+C stops it.
- **Served live,** the page writes through the server (no folder access needed), and with auto-apply each save is applied at once (`tracker.py apply`) and the page reloads its data; Undo in the message undoes that apply if it's still the latest. Several quick Review decisions (bulk approve) are applied together. Activity offers Undo on the latest apply.
- **Drafts and conflicts** go through `review.py` on the server, so the draft-versus-items check, the possible-internal-content check and the clean-up all run (there is no Word label check, BL-121); its questions come back in the ask creaite panel as buttons (check again, file it anyway, update the items to match the draft, rebuild the items, keep the items as staged; keep or override a conflict).
- **Files** open in their own app (Word, Finder) through the server.
- **ask creaite** runs `claude -p … --output-format stream-json` in the vault with a strict allowlist: reading tools only (Read, Grep, Glob, skills, `tracker.py read/query`, `review.py list/pending`, Microsoft 365 search and read). Asked for a change, Claude answers with a `CONFIRM: title | what happens` line, which the panel shows as a card; only after Alan confirms is the follow-up run with the write tools (staging, `tracker.py apply/undo`, `review.py`, `minutes.py`, `docx_tools.py`, `excel_sync.py`). Sends, deletes, `git push`, `rm`, `curl`, web fetches and edits to scripts or the database are always refused. The session is resumed across messages. Since BL-141 a chat also reads the home folder and the projects root (`--add-dir`) and changes files through `filechange.py` (ordinary at once; sensitive on the one card), behind the guard hook passed with `--settings`; see "ask creaite as the administrator" below.
- **Security:** 127.0.0.1 only; a random token per run, given only to the page the server serves; Host must be 127.0.0.1 or localhost; file access through a fixed allowlist. `/health` is open (it reveals nothing) so the file version of the dashboard can find a running server and offer a link to the live one.
- **Server down:** the red alert and held saves as before; in the file version, saves use folder access and wait for "apply".

## Step 4d build notes (2026-10-05)

- **Review details (BL-25):** each proposed item shows what's needed to decide: owner, due date with "in N days" or "overdue", priority (or impact × likelihood → priority and the response for RAID), workstream and milestone; changes show each field before → after. Urgent is red; High, a missing owner or due date, and overdue are amber. The same details are in `review.py list` and the Excel Review sheet.
- **Filters (BL-19):** project and priority are multi-select menus like owners (search, Select all, Clear).
- **Edit icons (BL-21):** a pencil, bottom-right of each editable tile; the Current state tile also has a picture icon (Add image) and the Milestones tile's pencil adds a milestone.
- **ask creaite (BL-20, BL-24):** + starts a new chat; the clock lists earlier chats (kept by the live server in `system/staging/ask/<chat>.json`, never in the browser) and reopens one, resuming its Claude Code session. Suggestions appear as Alan types, from the skills' trigger phrases and the `CLAUDE.md` command table (gathered at build), plus project codes, open item IDs and review numbers; `/` lists everything. **Nothing is chosen until Alan chooses it (BL-199):** `AC.i` is -1 (no `.act`, every option `aria-selected="false"`, no `aria-activedescendant` on `#chIn`), and a hint line heads the list ("Enter sends what you typed · ↓ ↑ choose a suggestion"; with one chosen, "Enter puts this suggestion in the box · Esc closes"). Enter with none chosen closes the list and sends the typed text; ↓ chooses the first, ↑ the last, then they wrap; Enter with one chosen fills the box (never sends); Tab fills the chosen one or, with none, the first (it never sends); Shift+Tab is left alone; a click picks; Esc closes the list, not the chat. Options highlight on hover.
- **Milestones (BL-23):** click a milestone to edit its name, forecast, status or order; Mark done (today's date if it had none); Rebaseline (needs a reason, logged as the item `note`); Remove (two clicks; status Cancelled and its linked actions and RAID items unlinked in the same proposal). Two unapplied edits to the same milestone conflict by design, as for any item.
- **Current state (BL-22):** a small built-in editor (bold, italic, bulleted and numbered lists) that saves Markdown to the project's `CLAUDE.md`; rendered on the page and in the Word weekly report. Edits made in a Word draft come back as plain text with lists kept.
- **Add image (BL-12):** PNG or JPEG up to 2 MB with a caption; staged in `system/staging/uploads/`, then the proposal copies it to the project's `images/`, adds the `![caption](images/…)` line and removes the staged copy. Undo reverses all three.
- **Pickers (BL-18 A):** owner and workstream pickers list the project's own people and workstreams first (from its items and the register's stakeholders), then everyone else.
- **Additions (BL-26 to BL-30):** closing an item fills Closed as DD-MM-YYYY (BL-30); milestone pickers show names, keeping the ID as the value, with a linked Cancelled milestone marked (BL-27); a proposed action or RAID item without Raised gets the source's date, else the staging date, shown in Review and filled by `tracker.py apply` (BL-26); milestones carry % complete, set or calculated, on the timeline (a partly filled dot), the list and both Word reports (BL-29); choosing a project for an uncertain item offers "Remember … for CODE", which stages a register change through `/api/learn` and `propose.py learn`, with the guardrails (no single keywords, never `identity.own_domains`, never two project rows) (BL-28).

## Step 4e build notes (2026-10-05)

- **Current state (BL-31):** the editor spans the panel, starts 360px tall and stretches by its bottom edge; the panel is 640px wide while Current state is edited.
- **History (BL-32):** a dropdown under the clock icon, over the current chat: the 10 most recent chats (title, DD-MM-YYYY HH:MM, message count; the current one marked). The live server keeps 10 and deletes the oldest when an 11th starts.
- **Stop (BL-33):** each request carries a run ID; Send becomes Stop (square in a circle), and Esc in the input stops too. `/api/ask/stop` ends a read-only run at once; a confirmed write ends after the running step's result. If the page goes away, the server stops the run itself. The partial reply stays, with "Stopped" (or "Stopped after the current step finished"); the chat history marks it; the next question resumes the session.
- **Fixed on the way:** the live server read Claude Code's output in blocks, so steps reached the page only at the end; it now reads line by line.
- **Run report now (BL-34):** first in a report reminder's menu; it opens ask creaite and sends the reminder's command ("weekly report ABC for week ending DD-MM-YYYY", one reminder per project still owed; "client report ABC"); the reminder shows "Running…" meanwhile and stays until the report is filed. Greyed out ("Needs the live server") in the file version.
- **Allow list fixed:** ask creaite can now run `report.py` (data read-only; drafting after confirmation), `propose.py` (preview and learn checks read-only; staging after confirmation) and `sweep.py` (due and window read-only; the rest after confirmation). Before, reports, cx-edit and sweeps couldn't run from the panel.

## Step 4f build notes (2026-10-05)

- **Panel width (BL-35):** a grip on ask creaite's left edge (a separator with the name "Resize ask creaite"): drag, or ← → in 20px steps; Home or a double-click resets to 420px. Kept between 360px and 70% of the window; full width below 600px. Stored in the browser (`creaite-chat-width`), a screen preference rather than a setting.
- **Runs keep going (BL-37):** the live server owns each run (`Run` in `live_server.py`: a reader thread, kept events, the chat file updated as it goes). Pages follow a run and can leave; `/api/ask/running`, `/api/ask/attach` (replay, then live) and `/api/ask/ack` let a reloaded page pick it up, including a confirmation card left waiting. One run at a time (409 while busy). Keep-alives every 2 s notice a closed page; an unwatched run ends after `assistant.run_timeout_minutes` (30). Stopping the server ends any run; its chat shows "Interrupted" next time. This reverses BL-33's stop-on-disconnect.

## Step 4g build notes (2026-10-05)

- **History (BL-38):** `go()` pushes a history entry; the address holds the place: `#cockpit=ABC`, `#work=raid&v=open&p=ABC&from=ABC`, plus `&item=ABC-003` for an open item (opening or closing an item replaces the entry, so Back skips rows). `popstate` restores view, Work tab, view, project filter, breadcrumb origin, the open item and scroll. `#item=ID` alone opens that item in Work. A reload restores from the address. While an edit is open, Back is undone and the panel shakes with the usual message; links and the switcher do the same.
- **Links:** project page figures (Overdue actions, Urgent risks, Due Nd; since BL-185 Milestone slip goes to the Milestones card and the No due date figure is gone from the row, the card stays), card headings and "All N …" open Work filtered to the project (and set the breadcrumb Projects › ABC › RAID); the rail opens Work with its filters as they were. Project codes on Today, in Work rows, Review group headings and the panel header open the project page. A switcher (‹ select ›) and [ ] move between projects in portfolio order.

## Step 4h build notes (2026-10-05)

- **Themes (BL-39):** Settings › General › Appearance › Theme picks a named palette (`dashboard.palette`, default "creaite", with a swatch); Mode is the existing System, Light or Dark (`dashboard.theme`). The `:root` tokens are the base that every theme overrides; every theme, creaite included, sets `data-palette` on `<html>` (BL-142) and needs three token blocks (light, system-dark, forced-dark) plus an entry in `THEMES`; it may also add shape rules scoped to its `data-palette` (Brightwater and Cursor do: radius and weight). **Five themes (BL-142):** creaite (the default; was creaite teal), Brightwater, Notion, Linear and Cursor; creaite standard and Minimals were removed. Saved `creaite standard`, `Minimals` and `creaite teal` (and any name the page doesn't know) open as creaite through `THEME_OLD`/`themeOf`, and `update.py tidy` (also `merged_changes`) rewrites the saved value in `settings.json` to `creaite`, logged. **Brightwater** (BL-67, from Alan's Brightwater design system: Off-White and Charcoal grounds, Charcoal links, focus and primary buttons on light; Signal Green as the accent on dark and, on light, only as a fill under Charcoal text (active rail item, pressed chips), since it is 1.2:1 on Off-White; Violet only for marks (ask creaite's border, the Today sun); creaite standard's health colours; ABC Diatype when installed, not embedded, never Aptos; 6px cards and 4px controls; the Today view uses the brand sans, not the serif). An unknown name falls back to creaite. ⌘K lists "Theme: <name>". Dashboard only: Word reports, minutes and the Excel export keep the Brightwater template.
- **Today day view:** a warm band heads Today (soft paper tones, `--day-*` tokens with dark variants, in every theme): the date, a serif headline naming the day's shape ("Three meetings, Alan, with the late morning wide open"; worked out from the largest free gap between 08:00 and 18:00), the existing brief line, then a line (80% of the width, centred) from 08:00 to 19:00 (wider if meetings fall outside) with a bump and dot per meeting (dot size by length; past meetings faded, the current one in the sun colour), a sun at the current time, a small mark over a free evening, and faint 09:00 / 12:00 / 15:00 / 18:00 ticks. Below it, one column per meeting: time, title (with a project link when the title names a project's code or name), place and "You organise". All-day items are listed on one line. The data is `system/staging/calendar.json`, saved by cx-sweep (`sweep.py calendar`; times, titles and places only; private meetings saved as "Busy"); `sweep.sources.calendar` (Settings › Sweep › Outlook calendar) turns it off. With no calendar for today, the line is flat with a note. The brief's sections below the band are described under "Today as a brief (BL-72)".
- **Moving between days (BL-293):** the date becomes a day line (`components/today/DayTabs.jsx`, a `Tabs` block): every day in `D.days` (`build_dashboard.calendar_days`), the chosen one written out, today in the accent colour, a "Next week" divider. Click, the hover chevrons, a two-finger swipe (wheel `deltaX`, one day per gesture, momentum ignored), a touch drag, `[` `]` and `T` (Today only, never while typing or with a dialog open; never hinted on screen) move it; ← → Home End move inside it. The chosen day is in memory only (`stores/today.js`), so a reload opens on today. Only the band follows it (`dayShape(ev, first, day)`, `DayCurve`, `DayMeetings`); off today there is no sun or Now, and one italic line says the rest is as of today. Past days show each meeting's `follow` state (Minutes made, Draft in Review, Transcript ready with Process, No transcript, Not checked yet with Check); today and later one pill per meeting (`Prepare` when `plan` is set, else `Prep`), with BL-287's Open icon and "Prep page made" when `prep.page` (or `SA().prep_pages`) has one. Confidential meetings: title and times only. The band sets `overscroll-behavior-x: none`.
- **Page tone:** every view's body uses the same soft paper tone as Today (`--day-page` on `.view`, light and dark, in every theme).
- **Work search:** a **Search this list** box leads the Work toolbar on Actions, RAID and Decisions. Free text, case-insensitive; every word must appear somewhere in the item (ID, text, owner, workstream, response, latest update, source, status, project code or name). It narrows the current saved view and combines with the owner, project and priority filters; it stays when switching tabs, counts as a filter for Clear filters, and is kept in the address (`&q=`). Enter opens the first match; Esc clears.

## Review nesting (BL-41, 2026-10-06)

- **Grouping:** by proposal file: a cx-minutes proposal's items sit under its draft minutes row, in each project the meeting covers (only that project's items). Items with no project yet, and items from mail, Teams, cx-edit or the dashboard, stay at project level.
- **Parent row:** "▾ N items" collapses or expands; **Approve all from these minutes** approves the routine children (the bulk rule is unchanged: minutes, new decisions and new risks stay for one-by-one approval, and the message says how many are left); the parent's checkbox and X tick its routine children. Refresh items and Open draft stay on the parent.
- **Rejecting minutes** leaves their items pending; the message offers "Reject them too".
- **`review.py list`:** items from pending minutes follow the minutes line, indented with ↳, numbered in order. **Excel Review sheet:** a "From minutes" column, added last so Decision and Note keep their columns.

## Step 4j build notes (2026-10-06)

- **Current state in place (BL-47):** the pencil turns the card into the editor: a toolbar (bold, italic, strikethrough, sub-heading, bulleted and numbered lists with indent and outdent, quote, link, unlink, clear formatting, undo, redo), Save and Cancel; ⌘↵ saves, Esc cancels, ⌘K adds a link (an inline field, no browser dialog), ⌘⇧X strikes through, Tab nests a list item. Paste keeps only those formats. Leaving with unsaved changes (another view, Back, another project or item) shows "Discard your changes?" in the page. The editor survives a refresh of the page's data. Saving is unchanged: a proposal that rewrites the section in `CLAUDE.md`. The side panel's current-state mode and its wide panel are gone.
- **Markdown:** `### ` sub-headings, `> ` quotes, lists nested two spaces per level, `~~strike~~`, `[text](https://…)`, alongside bold, italic and lists; the same parser rules in `dashboard.html` and `docx_tools.py`. In Word: sub-headings use the minutes' topic-heading style (a `{{STYLE_TOPIC}}` sample in the weekly template, removed when filled), quotes are indented italics, nested items indent 360 twips per level, links read "text (address)".
- **BL-44:** the panel no longer drops below the project page; the wide panel that caused it is gone, and a test checks the health panel opens on the right.
- **Activity (BL-43):** each line names the item: "ORX-A-002 · Send revised SOW — created (owner Sara, due 14-10-2026)", "ORX-A-002 · Send revised SOW — status: Open → Done". New items show the text as created (actions with owner and due date, risks and issues with type and priority); changes show the item's current text. The ID opens the item (milestones and projects too); long text is cut at about 80 characters, full on hover. Lines added by the dashboard before a reload use the same form. Dates show DD-MM-YYYY.
- **Steering one-pager (BL-92, sprint 5):** a meeting whose title contains "steer" and that Prep matches to a project (not the portfolio) gets, at the top of its Prep brief, **Draft the one-pager** and **Draft the client-safe one**: buttons that ask creaite (`data-ask="steerco one-pager ABC [client-safe]"`; the cx-steerco skill; confirmed in the chat panel) and close the brief. Code is in `/* ===== Steering one-pager (BL-92, sprint 5) ===== */`, wrapping `drawPrep`. The Word page is `report.py steerco-data` / `steerco-stage` (section "Steering one-pager" in `report.py`) filling `steerco-template.docx` (built by `dev/tools/build_templates.py steerco`): Overall Status (trend, evidence and early warnings, internal only), What Changed since the last one-pager in the Steerco folder (else 14 days), Milestones, Top Risks and Issues, Decisions Needed (for the committee, for Alan), Blockers (dependencies attached to a milestone, and the `links` table), Flow (internal only) and Next Period (actions overdue or due in 14 days). A section with nothing to say is removed from the template before filling. Caps keep it to a page: 8 changes, 7 milestones, 5 risks, 6 decisions, 6 blockers, 8 actions (the rest as "+n more in the tracker"). **Client-safe:** only client-visible items with complete messaging, in their client wording, no IDs, owners or priorities; an item whose label excludes it (`behaviour` or `default_choice` exclude: Sensitive) is left out and reported unless `include_labelled` names it, which is logged on the report block. **Internal:** items with a label are marked `[Sensitive]` and Overall Status warns. Filing: `copy` into the Steerco folder as `YYYY-MM-DD <Project name> Steerco one-pager[ (client)].docx` (the project's name, never its code, BL-101; ` 2`, ` 3` for a second the same day; a file already there is never replaced), a `mkdir` first when the folder is missing. Approval: the client-safe version gets the client-report checks (`review.client_findings`) and BL-94's label check (an excluded label's item by ID, its wording or its client wording); `--as-staged` files it anyway.
- **Decisions needed (BL-53, sprint 5):** a decision has `status` (Pending, Made, Withdrawn), `made_by` (who it is asked of while pending), `needed_by` (the dashboard's `due` for a decision), `date` (when made; empty while pending), `reasoning` (`why`), `options` (`opts`), `revisit` and `replaces` (`rby` is the decision that replaces it). The data carries them as `st`, `by`, `due`, `date`, `why`, `opts`, `revisit`, `replaces`, `rby`. Code is in the page's own section `/* ===== Decisions and schema (BL-53, sprint 5) ===== */`, which extends the views above by wrapping their functions, with a few edits to the existing lines (the panel's fields and validation, Work's Decisions filters, views and columns, the Draft email builder).
  - **Panel:** Status, Decision maker, Needed by, Date made, Source (new), Reasoning, Options considered, Revisit (a date or words), Replaces (this project's other decisions) and, read-only, Replaced by. A pending decision's panel has **Record the decision** (opens the form filled in: Made, today, the same record) and **Withdraw** (second press confirms). A Made decision needs its date, a pending one has none (the form clears it), and a made one can't go back to Pending.
  - **Work › Decisions:** columns Status and Needed by (late in red with the days), filters Status and Needed by (Overdue, soon, no date, later), views Pending (soonest needed first) and Overdue, and, in the New tile's menu on Decisions, **Ask for a decision** (opens the empty peek as Pending, BL-179; was the Decision needed button, BL-159).
  - **Today › Needs attention:** one line after the proposals, "2 decisions waiting on others, 1 for you, 1 late", the first three linked. **Project page:** a Decisions needed card (and made decisions whose revisit date has come). **Person page:** "Decisions we're waiting on from <first name>". Recent decisions leave pending ones out.
  - **Draft email:** the person's pending decisions are offered as "Decision needed: …" (unticked and marked internal for someone outside the own domains, like any item that isn't client-visible), with one extra sentence asking what they have decided. The builder is unchanged when no decision is picked.
  - **Early warning** `decision_overdue` (`warnings.decision_overdue`: `on`, `days` late, default 1): a pending decision past `needed_by`, shown on the decision, the project page and Settings › Early warnings. **Reminder** `revisit:<ID>:<date>` (Today and the session start): a made decision whose revisit date (YYYY-MM-DD) has come; a revisit in words is shown on the decision only. **Reports:** the weekly report's Decisions lists pending ones as "Needed: …" and its data has `decisions_needed` and `decisions_to_revisit`; a client report shows a pending decision only if it is client-visible; one-off documents list decisions with Status and Needed by (`status` filter pending, made, withdrawn).
- **Work filters (BL-45):** one filter model for Actions, RAID and Decisions (`FDEFS`): Status, Due (overdue, due soon, no date, later), Owner (Me first), Project, Priority on the toolbar; "+ Filter" for workstream, RAID type, response, source (minutes, mail, Teams, dashboard, Excel, cx-edit), client-visible and a due-date range; Decisions have Made by, Project, Date and Client-visible. Every option shows its count given the other filters (dimmed at zero). Views are presets of these filters (`PRESETS`); the current filters are matched back to a view, else "Custom", which can be saved (`settings.json` `work.saved_views`, offered in Settings' "Actions open with"). An applied-filter row shows a chip per filter, Clear all and "Showing N of M". Headers sort (asc, desc, default). `/` focuses search and F opens the filters in Work. The address carries the view, or the filters, plus sort and search.
- **Project page lists (BL-48):** Actions due or overdue shows at most `dashboard.project_list_cap` (10; Settings › Work and views › Project page), overdue first, oldest first; the heading shows the total and "+N more — see all ›" opens Work filtered to that project, open, overdue and due soon. Top risks (5) and recent decisions (3) were already capped, each with its "All N …" link.
- **Generate current state (BL-46):** a Generate button on the card opens a dialog: period (7, 14, 30 days or dates; default `dashboard.generate_days`), sources (tracker, minutes and transcripts, reports, updates, items already swept; documents off by default), an optional steer, and when mail was last swept. `/api/current/generate` gathers the material with `report.py current-context` (vault only), fills `system/templates/current-state-prompt.md` and runs Claude through the run engine with read-only tools (`kind: generate`, one run at a time, Stop; BL-326: kept in the history only as its plain ask and closure, in the day's "Runs creaite started" chat). The reply's draft opens in the in-place editor, marked unsaved, with "Drafted from" listing its sources; saving is the usual proposal.

## Step 4k build notes (2026-10-06)

- **Workstreams per project (BL-57):** Settings › People and projects › Workstreams lists workstreams as "CODE · name" with that project's counts; rename, merge and retire act on one project (stored as `CODE|name` keys in `settings.json` `workstreams`; a plain name from before still counts everywhere). After a rename, other projects using the name are offered "Also rename in …". The picker offers only the item's project's workstreams, and a new name needs a second Save. cx-minutes and cx-sweep reuse only the matched project's workstreams.
- **Health against the evidence (BL-52):** wherever health is set (the dashboard's health editor, cx-edit, the weekly report), it's compared with the health criteria from Settings (overdue actions, Urgent risks and issues, milestone slip; `report.criteria`). The editor shows what the evidence suggests as you choose; a health better than the evidence won't save until the reason is a new one. cx-edit's preview shows the evidence and refuses to stage without a reason; `weekly-stage` refuses without one. creaite never changes health itself.
- **Intake (BL-42 phase 1; called Inbox until BL-70):** a rail view with a count of new files. Drag and drop or choose files (`/api/inbox/upload`: .vtt, .docx, .txt, .md, .pdf; 25 MB each; names cleaned; never overwriting; requests over 40 MB refused). Each file shows type, size, date, likely project (a code in the name, else the register's name, aliases and keywords) and state (new; processing; draft ready when a pending minutes proposal uses it; already processed when an identical transcript is filed). "Process selected" sends cx-minutes through the run engine as a confirmed request; progress shows in ask creaite, drafts land in Review. Phases 2 (calendar meetings) and 3 (fetching transcripts) need the live vault.
- **Generate as an icon (BL-61):** a sparkle in the Current state card's corner group (Generate, Add image, Edit), dimmed with "Generate needs the live server" or "ask creaite is busy" when it can't run; the heading stands alone.
- **Work filter fixes (BL-62):** a menu keeps its search text and focus after a tick; Clear all returns to the tab's first view, All open on Actions and RAID (no search, no project; Alan, 11-10-2026: before, the tab's opening view); each tab keeps its own filters, search and view, and nothing carries between tabs; closed items are never "Overdue", "Due soon" or "Later"; Workstream has "No workstream"; search matches dates as shown (DD-MM-YYYY); Decisions' "Last N days" excludes future dates.
- **Intake preview and remove:** each row has Preview (`/api/inbox/preview`: text files as they are, Word text extracted, up to 200,000 characters, shown in a dialog with "Open in its app"; a PDF opens in its app) and Remove (`/api/inbox/remove` moves the file to `system/backups/inbox-removed/YYYY-MM-DD/`, never deleting it; Undo calls `/api/inbox/restore`). Only files directly in `inbox/` are reachable.

## Insight and capture (BL-51, BL-49, BL-54; sprint-insight, 2026-10-06)

- **Early warnings (BL-51):** `build_dashboard.early_warnings` works out read-only signals at build time, never stored as tracker rows: an open action whose due date was changed `warnings.redated.times` (3) or more times (`change_log`, undone applies and first dates not counted); an open risk with no response raised in `warnings.repeat_risk.mentions` (3) or more meetings or sweeps (the first plus repeats in `system/staging/mentions.jsonl`, which `minutes.py stage` and `sweep.py stage` append to when they skip a duplicate of an open item); a planned milestone whose forecast moved later in `warnings.milestone_slip.weeks` (2) or more consecutive weeks, the latest this week or last; an open action older than `warnings.long_open.factor` (2) times the median days to close of the project's closed actions (the portfolio's if under 5) and at least `min_days` (14). Each has `on`. The key-person signal waits for the people register (BL-55). Shown as a lightning flag with the reason on hover (Work, Today, project page lists, milestones), in the detail panel, a project-page card and an evidence figure, the portfolio evidence and the health editor's evidence; `report.py` adds them to `weekly-data` (`early_warnings`) and `health_evidence` (`warnings`). Settings › Health and warnings › Early warnings edits `settings.json` `warnings`; the live dashboard reloads its data at once.
- **Meeting prep (BL-49):** `build_dashboard.prep` gives each of today's non-private meetings (`calendar.json`) a series (`series_of`: the title in lower case without `[CODE]` tags, numbers or punctuation), a project and how it was found (`prep_match`: remembered for the series in `settings.json` `prep.series`; else a code, name, alias or keyword in the title; "portfolio" or several projects → `_portfolio`; else unsure), and `since`: the date of the series' last processed meeting (filed minutes and transcripts, `processed.txt` keys), else 7 days ago. Today's day view adds a Prep link to each; the brief (a dialog) lists open actions (overdue first), risks and issues with no response, decisions since `since`, and early warnings, eight per section with a link to Work; a portfolio brief covers every project (actions overdue or due soon only). Picking a project for an unsure meeting saves it to `prep.series`. cx-minutes treating the brief's items as expected after the meeting is not part of this sprint.
- **Quick capture (BL-54) is replaced by the capture drawer (BL-301 C, v2.0.0, below):** the card, `/api/capture` and its chips are gone; quick notes saved before stay in `minutes/` and cx-minutes still processes them. What follows is the old design, kept for reference.
- **Quick capture (BL-54):** a Quick capture card on the Intake page (below the file table, BL-66; ⌘K "Quick capture"; "note: …" in ask creaite): notes, project (or "Match from the note"), date (DD-MM-YYYY, today by default), who was there, title (else the note's first eight words). `/api/capture` validates them (notes up to 20,000 characters, an active project with a folder), matches the project with `build_dashboard.project_hits` when none is chosen (409 with the candidates when there are none or several) and writes `projects/<CODE> <Name>/minutes/YYYY-MM-DD Quick note - <title>.md` (a header with date, project, who was there and when it was captured, then the notes), never overwriting. The page then sends `process <note>` through the run engine as a confirmed request, as the Inbox does. cx-minutes reads the note directly, drafts no Word minutes unless asked, and stages it with `minutes.py stage` `"note"`: no file operations, each item's source is the note plus its section, and the meeting block (`kind: "quick note"`, nothing to approve) records the note in `processed.txt` on apply.

## Images in the Current state editor (BL-63, 2026-10-07)

- **In place:** an image is a block of its own in Current state's Markdown, `![caption](images/x.png)` on its own line, wherever Alan puts it (`mdBlocks`/`htmlToMd` in the page, `docx_tools.md_blocks` and `md_normalise`). The project page and the editor show it inline (`build_dashboard.memory` keeps the line and embeds the file as `{alt, ref, src}`); a missing file shows "Image not found".
- **Editor:** an Image button in the toolbar, or paste or drop a PNG or JPEG (2 MB each), inserts it after the block holding the caret (or the drop point), never inside a line; each image has a caption under it (one line) and × to remove it. Saving makes one proposal: each new image staged in `system/staging/uploads/`, copied to the project's `images/` (`YYYY-MM-DD-<caption>.png`), the text written with the image lines in place, the staged copy removed; undo reverses all of it. A removed image's file stays in `images/`.
- **Elsewhere:** `report.replace_current` (and the page's `replaceCurrent`) keeps images where the new text puts them and keeps any it leaves out at the end, so none is lost; the weekly report fills Current state's text and images in order from the saved text (`report.current_entries`; `{{IMAGES}}` stays empty); Generate and cx-weekly-report are told to keep image lines where they are. The approval check leaves captions out of the comparison, as before.
- **Gone:** the Add image panel and its pencil; ⌘K "Add image: CODE" opens the editor and the file picker. Existing images (at the end of Current state) need no migration.

## People (BL-55, BL-56)

- **The register (BL-55):** `projects/_portfolio/people-register.md` (path in `paths.PEOPLE_REGISTER`), beside `project-register.md`; data, not a setting. One table under `## People`: Full name, Email, Aliases (comma-separated), Organisation, Role, Hidden (`yes` keeps a person out of the owner picker). Everything but the full name is optional; Settings marks incomplete entries ("no email"). The template is `system/templates/people-register-template.md`. The old `settings.json` `people.added` / `people.hidden` move into it once (`propose.py people migrate`, offered by cx-setup); until then the dashboard still lists them and Settings says so.
- **Matching (the same rules in `propose.py` and the page; `dev/tests/test_people_dashboard.py` checks they agree):** the text is compared loosely (case, dots and commas ignored, a bracketed company or role dropped). An exact match of the full name, an alias or an email address is *the person* (status known, alias, email) and is mapped to the full name. Otherwise a *likely* match is offered, never used: every word of the text is a word of a name or alias ("Sara", "Khan"), a first-name prefix with a last-name prefix ("S Khan", "Sara K"), initials ("SK"), an email's local part read as a name, or a spelling within one letter (two for names of ten letters or more). Alan's own name and short forms (`identity`) count as a person. Names on items that aren't registered count as known, so nothing existing is ever prompted about.
- **The new-person prompt:** wherever an owner (or a decision's "Made by") is typed or saved (owner picker, the detail panel, a proposal edited in Review): a likely match shows first ("Is “Sara” Sara Khan?", with a tick to remember the alias); otherwise a dialog asks for the full name (required), email, aliases, organisation and role. In the picker the same two choices are rows in the list ("Is “SK” Sara Khan? — Yes, and remember it", "＋ New person “SK”…"). Cancelling saves nothing. A name that differs from what was typed keeps the typed text as an alias.
- **Settings › People and projects › People:** the register as a table (name, email, organisation · role, aliases, counts of actions and RAID), with Add a person, Edit (or Add details for a name that is only on items), Rename, Merge into…, Remove from list and Restore. Rename and merge change every action, RAID item and decision in one apply; a merge also adds the old name (and its aliases) to the person who stays. **Filter (BL-264):** a search box (`#pmQ`: name, alias, email or role, matches marked; Esc clears; `/` focuses it on this page only) and one funnel (`#pmFBtn`, Work's `.ib.bd` with the `.dot` count; `F` opens it) with Work's field picker (`.fpmenu`/`.fpick`/`.fpf`, `isNotSeg`): Who (Project, from items and `D.stakeholders`; How they're involved, following the chosen projects; Organisation, with "No organisation"; People I follow, the register's VIP column, never labelled "VIP", BL-288) and Tidy up (In the register; Needs attention: No email, No organisation or role; Removed people: Hidden (default), Shown with everyone, Removed only). Any of within a field, and across fields; Project and Organisation have is not. Only fields and choices in the data are offered, each with its live count given the other filters (a choice that leaves nobody is greyed). Chips (`fchipHtml`, shared with Work) open the picker at their field; "Removed: hidden" is a quiet outlined chip from the start. The count line: "Showing n of N people · n removed hidden Show · n need attention Show". Nothing matching shows Work's empty state (`relaxHtml`). With projects chosen (not is not), the Actions and RAID headings say "· this project" or "· n projects", the counts and their links cover those projects (several: `extra.p` in `openWork`), and a person who is only a stakeholder there says so. The filters (not the search) are kept until cleared in `localStorage` `creaite-people-filters` (via `store`, as Work's remembered display choices). Tests: `dev/tests/test_people_filters.py`.
- **Proposals:** every register change is a `file_changes` write of the register file (`source_run: dashboard`, already approved), with the items in the same proposal for a rename or merge, so it's logged, applied with the other proposals and undone with Undo.
- **Extraction:** cx-minutes and cx-sweep run `propose.py people map <draft>` before staging; it maps exact matches to full names and flags the others `unknown-owner`. Review shows an owner the register doesn't know as "(not in the people register)". cx-edit (`propose.py preview` and `stage`) maps and notes the same way.
- **People view and person pages (BL-56):** a **People** rail button (after Work; hash `#people`) lists everyone who owns an item or made a decision, plus registered people who aren't hidden: name (Alan first, as "You (Alan Carter)"), organisation, role, projects, open actions, open RAID items and overdue, sortable by clicking a heading (default overdue, most first). A name not in the register is tagged "not in register". Counts only: no scores, ratings or on-time percentages (a test checks the page has none).
- **A person's page** (`#person=Full%20Name`, breadcrumb People › Name; rail keeps People current): name, organisation · role · email, aliases, **Edit details** (or **Add details**); counts (open actions, open risks and issues, overdue, projects); **Open work** grouped by project (BL-68): a heading per project (code and name), and under it an **Actions** table and a **Risks and issues** table drawn by Work's own row code (`rowHead()`, `rowHtml()`, `cell(k,x,t)`, `orderedCols(t)`, `rows(t,list,sort,default)`), without the Owner column. The columns, their order and widths are Work's for that type (shared, remembered). Headings sort (`PRS`, the page's own sort per type; default overdue first, then by due date). A row opens the detail panel and takes the selection highlight; double-click a cell edits it in the panel (a proposal, as in Work); J and K step through the rows; early-warning flags show on the title. **See all actions in Work** and **See all risks and issues in Work** open Work filtered to the person (Owner filter, every spelling of their name, open items; BL-45). **Hand over…** shows a tick column in each table (`th.pickcol`, `input.handbox`); clicking a row toggles it, and sorting keeps the selection. My work and a project page's cards stay short; **Decisions made** in the last 90 days, as one Decisions table (Work's Decisions columns: decision, status, made by, needed by, date, source); **Workstreams led** when `D.leads` has any (`build_dashboard.workstream_leads` reads a `workstreams` table with a `lead` column once BL-58 builds one, else `{}`; lead names map to people through aliases). Items are grouped under the person by exact match of the full name, an alias or an email, so "Sara" and "Sara Khan" are one person; a name that matches nobody is its own entry.
- **Links both ways:** an owner or "Made by" name is a link (`data-person`) in Work's owner and Made by columns, the detail panel and Review's detail line; each opens that person's page. Your own name opens My work. The command palette lists people under "People" and "Go to People". Back and Forward work as for other views. Not built here: Prep 1:1 and the handover (they wait for meeting prep, BL-49), "You owe them" and "Waiting on" (BL-50), early-warning flags (BL-51) and closed items in the last 14 days.

## Workstreams (BL-58, sprint-workstreams, 2026-10-07)

- **Storage:** a `workstreams` table (`id` `CODE-W-NNN`, project, name unique per project in any case, lead, keywords, aliases, channel, status Active or Closed, created, source, updated_at). Changed only through proposals (`table: "workstreams"`: new, update, cancel = close). `tracker.py` refuses an action or RAID item whose workstream isn't an Active workstream of its project (blank is allowed) and stores the workstream's own spelling; a rename carries the project's items with it in the same apply (logged, one undo); a project can't change. `tracker.py migrate` seeds each project's workstreams from the items and the old `settings.json` `workstreams` lists (`CODE|name`; hidden ones Closed), once; `propose.py workstreams retire-settings` clears those lists.
- **Matching:** cx-minutes and cx-sweep match the project first, then its own workstreams by name, keyword, alias, lead or channel; staging (`tracker.flag_workstream`) keeps a match in its spelling and blanks an unknown name, flagging it `new-workstream` with `ws_suggest`. A workstream name in more than one project is never project evidence; one unique to a project is weak.
- **Dashboard:** `collect()` adds `ws_table` (each workstream with open action and RAID counts). The picker (`wsFor`) offers the project's Active ones; a new name shows "Add “X” to ORX's workstreams?" with suggested lead, keywords, aliases and channel (`wsSuggest`, from the items and sources that mention it), and the second Save puts the new workstream in the same proposal, ahead of the item. Settings › People and projects › Workstreams lists the table by project (filter, show closed) with Add…, Edit (inline; Enter saves, Esc cancels), Close and Reopen, each a proposal. The project page does not show workstreams (BL-69 removed the card BL-58 added). Review rows flagged `new-workstream` offer Add workstream (into the same proposal, ahead of the item, with the suggested details) or Leave blank (`review.py workstream N add|skip` in Claude Code). Before migration (an empty table while items have workstreams) Settings shows BL-57's lists.
- **Excel:** a Workstreams sheet (ID, created, source and updated locked), imported like the others.

## Person pages, phase 2 (BL-56, sprint-workstreams, 2026-10-07)

- **Draft email (BL-83, replaces Prep 1:1):** a button beside Hand over on the person's page (disabled, with "Nothing open for <name>, so no email is needed.", when nothing is open). See "Draft email (BL-83)" at the end of this file.
- **Handover:** "Hand over…" turns the Open work list into a selection (tick items or All) with a "hand over to" field; the name goes through `ownerFinal` (the register, or the new-person prompt), and every picked item is an owner update in one proposal, so one undo reverses it.
- **On the page:** early-warning flags on their items (`decorateWarnings`), the key-person note, and "Closed in the last 14 days".
- **Key-person signal:** `early_warnings` (`warnings.key_person`: `share` 50, `min_items` 6, `on`): a project with at least `min_items` open actions and RAID items where one owner (not TBC) holds more than `share`% raises `{k: "projects", sig: "key_person", who}`, shown on the project page (linking to the person), the person's page, the portfolio and health evidence and the weekly data. Counts only; never in client reports. Settings › Early warnings shows each signal's second threshold under it.
- **Promises (BL-125 A):** a **Promises** card (`#ppPromises`, `personPromises` in `today.js`, added by `renderPerson`) right after Open work, when there is any open promise between the person and Alan: "<First name> promised you" (overdue first, each with how late and "quiet for n days") and "You promised <First name>" (soonest due first). Names are matched through the people register's aliases (`resolver`). It shows even for someone who owns no items. Promises between two other people are not listed.

## Search (BL-07)

- **Where:** a search box at the top of Files (`#vsInput`; while it has text the results replace the file list, and Esc clears it), and a "Search the vault for “…”" row in ⌘K from three characters, with the top five results as a "Search results" group beneath the jump list. Optional project-scoped box on the project page: not built (a project code in the text does the same).
- **Route:** `GET /api/search?q=&project=&person=&kind=&from=&to=&skip=&parse=` (live server, token, read-only). Returns `{chips, terms, groups: {items, meetings, reports, documents}, totals, rebuilt}`; each group is capped at 20 (`totals` has the real count, shown as "20 of 57"). A result has `kind`, `title`, `snippet` (HTML-escaped, matches in `<mark>`), `project`, `date`, `date_shown` (DD-MM-YYYY), `meta`, and `opens`: `{type: "item", table, id}` (opens in its panel; a milestone on its project page), `{type: "preview", path, q}` (meetings: `POST /api/search/preview` returns the text with the words marked, scrolled to the first) or `{type: "file", path}` (reports and documents: the system viewer, as Files does).
- **Query understanding** (`system/scripts/search.py`, plain code): project codes (the tracker's, any case), people (names, aliases and emails from the people register, and Alan's own name and short forms; a two-letter alias only in capitals), types (risk, issue, assumption, dependency, action, decision, milestone, minutes, transcript, report, document), dates (today, yesterday, this or last week (Monday to Sunday) or month, `since DD-MM-YYYY`, `in September [2026]` (the latest one that has passed when no year is given), or a date). Question words and other stop words are dropped unless nothing else is left. Each recognised phrase is a chip `{kind, value, label, words}`; removing one sends `skip=kind:value`, which leaves the words out entirely. Explicit `project`, `person`, `kind`, `from` and `to` are added as chips too.
- **What is searched:** `vault_search` (FTS5, stemmed, last word as prefix; ranked) for meetings, reports and documents, and plain SQL `LIKE` on the text, owner, workstream and ID fields of actions, RAID items, decisions and milestones. A person filter matches an item's owner (or "made by") by name or alias and a file by the person's name or alias in its text. `search.include_documents` (default true) leaves documents out when false.
- **Settings:** `search.debounce_ms` (150), `search.ask_budget_chars` (40000), `search.include_documents` (true).
- **Without the server:** a plain "Live server needed" note and the start command.
- **Ask (part 2):** the search answer is called **ask creaite** (BL-88; other "Claude" labels are unchanged). The ask creaite button beside the bar asks (the old result row `data-vsask` is gone); Enter on question-like text (ends in "?", or starts with what, why, how, when, who, did, has or is) runs it, Enter on other text searches at once, and ⌘K shows an "ask creaite: “…”" row for a question. `POST /api/search/ask` gathers the context (`search.py context`: the best passages with their surrounding paragraph and file › section, and the matching tracker rows, within `search.ask_budget_chars`, documents labelled "Document (internal)"), fills `system/templates/search-prompt.md`, and runs Claude through the run engine with kind `search` (read-only tools, one run at a time, Stop with `/api/ask/stop`, reattach with `/api/ask/attach`; not saved as an ask creaite chat). The stream starts with a `sources` event (`{question, sources: [{name, stem, path, kind}…]}`), then `text` events, then `done {session}`. The answer card shows the text with `[file name › section]` and `[ABC-A-003]` citations as links (a part that isn't a known source stays plain text); a file opens in the preview (meetings) or the system viewer, an item in its panel. **Continue in ask creaite** (`POST /api/search/continue`) keeps the exchange as a chat under the run's Claude session ID, so the assistant resumes that session. A page reload mid-answer reattaches and replays it.
- **Enrichment:** `search.py enrich set --path … --summary "<two lines>" --tags "<3 to 8>"` (written by cx-minutes at staging for each minutes and transcript copy, and by cx-weekly-report and cx-client-report for the report they stage) writes `system/search/<sha256 of the vault path>.json` = `{path, summary, tags}`. `tracker.py index` folds a file's summary and tags into its indexed content (a meeting filed in several projects takes any copy's), so keyword search matches them with no schema change; a search with no words (filters only) shows the summary as the snippet. `search.py enrich --missing [--json]` lists indexed meetings and reports without one, newest first, for cx-setup's one-off backfill (it asks first, works in batches, writes only `system/search/`). Never run per search; the live server never enriches.

## Decisions in meeting prep (BL-113, sprint-debug-log-2b)

- **The brief:** a marked wrapper around `drawPrep` (`/* ===== Decisions in meeting prep (BL-113) ===== */`) adds two sections after "Decisions since then": **Decisions needed** (`decPending()`, late first as everywhere: `decRank`; who is asked, `decAsked`, and the needed-by date in words with `decLate`) and **Decisions to revisit** (`decRevisit()`: made, with a YYYY-MM-DD revisit date that has come; a revisit written in words has no date and is not listed). The invite's ranking applies (`m.rank.decisions`), with its reason in `.prepwhy`. A portfolio meeting lists every project's, with the project link. Each line opens the decision's panel; past 8, "+n more — see all" opens Work › Decisions (`decisions:pending:CODE`, `decisions:all:CODE`). It draws once (`#prepDecs`).
