- **The data:** `build_dashboard.decision_for_prep(d, since, today)` (made since the last meeting, or Pending, or Made with a revisit date reached) feeds `prep_rank`, so the invite can rank a pending decision, and `prep_page.context`, which labels items `Decision needed`, `Decision to revisit` or `Decision` and gives each the date that matters (needed by, revisit, made); the Prep page's prompt says what the two types mean, and the built page lists them in its Work items table.

## Prep updates (BL-65)

- **What Review shows:** an update proposed from a meeting (`op: update`, with `why`) is a `kind: "update"` row nested under its minutes in each project the meeting covers, exactly like its new items (BL-41). Its title is the item's ID and text; the detail line lists each changed field as `field: before → after` (dates DD-MM-YYYY, `—` when empty) and, in quotes, what was said (`rec.why`); its source is the transcript copy and section. `review.py list` prints the same as `Update · ABC-A-003 <text>: status Open → Closed` with the why after it.
- **Never routine:** `review.individual_only` returns "closing update" for an action or RAID update whose status becomes Closed or Cancelled (and for a promise marked Done or Dropped only when it is a BL-218 probably-done guess: otherwise routine, BL-125 A decision 6), so `Approve routine`, the minutes' "Approve all from these minutes", `review.py bulk` and the Excel review treat it as one-by-one; the other updates (a new due date, a response, a latest update) are routine.
- **Code:** `review.update_parts` / `update_text` (the before values are read from the tracker each time), `build_dashboard.review_queue` (the title, `why` and `src` of an update), and the "Prep updates (BL-65)" section of `dashboard.html` (adds the why to `revDetail`). A cx-edit or sweep change keeps its "Change" label.

## Meetings in Intake (BL-42 phase 2, sprint-meetings, 2026-10-08)

- **The saved calendar:** `sweep.py calendar` keeps days from the window start (`sweep.py calendar-window`: settings.json `inbox.meetings_from`, "monday" for Monday of this week or a number of days back, to 7 days ahead) and, per meeting, the event `id`, times, title, place, `teams` (the online-meeting flag) and `organiser` (true when Alan, else the organiser's name); a private meeting is "Busy" with its ID and Teams flag. Never attendees, bodies, join links or message text. An older `calendar.json` without IDs still loads.
- **The data:** `collect()` adds `meetings` (`inbox_meetings`): `{fetched, from, rows}`, each row a timed meeting from the window start to today (newest day first, each day in time order; all-day entries left out) with day, start, end, title, `teams`, organiser, `private` (also a "Busy" row from an older calendar), `id`, the likely project and how it was found (`prep_match` with `prep.series`; private meetings aren't matched), its `series` and `processed` (the date from `processed.txt` for `calendar:<id>`, via `minutes.meeting_key` and `processed_on`). It reads `calendar.json` and `processed.txt` only.
- **The page:** a Meetings card at the top of Intake, above the drop zone and file list: "Calendar read …", Refresh, Select new and Process selected; rows grouped by day (weekday DD-MM-YYYY, newest first) with a tick box (disabled without an ID, once processed, with a draft waiting or while processing), time, title (private rows marked), Teams icon or "In person", organiser ("You" or a name), the project menu (Not sure, the projects, Portfolio; a change is remembered for the series in `prep.series`, as meeting prep does; private rows only for the row), state (Not processed, Processing…, Draft ready in Review, Couldn't with the reason from ask creaite's last reply, Already processed with its date) and Transcript ("Checked in the live vault", a placeholder for phase 3). Select new ticks unprocessed, non-private meetings. Process selected (BL-119, `processMeetings`) takes each ticked meeting in turn: one already loaded (`m.loaded`) goes straight to cx-minutes; any other is loaded first (`jobLive` on `/api/transcripts/load`, state "Loading the transcript…"), the data is reloaded, and the saved file is sent through the run engine (`askLive`, confirmed) as `process inbox/<file>`; then the data is reloaded again. A row with no transcript to load (`mtgSkip`: Not transcribed, No transcript, No access, not a Teams meeting) or one the load found nothing for is set to `skipped:<reason>`, shown as a **Skipped** tag with the reason, and the loop carries on; Claude never searches Teams for it, and the chat opens only when something runs; held without the live server or while ask creaite is busy. Refresh sends "refresh calendar" (cx-sweep) and reloads. Empty states: "No calendar yet: it comes in with the sweep" and "No meetings since …". ⌘K lists "Inbox: process meeting <title>" for each meeting that can be processed. `meetings` rows also carry `draft` (a pending cx-minutes proposal for `calendar:<id>`).

## ask creaite: permission requests (BL-75; changed by BL-140 and BL-141, sprint-admin)

- **Protocol:** the panel's system prompt tells Claude, when one rule would let a request through, to end with `PERMISSION: <rule> | <why>`, a script rule written `Bash(python3 system/scripts/<name>.py <subcommand> *)` (never `:*`), and to read `.claude/settings.json` first: if the script is already allowed, the refusal was about the command's shape, so it says what was refused. It is told to run each script on its own (no `cd … &&`, pipe, redirect, heredoc or `$(…)`).
- **What the server does with it (BL-140):** `permissions.check_full` reads the old `…:*` form as `… *` and gives a state: `allowed` (already allowed by `covered`), `out_of_bounds` (it would run anything, or is `permissions.py`, or reaches the database, the engine or the permission files), `refused` (not something creaite adds, or of no use to the panel: an `Edit`/`Write` rule, or a `Bash` rule that isn't a creaite script, since the guard allows neither), or addable. An addable rule becomes an item on the one approval card (below); the others are a `permission` event with `rule` (normalised), `asked` (as written), `reason`, `covered`, `state` and `refused` (what was refused, by its shape, from `refused_shape`).
- **Information, not a card (`permissionInfo`, `permissionInfoText`):** `allowed` reads "Nothing to add: <rule> (asked as <form>) is already allowed by <rule>. The request was refused for another reason: <shape>. ask creaite can run the script on its own instead." with a **Try again** button that sends "<rule> is already allowed, so no rule is needed. Run the script on its own … and carry on with my last request."; `refused` gives the reason only; only `out_of_bounds` adds "If you do want it, edit .claude/settings.json yourself in Terminal."
- **Refused step (BL-201):** a `denied` event carries `plain`, one `{line, raw, recovered}` per refusal from `refusal_view` in `live_server.py`. The page (`refusedSteps`) shows each as a `.step.refused`: the `line` (one sentence: what was tried, in the project's terms, why not, and what ask creaite does instead; never the command), and a closed `<details>` **Show the command** holding `raw` (the tool and its command or path, up to 600 characters). ⚠ (`.rfwarn`, class `stop`) only when the run didn't recover: no tool call after the refused one succeeded. Identical refusals show once. An event without `plain` (an older server) still shows "⚠ Refused: <tool and input> (<shape>)". The debug log keeps the shape (`refused_shape`, e.g. `propose.py with a pipe`), never the text. Display only: the guard is unchanged.
- **Claims checked (BL-314, v1.9.1):** before a chat's text is shown, `Run._checked` passes each text block through `claims.check` (`system/scripts/claims.py`, plain code, no model call). Claude's stream-json (no partial messages) gives each text block whole, so the block is the safe boundary: it is checked whole, then emitted. Every work-item ID (`ABC-A-001`, RAID `ABC-001`, `ABC-D-001`, milestones, promises; not inside a ``` fence) is looked up through tracker.py's read functions (`connect_or_raise`, `item_row`, `fetch`; read only, one connection per block). A title given beside the ID (after `·`, `—`, `–`, `:`, ` - `, in brackets, or in a table's title column) that doesn't match (lenient: a shortening or a close paraphrase passes) gets "(⚠ doesn't match the tracker: <real title>)"; an owner given beside it (`owner X`, `owned by X`, a register name or alias in a separated piece or brackets, or a table's Owner column) that isn't the item's owner (full name, first name or alias) is replaced by the tracker's owner with "(corrected from the tracker: the reply said X)"; an ID of a registered project that isn't in the tracker gets "(⚠ not in the tracker)". The `text` event then carries `checked` (`[{id, kind: title|owner|missing, said, real}]`, only when something changed) and the page adds a quiet `.step.checked` line, "Checked against the tracker: 1 title didn't match, 1 owner corrected." (`claimsNote`). The chat history keeps the checked text. Only `kind "ask"` runs are checked; a fault shows the text as it was. The debug log keeps IDs and kinds, never words.
- **The plain "I can't" (BL-314):** a `CONFIRM:` for something the panel can never do, whatever is confirmed (`claims.cant`: send, forward or reply to mail; post in Teams; create, move, cancel or answer an invite; change SharePoint or OneDrive files online; start, stop or install the live server, the app or the login agent), is not offered as **Go ahead**: the server emits a `cant` event `{what, line, go}` instead, and the page (`cantCard`) shows `.msg.a.cant`, one sentence ("ask creaite can't send, forward or reply to email: Microsoft 365 actions stay off in the panel. …") and a button to where it can be done (`go`: `{email, mode}` opens Draft email for that person, else People; `{page, arg}`: Today, Files, Settings › Live server). A refused tool call of the same kind (`claims.cant_denied`: a Microsoft 365 action tool, `live_server.py`, `launch.py`, `launchctl`) leads the `denied` lines with the same event. The sentence is kept in the chat's history. `ASK_PROMPT` tells Claude to say "I can't" first and never to CONFIRM these. Wording and routing only: what is allowed or refused is decided by the guard (reach.py, the hook, the tool lists), unchanged (test_guard_pinned). Routing chat changes through the dashboard's own actions (BL-314's "Same actions") is built too, on Alan's guard decision: see "ask creaite's changes through the dashboard's own actions" below.
- **A run creaite started itself (BL-326, v1.9.5):** every run of the run engine whose kind is not `ask` (a chat) or `search` (a search answer) is a run creaite started: `prep` (the Prep page, its queue and BL-307's Go ahead), `generate` (Generate, Make it executive, every AI draft on `/api/draft` including Draft agenda, Polish and the status forms, Shape this meeting, Draft from the message), `shape-closed`, `check` and `load` (a meeting's transcript) and `sweep` (Close the day's catch-up). `Run.plain` says so and `summary()` carries `plain: true`. Wherever ask creaite shows one it shows three things and nothing else:
  - **the ask:** the run's `label`, one plain line as if Alan had typed it, never its prompt: `prep_page.ask_line` ("Prep page for <meeting>, Thursday 15th October 10:00"), `drafts.run_lines` (one line per draft kind, `RUN_LINES`), `current_lines` (Generate, Make it executive), `transcript_ask` ("Check the transcript of “…” (DD-MM-YYYY at HH:MM)", as Intake writes it), "Draft an action from the message", "Catch-up sweep: mail and Teams since the last sweep"; `PLAIN_ASK` for a kind started without one;
  - **the activity:** the working line and "✓ n steps", each step from `plain_step` (a creaite script, a file, a search, a Microsoft 365 tool in words: "Reading the tracker", "Searching mail", "Reading Teams channels"), never `describe_tool`'s command line; a chat keeps `describe_tool`;
  - **the closure:** one `closure` event `{ok, line, href?, link?, stopped?}` just before `done`, from `Run._closing`: Stop (`PLAIN_STOPPED`, or the kind's own: "Stopped: no prep page was written. Press Prep page to start again."; `PLAIN_TIMED_OUT` for the watchdog), a failure (`PLAIN_FAILED`: "Couldn't finish: … Try again; … Settings › Diagnostics has the details.", never the stderr line), else the run's closer (a Prep page is built as the run ends, `prep_close`: "Saved the prep page in <project> › <folders>" with `#prep=<dated key>`), what its job set (a draft: `drafts.run_closure`, or "Couldn't finish: the draft couldn't be read"; a transcript job's own message, with `#intake`), or the kind's done line and link (`#cockpit=CODE`, `#item=ID`, `#review`, `#prep=…`). A link is only ever a place in the dashboard (`#…`).
  - **The page:** `attachRun` draws any run (it no longer skips `generate`, `check` and `load`); `attachDraw` draws the label as Alan's message; `askStream(…, plain)` (from `attachRun`'s `run.plain`, and `jobLive`) draws only `step`, `closure`, the cards (`changes`, `editcard`, `fieldcard`) and `done`; `text`, `sources`, `draft`, `transcript`, `denied`, `error` and `stopped` are not drawn (the last two set the badge's outcome). `runClosure` draws `.msg.a.runend` (`.info` when not ok): the line, then " · " and an `a.hlink[data-runhref]` that goes there through `parseHash` and `restoreNav`. The feature's own reader (the Prep page button, Generate, the draft panes, Close the day) still reads the stream as before, its `text` included; `prepPage` and `runGen` now ack their run, so a run whose page saw its end isn't drawn again at the next load. Close the day's `cdSourceOf` also reads the plain step words.
  - **History:** `_close` keeps the run in the day's own chat, `runs-YYYY-MM-DD` ("Runs creaite started, DD-MM-YYYY"): the label as Alan's message, the closure line as the reply (`plain: true`, its `href` and `link`), the plain steps; never the prompt or the reply. The chat keeps the session Alan's own messages in it ran in (`keep_session`). One chat a day, so creaite's runs never crowd Alan's chats out of the ten kept. `loadChat` draws a `plain` reply with `runClosure`. The `done` event of such a run carries no chat or session, so the panel never switches to it.
  - **The run record:** the debug log has "<run> <kind> started: prompt n characters" and "<run> <kind> ended ok|failed|stopped: n steps, reply n characters" (IDs and counts only, BL-102), read in Settings › Health › Diagnostics. The VIP check is not a run of the run engine (`vip_check`, its own `claude -p`) and never shows in ask creaite. Display only: the guard, the tool lists and the routes are unchanged (test_guard_pinned, test_reach). Tests: `dev/tests/test_run_chat.py`.
- **Retired (BL-141):** the "Allow <rule>" confirm card, `/api/permissions/apply` and `/api/permissions/edit`, the `PERMISSION-EDIT:` and `PERMISSION-FILE:` cards and the permission-file run. `/api/permissions/undo` stays for rules added before.
- **Documents:** `report.py adhoc-data` is a read tool; building a document (`report.py adhoc` through the cx-document skill) is confirmed first like any write.

## ask creaite as the administrator: the one card and What ask creaite changed (BL-141, sprint-admin)

- **The card (`changesCardHtml`, `changesFlow`):** a `changes` event at the end of a run (or `/api/changes/pending` after a reload, `pendingCard`, which opens the chat on the card's chat) shows one `.confirm.chgcard` card: the title "<n> change(s) need(s) your approval", the line "Nothing has been changed yet. Approve makes all of them, Decline makes none. Each is backed up first and can be undone in Activity.", then a numbered list, one item per change: a **Sensitive** tag (`.tag.chgsens`: `--warn` text on `--warn-soft` with a border; the word is always there, colour is never the only cue; 4.5:1 or more in all five themes, light and dark), the title ("Change <path>", "Create <path>", "Delete <path>", "Move <path> → <path>", "Allow <rule>"), the reason and the run's why, and the change: the changed lines (`.permdiff`) for text, the sizes otherwise, the file for a delete. Items refused whatever is approved are listed under "Refused, whatever is approved". Buttons **Decline** and **Approve** (primary). The card's markup is all in `changesCardHtml`, so sprint-motion can animate it.
- **Approve** posts `/api/changes/approve {card}`; the card's buttons give way to "Approved" (or "Approved, with problems"), a message lists each result (✓ or ✗ with its line), a snack offers Activity, and the page sends "The approved changes are made. Carry on with my last request." in the same chat. **Decline** posts `/api/changes/decline` and shows "Declined: nothing was changed". An answered, replaced or expired (30 minutes) card says so and changes nothing. The page never holds an approval or the content: only the card's ID.
- **Risk tiers and the rollback line (BL-194, sprint-ask-creaite-access):** each item carries `tier` (`sensitive` or `high`) and `tier_reason` from `reach.tier`, which the server works out (the page only shows it). Each `<li>` is `.chgtier` on its tier's band: **Sensitive** (`.tier-sens`: `--warn-soft` ground, a 4px `--warn` left edge, the `.tag.chgsens` tag with a triangle icon, `alert`) or **High-risk** (`.tier-high`: `--bad-soft`, a `--bad` edge, `.tag.chghigh` with a shield icon, `shield`). The word and the icon are always there; text on a band is `--ink`. High-risk: a permission file (`.claude/settings.json` and `settings.local.json`, the vault's and `~/.claude`'s, `.mcp.json`), or new content that changes a permission key (`reach.permission_change`: allow, deny, ask, additionalDirectories, defaultMode, hooks, at any depth), so `system/config/settings.json` is High-risk when such a key changes and Sensitive otherwise. A High-risk item adds "High-risk because <tier_reason>." to its reason line and, under its change, the rollback line (`.chgback`): "Way back: undo it from Activity › What ask creaite changed, or run `python3 system/scripts/filechange.py undo`" with **Copy** (`data-chgcopy`; if the clipboard is refused, the command is selected and a snack says so). After Approve the command names that change (`… undo <n>`, from the result's "(change n"). With any High-risk item, a line under the title: "<n> is/are High-risk: it changes / they change what ask creaite is allowed to do. Read it/them closely." One Approve and one Decline for everything, as before. Every text pair on both bands is 4.5:1 or more and each band's edge 3:1 against the card, in all nine themes, light and dark.
- **Activity › What ask creaite changed (`renderAskChanges`):** below Activity's table, a heading, a line ("Files ask creaite created, changed, moved or deleted, today and the last seven days. Each was backed up first.") and a table: When (DD-MM-YYYY HH:MM), Tier (BL-194: the card's **Sensitive** or **High-risk** tag with its icon, "Ordinary" for a change made at once, "—" for one logged before 1.5.1), Change ("Created/Changed/Moved/Deleted <path>", "→ <path>" for a move, "approved on the card" when it was), Why, and **Undo** (`/api/changes/undo {id}`; "Undone" once undone). Empty: "ask creaite hasn't changed any files in the last seven days."; without the live server: "This list needs the live server."

## Intake (BL-70, sprint-today-brief)

- **The Inbox screen is renamed Intake** (it is where material comes in: files, meetings, Quick capture). What Alan reads changes: the rail label and tooltip, the page heading, the badge's accessible label ("n new files in Intake"), ⌘K ("Go to Intake", "Add files to Intake", "Process new Intake files", "Intake: process meeting …", the group for files waiting), the drop zone's label, the messages, the Files view's "Intake" entry and quick view, Help, the live server's "isn't in Intake any more", the sweep summary ("n transcript(s) to Intake (inbox/)") and the session-start line ("n files waiting in Intake (inbox/)"). The screen's hint says where files land: "saved to the inbox folder".
- **Names that stay:** the `inbox/` folder (`paths.INBOX`), the view id `inbox`, the `D.inbox` data key, `files_show_inbox`, the Files project marker `inbox`, the `/api/inbox/*` routes and the Settings source switch "Inbox" (that is Outlook's inbox, a sweep source). The address is `#intake`; `#inbox` still opens the screen, so saved links and the browser history work.

## Intake in three sections (BL-73, sprint-today-brief)

- **The page:** under the Intake heading and its hint, three framed panels (the theme's own card frame, so line, surface, radius and shadow follow the theme, light and dark), 28px apart, stacked full width in this order with the names exactly as Alan wrote them: **Calendar import** (`#isecCal`: the Meetings list from BL-42 phase 2, its "Calendar read …" line, Refresh, Select new, Process selected), **Upload files** (`#isecUp`: the drop zone, choose files, Select new and Process selected, and the table of files waiting in `inbox/`) and **Quick capture** (`#isecQuick`: the notes box and its fields, Clear and Process).
- **Each panel:** an icon (calendar, upload tray, pencil), an h2 heading (the panel is `aria-labelledby` it), a one-line purpose ("Pick meetings from your calendar and load their transcripts." / "Drop transcripts or minutes you already have." / "Type rough notes from a call or conversation that has no transcript."), and its own buttons; there is no shared toolbar. Narrow windows keep the stack.
- **One run at a time:** while ask creaite is busy the Process buttons in all three panels are disabled with the tooltip or message "ask creaite is busy; try again when it's finished".
- **Clear day (BL-104, Sprint 4a):** each day heading has **Clear day · n** and each row a **×**. They save the listed meetings' keys (the calendar event ID, or `day|start|title` without one) in `system/staging/intake-cleared.json` through `POST /api/intake/clear` (`clear`, `restore`, `restore_day`), so they stay out after Refresh, the next sweep and in later weeks; a meeting added to that day later shows as new. Entries older than the look-back window are dropped. `inbox_meetings()` leaves cleared meetings out of `rows` and lists them in `cleared`. Cleared is not processed: `processed.txt` and the tracker are untouched, and Today and meeting prep still show the meeting. Selection (Select new, Process selected) and the section's counts ignore cleared meetings; the Intake rail badge counts files only. A row whose `transcript` is `available` and not processed makes Clear day ask first, in the page (a bar with Clear anyway and Cancel). The message after clearing offers Undo; **Cleared (n)** under the list expands to the cleared meetings by day with Restore and Restore day. Needs the live server.
- **Upload files lists only uploads (BL-137, sprint-final):** `build_dashboard.inbox()` marks each file `uploaded` (false when the loader recorded an event ID against it, `event`); the page lists `uploadedFiles()` (`uploaded !== false` and no `event`), so a calendar-loaded file is never there at any stage, nothing is inferred from its name, and the "From calendar" tag is gone. Select new and Process selected act on uploads only. The meeting row shows **Loaded** (not processed yet), Processing, Draft ready or Already processed, and its Transcript cell has **Preview** and, before it is processed, **Remove** (`removeLoaded`: the transcript and its chat together, `/api/inbox/remove` for each, one Undo that restores both). The Intake badge and the "waiting" counts still count a loaded transcript that has not been processed (`D.inbox` keeps every file).

## Quick capture date and Title (BL-74, sprint-today-brief; retired with the form in v2.0.0, BL-301 C)

- **The date is picked, never typed:** a read-only field (`#qcDate`, `data-pick`) with a calendar button ("Choose date"); clicking the field or the button opens the calendar popup, choosing a day fills DD-MM-YYYY and closes it (stored YYYY-MM-DD, as before). The "Use DD-MM-YYYY" message is gone.
- **Always today when opened:** the form shows today when it opens (the notes box, ⌘K "Quick capture", "note: …" in ask creaite) and again after Process or Clear; the popup's Today button resets it. A failed save re-opens with the date that was tried.
- **Past dates only:** `data-max="today"` greys out and disables later days (and the popup stays on or before today); `data-noclear` hides the popup's Clear for this field.
- **Popup options, for every date field (`openCal`):** `data-max` (a latest day, or "today"), `data-noclear`, and a read-only field that opens the popup (`data-pick`; Enter, Space or ↓ also open it). **Keyboard:** the popup takes focus on the selected day; ← → move by a day, ↑ ↓ by a week, Home and End within the week, PageUp and PageDown by a month; Enter or Space chooses, Esc closes and returns focus to the button. The day buttons have the date as their label.
- **Title:** the Title box is 400px wide (about 2.5 times before), `max-width:100%`; the row wraps as before, and below 640px the Title takes its own line at full width. Project, Date and Who was there keep their sizes.

## The live server check (BL-71; the rail dot removed by BL-143)

- **No dot in the rail (BL-143; was `#railLive`, BL-71 and BL-82):** the live server check replaces it. Stopped: the red `#liveAlert` (the restart command, Copy, **Check again**). Running: nothing is shown. The file version of the dashboard while the server runs: the alert's "The live server is running. This copy of the dashboard is the file version; open the live dashboard…". What the dot's tooltip said (when mail and Teams were last swept, when the data was built) is a plain line, **Mail, Teams and data**, in Settings › System › Live server and backups.
- **Top bar (BL-81):** the search box at the left; ask creaite and the theme button at the right edge (`#askBtn` carries the auto margin, so they stay there whether or not the status strip shows; with the strip showing it sits just before ask creaite). "Swept …", "Data …" and Live are gone; the held-changes count and the vault button (Connect, Reconnect, Read-only) remain as a small strip that is empty (and takes no space) when neither applies. The red alert banner is unchanged.

## Today as a brief (BL-72, sprint-today-brief)

The style reference is `dev/reference/morning-brief-style.html`: the markup, styles and structure of the Morning Brief with fictional content only (FAL, ORX, MER, TID; Jane Doe, Omar Ali, Sara Example). Alan's original holds real names and is never copied into the repo.

- **Layout:** the headline band (date, headline, day line, meetings, as before) and every section sit in one `.col80` column: 80% of the page, centred; at 640px and below, full width with 16px gutters. Section headings are small uppercase and bold (`.th`, weight 700 in every theme by `#tBrief h2.th`, because a theme's own `h2` rule is more specific than `.th`; BL-79); items are numbered sentences (`ol.bl`) with hairlines between them and no cards; bold linked titles (`a.t`), links inside the sentence (`a.s`). Colours are the theme's own `--day-*` tokens, so every theme and mode follows.
- **Order (BL-319, v1.9.2: Today as one home):** the day band (BL-293's, unchanged, at full width), then in `#tBrief.thome` (980px reading width, centred; full width under 640px): **To decide** (`#tdSec`), **First thing** (BL-295), **Decisions needed** (BL-210, only when one waits), **Needs attention**, **Your commitments** with **You owe** and **Waiting on** inside it (`.tsub`, BL-125 A, only when they have any), **Week ahead**, **Resolved** (`#resFold`, a closed `<details>`: one line, "Resolved n since …") and **Portfolio** (as before, with BL-285's hover).
- **The 8px rhythm (BL-319):** 28px between sections, rows at least 44px (8px padding), one heading style (`.th`: 13px, uppercase, 700, the count in `.cnt` at 400), no boxes.
- **Row icons (BL-319):** row actions are icon buttons: `IconButton` (src/components/IconButton.jsx, the component form of `ibtn`) in the Preact sections, `tib()` in the text-drawn ones. `data-tip` names the action on hover and keyboard focus; `aria-label` adds the row's words. `.tacts` sits at 55% opacity until the row is hovered or has focus (100% without hover); nothing moves. Every action has Undo: approve and reject through `decide` (the apply's Undo, or `applySoon`'s), a message's dismiss its own, Mark done and Move the date are staged through `pkCommit` (Undo is `pkUndoLast`). The reminders' **…** menu stays (rare actions).
- **To decide (BL-319):** `toDecideRows()` in today.js, drawn by `components/today/ToDecide.jsx` into `#tdBody`: every pending proposal (time from its file name), every Intake meeting with a transcript ready (`mtgHasTx`) and every unseen alert from a followed person (`VIPS()`, tag "Message", never labelled, BL-288), newest first; five show (`EXPAND` key `td`), then "Open Review (n)". Icons: **Approve and save** only when `tdApprovable` (`routineKid` and not a draft or file change: the bulk rule), else **Open in Review** (`tdToReview`, highlighted); **Reject, with a reason** (not on drafts or file changes) opens BL-315's reasons menu (`rjMenuFor`, after `rjLoadGuesses`: the same menu as Review, with the row's guess and Undo); **Process the transcript** (`processMeetings`, live server); a message's **Make a work item** and **Dismiss** keep `data-alertdo` for settings.js's handler. After a row goes, focus moves to the same action on the row in its place (`tdFocusAfter`, `alertFocus`).
- **Needs attention:** worded from templates in the page (`attentionItems`, no model call). **BL-319 (Alan, 14-10-2026):** no per-meeting proposal groups and no **Approve n routine** any more: proposals are To decide's alone, and nothing on Today shows a staging file name. First the pending decisions' line, then report reminders, each with a **…** menu (Run report now, Dismiss for this session, Dismiss forever). The top five show, then **+n more** (and "Show fewer").
- **Your commitments:** your overdue and due-soon actions (title, how late or soon, project, latest update).
- **You owe and Waiting on (BL-125 A):** two sections after Your commitments (`#tOwe`, `#tWait`, drawn by `renderPromises` in `today.js`), each hidden when empty. The data is `D.promises`, built by `build_dashboard.promises_data`: the open promises on active projects made by Alan or to him (`{id, p, t, by, to, due, raised, src, od, quiet, list}`); `list` comes from `tracker.promise_lists`, so the page, `tracker.py read promises --list owe|waiting` and the weekly report agree. **You owe:** `list` "owe", soonest due first, undated last; each line "To <person> on <project>: due in n days" (late in `.late`); the heading's count adds "· n overdue". **Waiting on:** `list` "waiting" (made to Alan, past due), most overdue first; each line "From <person> on <project>: n days late, quiet for n days" (`quiet`: days since raised or the last mention in `mentions.jsonl`). The heading links to Work's **Chase view** (`data-nav="all:chase:"`). People link to their page (`data-person`), never labelled VIP (BL-288). Capped at `listCap` with "+n more". A promise between two other people is in neither list.
- **Resolved:** replaces "Changed since your last visit": actions and RAID items whose status changed to Closed since the last visit (from the change log, ignoring undone changes), top five then "+n more", with **See all activity** (Activity). Empty: "Nothing has been resolved since <date>."
- **Week ahead:** from `D.week`, built by `build_dashboard.week_ahead` (`week_window` for the window): BL-319, day by day from today to next Monday (a Monday looks to the Monday after). Drawn by `components/today/WeekAhead.jsx` from `weekGroups()`: per row a tag (kind and priority), the title (opens the item, a meeting's Prep, a milestone's project), who and the project, why it is there (`.wkwhy`: due, due over the weekend, n days overdue, needed by, n days late, due · at risk or on track, worth preparing) and its icons (`workActs`). Pending decisions needed in the window land on their day; open work already late (of the priorities `dashboard.week_ahead_priorities` lists; `week_late`) and late decisions lead the first day with `od`; a milestone carries `slip`. A line under the heading says what the setting lists ("Urgent work, every milestone and decision, and meetings"); nothing is hard-coded. Today's own meetings stay in the band, and today's actions that are yours stay under Your commitments. Per day: meetings from the saved calendar, open actions and RAID items due (any owner), and milestones by forecast date. **No weekly or client report lines (BL-127)**: those are the reminders in Needs attention (`build_dashboard.reminders`), so the same job never shows twice; the Week ahead no longer previews a report before it is due. A weekend day appears only when the calendar has a meeting on it; other items due on a weekend move to the following Monday, marked "due over the weekend". Days with nothing are skipped; up to six lines a day show, then "+n more".
- **Portfolio:** the table, last, without a card frame. BL-285: one line per project; the project's description is not printed under its name but is the name's tooltip (`data-tip`; the name is a `button.pfname` that opens the project, so keyboard focus shows it too), read by screen readers from a hidden span (`aria-describedby`); an empty description has no tooltip. The columns (`th[data-col]`: project, health, evidence, trend, next) resize with Work's resizer (`colResizer`: pointer drag, the keyboard separator ←/→, 60 to 900 px, double-click resets), remembered in `creaite-colwidths` under `portfolio|<column>`; a resize never opens the project. Projects shows the same table with the same widths.
- **Unchanged:** the day line and meeting list, the Today ⌘K entry, Quick capture (on Intake) and the schema. `CREAITE_NOW` ("YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM") fixes the build's clock, as in `sweep.py`.

## Current state: images and saving (BL-76, sprint-today-brief)

Found by testing in a real browser against the real live server (`dev/tests/test_current_state_live.py`, driven by `dev/tools/cdp.py`). Four faults, each fixed:
- **Dates inside names:** the page shows dates as DD-MM-YYYY by converting every `YYYY-MM-DD` in Current state. That also rewrote an image's file name (`images/2026-10-08-plan.png` became `images/08-10-2026-plan.png`) and a link's address, so a newly added picture showed "Image not found" after saving, and saving any text edit wrote the wrong name back into the project's `CLAUDE.md`. Now `fdMd` converts dates in the text only, never inside a Markdown link or image target or a web address.
- **A removed image came back:** saving put an image the text no longer had back at the end ("none is lost"). An image taken out with its × is now dropped from the text (the file stays in `images/`); a drafted text (Generate) still leaves the old images where they are.
- **A failed save lost the work:** the editor closed before the save had worked, so a project with no `CLAUDE.md`, a stopped server or a refused picture lost the text and the pictures. The editor now stays open, with everything in it, until the save has worked, says "Not saved" and why, and takes away any picture it had staged in `system/staging/uploads/` (`live_server.py` allows removing only those).
- **Clearer messages:** a file that isn't a PNG or JPEG, or is over 2 MB, is named: "IMG_0001.HEIC can't be added: only PNG or JPEG images".
Only the × removes a picture: Backspace and Delete don't, because its caption is editable.

## Work filters, second review (BL-77, sprint-today-brief)

Reviewed in a real browser against the real live server, with real clicks, in a vault of about 60 actions, 30 RAID items and 12 decisions, checking every result against an oracle written in Python (`dev/tests/work_data.py`, `dev/tests/test_work_filters_live.py`). Checked and correct: every option of every toolbar and "+ Filter" filter on all three tabs, filters combining (different filters AND, values within one OR), the menu counts, search (every word, any order, dates as shown), chips, Clear all, the built-in views, Save and delete a view, each tab keeping its own filters, sorting, the Files list (tree, quick views, name, type, From and To, the calendars), the People counts and sorting, and ticking in Review. Four faults, each fixed:
- **Every filter menu closed the instant it opened.** The opening click and the "close on a click elsewhere" listener were both on `document`, and `stopPropagation` doesn't stop a second listener on the same node, so the menu opened and closed in the same click. (The old tests clicked the button and read the menu's contents without checking it was open.) The closer now ignores clicks inside the filter bar.
- **"No filters at all" came back as "All open" after a refresh.** The address had nothing to say for a list with every chip removed (to see closed items too), so a refresh or a copied link fell back to the default view. The address now carries `&f=none` for that state.
- **The project page's "Urgent risks" figure opened a different list.** It counts Urgent only and opened the "Urgent and high" view (3 on the figure, 9 in Work). It now opens Open + Urgent (`urgentonly`).
- **"All mine" kept the last filters.** It left Work's earlier project filter and search in place, so "All mine" could show a handful of rows. It now clears them, opens Work on the Mine view, and has its own address (`#work=actions&v=mine`), so a refresh, Back and a copied link keep any change made to it.
FR-68 lists the promises. Not covered by a new real-click test: Intake's file and meeting lists, and the vault search (BL-07), which have their own tests.

## Side panel: ⌘+Enter saves and closes (BL-78, sprint-panel-header)

**(For actions, RAID items and decisions, replaced by the work item peek: BL-176, sprint-peek, below.)** New items, milestones, rebaselining and project health keep it.

- **The key:** ⌘+Enter (Ctrl+Enter on Windows) runs the open form's own Save, with the same checks, and closes the panel. One handler on `document` (`saveAndClose`), active only while the panel shows a form (`EDITING && CUR`) and the focus is in the panel (or on the page body). It ignores key repeat and a save already running (`CUR.saving`), and does nothing from the ask creaite box, search or the Current state editor, and nothing in the read view.
- **`savePanel(approve, closeAfter)`:** an action, RAID item or decision saved in place closes the panel (the Save button still returns to the read view); with no change it just closes. A new item, a proposal, health and a milestone already closed on success. A failed check or a question (a new owner, a new workstream) leaves the panel open with the message.
- **Review:** it saves the proposal and never approves it; "Save and approve" stays a button.
- **Plain Enter** is unchanged: a new line in a text area, and in the owner box it picks the highlighted person (the box ignores Enter when ⌘ or Ctrl is held).
- **Footer:** under the error line, "⌘↵ saves and closes · Esc cancels" (`#pKeys`, "Ctrl↵" on Windows), only while editing, and (BL-169, sprint-polish-2) only once a field has changed, or for a new item once its first field has focus; a redraw hides it again. Help's keyboard table has the row. (There is no shortcut list in ⌘K; it only jumps.)
- **Tests:** `dev/tests/test_side_panel_live.py`, a real browser with the real key.

## Side panel: half the window while editing (BL-80, sprint-panel-header)

**(For actions, RAID items and decisions, replaced by the work item peek: BL-176, sprint-peek, below.)** New items, milestones, rebaselining and project health keep it.

- **Width:** reading stays 420px (360px under 1100px). While the panel shows a form (`EDITING`) `drawPanel` gives it the class `pwide`: `max(420px, 50vw)` (`max(360px, 50vw)` under 1100px), so on a window under about 840px it is simply the usual width. The inner box (`.pin`) takes the same width; a percentage there would resolve to 0 in the grid's `auto` column. `closePanel` removes the class; Save, Cancel, Esc and Close all go through `drawPanel` or `closePanel`, so the width follows. The existing 0.15s width transition is reused. (The class is `pwide`, not `wide`: `.wide` is the full-width card class.)
- **Which panels:** Edit on an action, RAID item or decision, a new item, a milestone (edit, add, rebaseline), health and project details, and a proposal opened for editing in Review.
- **The list** gives up the room and keeps its selected row (highlighted, in view); the table scrolls sideways as it does when narrow.
- **ask creaite open too:** the chat is fixed at the right edge; with it open the wide panel sits to its left (`body:has(#chat.on) .panel.pwide{margin-right:var(--chatw)}`) and takes the room that is left, so neither covers the other.
- **Below 640px** the panel keeps `max(360px, 50vw)`; whether it should take the whole width on a phone-sized window is left open.
- **Tests:** `dev/tests/test_side_panel_live.py`, in a real browser at 1400, 1200, 1000 and 700px.

## Side panel: a white ground while editing (BL-80, sprint-panel-header)

- **No tint:** editing no longer changes the panel's background. `.pb.editing{background:var(--edit)}` and the `--edit` token (cream in the retired creaite standard and Minimals themes, violet in Brightwater, dark variants) are gone; the panel keeps its normal surface: white in light mode in all three themes, the theme's dark surface in dark mode (unchanged from reading, not a bright white).
- **How editing still shows:** the "Editing — changes apply when you save" banner (now `--ink`, which reads on every surface; the amber and the Brightwater override are gone), the form fields with their borders, the half-window width, and the Save and Cancel buttons.
- **Test:** `test_today_brief.py` (`EditGroundTests`): in every theme and mode the panel's ground while editing is the ground while reading, white in light mode, and the banner meets 4.5:1.

## creaite theme (2026-10-08, was creaite teal; the default since BL-142)

- A named theme (`dashboard.palette` "creaite", second in `THEMES`; its ask creaite button and panel heading are teal) after the creaite how-to and first-day guides: ground `#F3F6F7`, white paper, ink `#14212A`, muted `#4D5C66`, hairlines `#D7DFE3`, teal `#0B6E7F` with `#DCEFF2`, amber `#8A5A00` with `#FBF0DA`; dark `#0F1518` / `#171E22` / `#E4EAED`, teal `#5FC4D6` with `#16363C`. Type: Schibsted Grotesk for headings (800, tight tracking), Source Sans 3 for text, JetBrains Mono for the date eyebrow, table headings and keys (named first; the vendored fonts come with BL-84, the fallbacks show until then). Cards 12px with hairline borders, no shadows.
- **Today follows it:** unlike the other themes, it sets Today's own `--day-*` tokens (a cool page, a pale teal band, teal for the sun and the now dot, the display face for the headline) in light, system-dark and forced-dark. `--day-page` is every page's ground, so the whole dashboard takes the guides' ground.
- Tests: `CreaiteTealTheme` (the guide tokens and type, Today's tokens in all three blocks with contrast, AA contrast for every text colour on every ground in both modes).

## Draft email (BL-83, sprint-draft-email)

- **What it is:** a popup, opened from a person's page, with a ready-to-send email to that person asking where their open items stand. Alan copies it and pastes it into Outlook. creaite never sends mail and never creates an Outlook draft; opening and copying write nothing (no proposal, tracker row, Activity entry or file).
- **The builder (`draftEmail`, in its own `Draft email (BL-83)` section):** a pure function of the person, the ticked items, the options and the time. It returns `{to, subject, html, text, items}`. Greeting by the time generated ("Good morning" before 12:00, "Good afternoon" to 17:59, "Good evening" from 18:00) and the first name; fixed opening and closing (no model call); one block per project (code and name) with a table of ID, item, due and priority; risks and issues also show their type and "Response: …"; order overdue (oldest first), then soonest, then undated; an optional "It would be helpful to hear back by Friday 15-10-2026." from the reply-by date; "Kind regards," and Alan's first name.
- **Colour, never alone:** overdue is red (`#B3261E`) and bold with "n days overdue"; due within the due-soon setting is amber (`#8A5A00`) with "due in n days" (or today or tomorrow); Urgent is bold. The values are one table, `DE_COLOURS` (red, amber and the ink all at least 4.5:1 on white and on the header row `#F1F3F4`).
- **Built for Outlook's Word engine:** inline styles on every element, tables with `cellpadding` and `cellspacing` and a bottom border on each cell, `font-family:Calibri,Arial,sans-serif` at 11pt, no classes, ids, variables, flexbox, images or web fonts. The preview is always a white card, whichever theme is on.
- **Subject:** project code(s) first, sorted and joined with " / ": `ORX · Update request: 4 open items (1 overdue)`; one item: `ORX-A-003 · Update request`. It reflects the ticked items and is editable.
- **The popup (`openDraftEmail`):** To (with Copy; "No email recorded"), Subject (with Copy), a tick box per item grouped by project, Include latest update (off), Please reply by (a read-only picker; only today and later, using the calendar's new `data-min` option beside `data-max`), the preview (editable in place), Copy email, Copy as plain text and Close. When options change after the preview has been edited, a note offers "Redraw it (your edits are lost)" instead of redrawing silently.
- **Copying:** Copy email writes the preview's current HTML and its text together with `navigator.clipboard.write([new ClipboardItem({"text/html", "text/plain"})])`, so Outlook pastes the table and colours, and what is on screen (with Alan's edits) is what is copied. If the browser refuses, the preview (or the field) is selected and the popup says "press ⌘C to copy". "Copied ✓" confirms each copy.
- **The safeguard:** `identity.own_domains` reaches the page as `S.ownDomains` (`build_dashboard.ui_settings`). An address not on one of those domains, or no address on record, is treated as outside: items not flagged client-visible are unticked and tagged "internal". Items marked confidential or restricted, and items whose source is in a project's `documents/`, are never offered (the popup says how many were left out); an item's source is never in the email; the latest update only if ticked.
- **Prep 1:1 is gone** (`p1Dlg`, `openP1`, the `#pp1` button). Hand over, the "Closed in the last 14 days" card (`P1_DAYS`), the warnings on rows and the key-person note stay.
- **Tests:** `dev/tests/test_people_dashboard.py` (`DraftEmailBuilderTests`, `DraftEmailPopupTests`) and `dev/tests/test_workstreams.py` (the button).

## Files with projects in OneDrive (BL-85, sprint-onedrive-projects)

- **Same four groups, other folders.** With `locations.projects_root` set, the Files view lists each project by its kind of file as before: **minutes** and **transcripts** (the mapped meeting folders), **reports** (the `Weekly` folder, where creaite files weekly and client reports together, and `Adhoc`), and **documents**: everything in the engagement folder outside the mapped meeting and report folders, `_Archive`, `_Templates`, creaite's images folder and the project's `CLAUDE.md`. A document's sub-folder (`00 Admin › Contract`) shows as its path below the engagement folder; agendas (`02 Meetings/Agendas`) are documents. Without the setting nothing changes.
- **Paths:** each row's path is stored as `projects/<engagement folder>/<path inside it>`, and Open file and Open folder go through the live server's `paths.resolve`, which allows only the vault and the projects root. The server's `fs` rules map the current-state image folder to the mapped one (`D.image_dirs`).
- **Dates:** a meeting's date is read from creaite's `YYYY-MM-DD Title` name or from Teams' (`Title-YYYYMMDD[_HHMMSS][UTC][-Meeting Transcript|Recording]`, `Title-YYYYMMDD`); a meeting with no date in its name takes the file's own date. Other files show a date only when their name has one.
- **Online-only files** (OneDrive placeholders) are still listed but are never read or indexed; `doctor.py` names them.
- **Review:** items extracted from a meeting whose date came from the file are flagged `date-from-file` and Review says "Meeting date taken from the file".
- **Output folders (Alan, 2026-10-08):** client reports file to `04 Reporting/Weekly`, with the weekly reports. `04 Reporting/Steerco` is separate: creaite never writes to it, and what is in it is indexed as a document. Current-state images file to `04 Reporting/Content`, which creaite creates the first time an image is filed (a missing folder is not an error). Both are in the `locations.folders` map and can be changed.

## Permissions and the assistant (BL-93, BL-96, BL-100, BL-86)

- **ask creaite and files:** it reads every project document, and in a confirmed request creates a file in a project folder (never overwriting) or changes, renames, moves or deletes the one file Alan named, through `filechange.py` (earlier version saved first, logged, undoable). It may edit one existing vault file at a time (CLAUDE.md, memory and portfolio files, inbox, schedules, drafts, templates, skills) the same way, and never the database, generated files, history, backups, releases or the engine.
- **Permission cards:** a run that needs a rule ends with `PERMISSION: <rule> | <why>` (Add the rule); a change to a permission file ends with one `PERMISSION-EDIT: <spec> | <why>` line each, shown as a card with the exact rule or setting added or removed and a Make this change button, never in bulk. The server carries it out (`permissions.py edit`, backed up, logged, with Undo in the message). For a freeform change to `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json` or `system/config/settings.json`, the run ends with `PERMISSION-FILE: <path> | <the exact change>`: the card names the file and the change, and Make this change starts a confirmed run that can edit that one file only, with its deny entries lifted for that run and put back after it; the result message has an Undo.
- **Images:** a paperclip beside the box, paste (⌘V / Ctrl+V) and drop on the panel; up to four PNG or JPEG images, shrunk to about 1600px and under 2 MB; thumbnails above the box with × to remove, click to enlarge; the bubble and History show them. They are deleted with their chat.
- **App:** the page links `/manifest.webmanifest`, registers `/sw.js`, keeps `<meta name="theme-color">` equal to the current `--bg`, and posts `/api/here` every 10 seconds. Settings › Live server and backups notes that the app belongs to the port and has Stop the live server.

## Mail, calendar and alerts (BL-97, BL-99, BL-98; sprint-sweep-alerts)

- **Ignore rules (BL-97):** Settings › Capture and alerts › Sweep ends with **Ignore rules**, read from `D.sweep_alerts.ignore` (`build_dashboard.sweep_alerts`, the rules as `sweep.py ignore_rules()` returns them). Each row: a switch, the name (a **built in** tag on the private rule), a one-line description ("Mail, Calendar · Subject or title begins with “todo”"), and Edit and Delete (none on the built-in rule). **Add a rule** and Edit open an inline form: name, where (Mail, Teams, Calendar), looks at (subject or title, sender, Teams channel or chat), match (begins with, contains, is exactly; for a sender also is the address or is in the domain) and text. Every change writes the whole `sweep.ignore` list through `writeSettings` (backup and log line). Names must be unique: the sweep's counts are keyed by them. The JS is in the marked "Sweep and alerts" block; it adds the list to the Sweep card by wrapping `renderSettings`.
- **VIPs (BL-99):** `D.sweep_alerts.vip` holds `{enabled, notify, check_minutes, last_check, alerts (unseen, from system/staging/vip.json), people: {name: kind}}`. `attentionItems` is wrapped so each unseen alert comes first in Today's Needs attention (class `vipl`): a **VIP** tag, "who: subject" linking to the message (new tab), then kind, sent time, project or "no project", a private or confidential marker, "found on start-up, received …" for a start-up find, and the gist; **Seen** posts `/api/vip/seen` (needs the live server). A badge on Today in the rail and "(n VIP)" in the window title count the unseen alerts. With the live server the page polls `/api/vip` every minute, redraws Today when the set changes and shows a browser notification for an alert that arrived while it was open (permission asked once; `vip.notify`). `renderReview` is wrapped to put proposals flagged `vip` first and tag them VIP. The person page and Settings › People show a VIP chip; the person dialog gains a **VIP** select (No, Management, Client, Yes) when editing, saved in the register's VIP column (an extra column, so the People code carries it unchanged). Settings › Sweep gains VIP alerts on/off, the check interval and the notification switch, with the honest limit spelled out.
- **Meeting prep ranked by the invite (BL-98 part 1):** `build_dashboard.prep` now covers today's meetings (key `HH:MM|title`) and tomorrow's (`YYYY-MM-DD|HH:MM|title`), each with `invite` (body as plain text, attendees, attachment names, organiser, recurrence, a confidential flag) and `rank` (`{actions|raid|decisions: {id: [score, reason]}}` from `prep_rank`: shared word stems weighted by how rare they are among the project's items, title words counting half again; a workstream or milestone the invite names; an owner who is invited or named; scores of 2 or more count). `calendar()` strips the body, attendees, attachments and organiser address from `D.calendar`, so invite text is embedded only in the Prep data. `drawPrep` lifts ranked items to the top of each list (highest first) with a `.prepwhy` line, says how many the invite made relevant, and adds a collapsed **Invite** section. The Week ahead puts a **Prep** link on tomorrow's meetings (`data-prepday`); the dialog says "Tomorrow".
- **Prep page (BL-98 part 2):** the Prep brief has a bar under its intro: **Prep page** (disabled with no project; titled "Needs the live server" without it, and refused with a message) and **Open last prep page** when `D.sweep_alerts.prep_pages` has one for the meeting. Pressing it opens a placeholder tab at once (so it isn't blocked as a pop-up), streams `/api/prep/page` (steps counted, **Stop** posts `/api/ask/stop`), then posts `/api/prep/build` and writes the returned page into that tab (or offers a link). With projects in OneDrive (`agendas_shared`) it asks once before the first save, since the Agendas folder may be shared. **Confidential notes (BL-301, plan #60):** `prep_page.context` lists only the notes not marked confidential (`research.notes`; `research.confidential_notes_left_out` counts the rest, never by name; both keys only when there are any), the research prompt (`NOTES_RULE`) and the shaping prompt (`drafts._shape_research`) tell Claude never to open another file in a notes folder, and `prep_page.hold_confidential` drops every finding whose source is a confidential note (its path or file name) and every finding, question, item reason or summary that quotes one (eight words in a row) before `build` renders and stores the page; `prep_view.assemble` applies it again, so the in-app view and a regenerated page drop a note marked confidential later. **In code (#60, Alan, 15-10-2026: option (a) and its follow-ups):** every run creaite starts itself that may read files (the Prep page, Shape the meeting, Current state's draft and rewrite, the AI drafts, a transcript check or load: `live_server.held_run`; Search's answer is treated like ask creaite) is started with the guard hook in mode `held` (`--settings`, set by the live server) and `CREAITE_RUN_KIND=<its kind>` in its environment; Close the day's sweep keeps mode `sweep` and adds `--held 1`. The hook (`reach.decide`, `prep_decision`) refuses a Read of a confidential note (or of any Markdown file marked confidential), a Grep, Glob or LS whose folder holds one, a creaite script given one's path, its typed draft and, while any note is confidential, the database and the backups (fail closed: an unreadable or online-only note counts as confidential), and in mode `held` a Bash call that isn't one creaite script on its own. In such a run `search.py query` leaves a confidential note out entirely (no title, link or match on its words) and `preview` answers as for a file the index doesn't hold. `search.py context` fails closed for every caller (`notes.held`), and in such a run leaves out its count of confidential notes that matched. Runs with no file tools (a narrowed Microsoft 365 draft, notes-find, edit-ai, a closed meeting's shaping) are unchanged, and so are ask creaite's chats, typed sweeps and confirmed requests. The run's read tools could still open a note by path: no guard rule stops that (reach and the tool lists are unchanged); what it brings back is filtered.
- **VIPs elsewhere (BL-99):** `cell` is wrapped so owner (`o`) and made-by (`by`) cells add a VIP tag; `FDEFS` gains a `vip` filter on all three lists (under "+ Filter", in the address as `vip=`); `openDraftEmail` is wrapped to add a VIP note at the top; `WSIG` gains `vip_overdue`; a weekly report reminder with `vip` (unseen VIP messages about the project) says so in its line.

## Dashboard pages (BL-87 to BL-95, sprint-pages)

No schema changes. One marked section in `dashboard.html`, `/* ===== Dashboard pages (BL-87 to BL-95) ===== */`.

### Files page: the real report folders (BL-87)
- **What is listed:** minutes, transcripts and reports (and the portfolio's minutes and transcripts), plus Intake. Reports are in three folders, carried in the file's `sub`: **Weekly** (weekly and client reports are filed together), **Adhoc** and **Steerco**. The tree is project › reports › Weekly, Adhoc, Steerco; the Location column shows the same; a chip per folder filters across projects. Left out: agendas, milestones, images, every other numbered folder, `_Archive`, `_Templates`.
- **Steerco** is `locations.folders.steerco` (default `04 Reporting/Steerco`; `reports/Steerco` in the default layout), read through `paths.folder`. creaite never writes there. Settings made before the key existed fall back to the default. Steerco is excluded from the document roots, so a file is never both a report and a document.
- **Report labels** (`paths.report_files`): Steerco first, then the weekly and client folder (Weekly), then the ad hoc folder; a file is listed once. When ad hoc documents share the weekly folder (the default layout's `reports/`), a file whose name says ad hoc is Adhoc.
- **Documents are set aside:** `dashboard.files_show_documents` (default off, beside `files_show_inbox` and `files_show_portfolio`; Settings › Work and views › Files). Off, `files()` neither lists nor walks them (`paths.project_files(..., documents=False)`). Search indexing (`tracker.index_files`) is unchanged and still reads documents.
- **Latest reports** is the newest file in each of Weekly, Adhoc and Steerco per project, by the date in its name, else its modified date (`mod`), not by file name.
- **Fresh when you look:** opening Files or Intake, and the window regaining focus (at most once every 10 seconds, on those pages only), call `GET /api/files` (live server, token; returns `{ok, files, inbox, at}`: only the two file lists, from `build_dashboard.files_view()`, which uses the same `files()` and `inbox()` as a full build). **Refresh** does it at once. A scan over one second shows "Checking…"; a failed one keeps the old list with "Couldn't refresh the file list (…); showing the list from 14:32". The note reads "Updated 14:32."; a dashboard opened from disk keeps "File list as of the last build." and Refresh says it needs the live server.
- **Keeps your place:** the filters, the selected file (`FSELFILE`), the scroll position and the open folders are not re-created by a refresh. Files that appeared since the last look get a **New** tag and the count line says "3 new"; opening the page again clears them. Files that went disappear.

### Files search: a centred bar with an ask creaite button (BL-88)
- **Layout:** `.vsearch` is a container (`cqw` units). The bar is centred at about 50% of the page, 420px at least and 760px at most; the **ask creaite** button sits immediately to its right on the same line, both 40px high. The filter chips, the note, the answer and the results are one centred column (`.vscol`) at 80% of the page; the tree, quick chips and file table stay at 100%. Under 800px the column is full width, the bar takes the whole line and the button is on its own line below, right-aligned.
- **Text:** placeholder "Search the vault"; the examples ("risks ABC last month", "what did Sara say about UAT?") are a muted, centred hint line under the bar (`#vsHint`) that hides once there is text.
- **The button** (`#vsAskBtn`, `data-state`): **idle** (no text; dimmed: the surface fill with an accent edge and label), **ready** (text, not a question: the theme's accent fill), **question** (the same, ringed, with a small ↵: Enter will ask), **answering** ("Answering…", the spark pulses; a **Stop** button (`#vsAskStop`) beside it does what the answer's Stop does). A pill with the app icon's four-point spark, a soft top highlight and shadow, a one-pixel lift on hover and press down on click; the spark turns slowly when the pointer is near. All motion stops under `prefers-reduced-motion`. Fill: creaite standard its teal with white text; Minimals its green; Brightwater Charcoal with Off-White text and a Signal Green spark on light, Signal Green with Charcoal text and spark on dark. The label meets 4.5:1 on its fill and the edge 3:1 against the page, in every theme and mode; focus has a ring. Tooltip: "ask creaite: an answer with a link to the source for every point."
- Without the live server a click shows the existing "Live server needed" note with the copy-command button.

### Current state in two resizable panes (BL-89)
- **One message, two panes:** the card keeps its heading **Current state**; the panes have no captions. Both are saved in the project's one `## Current state` section of `CLAUDE.md`, left first, with `<!-- creaite: second pane -->` between them (`docx_tools.PANE_MARKER`; `PANE` in the page). A text without the marker is just the left pane; with an empty right pane no marker is written. `docx_tools.split_panes`, `join_panes` and `one_message` (and `splitPanes`, `joinPanes` in the page) are the only places that know the marker. Only a marker outside every HTML comment counts: one quoted inside another comment (the project template's guidance quotes it) is text (Alan, 13-10-2026; `docx_tools.pane_markers` and `paneMarkers` in the page, kept in step).
- **Other readers see one continuous piece:** `md_blocks` ends the paragraph at the marker and drops it (`panes=True` keeps it, for `md_normalise`, which keeps the first and drops a trailing one), so the Word weekly and client reports, the approval check and search read the left pane's content then the right's with nothing between. `report.current_entries` treats the marker as a paragraph break. Images stay where they are placed, in either pane; `Client-safe: no` lines stay out of client reports in either.
- **Weekly report:** `report.replace_current` keeps the existing right pane when the new text has no marker (the right pane is Alan's own writing), takes a text with the marker as it stands, and puts the marker back where it was when the text comes back from Word as one piece. cx-weekly-report's instructions say so.
- **Generate** drafts the left pane only: `report.current_context` gives the left and right panes under separate headings, `current-state-prompt.md` tells Claude to read the right as part of what is said and never write the marker, and `genDraft` fills the left pane and leaves the right alone.
- **The divider:** `.curdiv` (role separator, `aria-valuenow`/min/max). Drag; width kept in this browser (`creaite-cur-split`, a percentage, shared by projects; default 62, never under 25 or over 75); double-click resets; ← → move it by 2, Home and End jump to the limits. The button on it folds the right pane into a 30px strip (`creaite-cur-collapsed`); the strip's dot shows when the pane holds text. The reading view's divider is a quiet line, thicker and accented when hovered or focused. **With nothing in the right pane (BL-117) the reading view still draws the same frame** (`.curpanes.read.solo`: the left pane in its saved share, a 10px gap and an empty, `aria-hidden` right column), with no divider, fold button or dot, so the left text keeps its width when notes are added or removed; the saved collapsed state is ignored there. There is no **Add notes** link (BL-116): the pencil opens both panes, and a click puts the cursor in the right one. Under 760px the panes stack, left on top, with no divider, and a solo view's empty column is not drawn, so the text is full width.
- **Editing:** the pencil opens both panes as rich-text areas under one toolbar (`.rtebar`), which acts on the pane the cursor is in; an image lands in that pane. One Save and Cancel, ⌘↵ saves, Esc cancels with the "Discard changes?" guard. One proposal, one apply, one Undo; **Activity** shows one line "ABC · Name — current state updated" for the apply (`build_dashboard.file_activity`, from the applied proposal's title, so it survives a reload).

### No due date: undated open work on the project page (BL-95)
- **Rule** (`build_dashboard.undated_counts` and `undatedWork` in the page, which agree): every action that is Open or Delayed with no due date, and every Open risk or issue with no due date (the response action's date); an assumption or a dependency only when it has a response action to date. Never a decision, a closed or a cancelled item; other projects' items don't count. Owners other than Alan count too (he chases the date).
- **The card** (`#undatedCard`): directly under "Actions due or overdue" in `renderCockpit`, hidden when nothing is undated, with no cap. The heading "No due date · n" counts actions and RAID items together, so it has no link of its own (a number beside a link always equals the list the link opens); two "All n ABC undated actions ›" and "All n ABC undated RAID items ›" links go to Work: the project, status open, Due "No date", for the actions (`actions:undated:CODE`) and for the RAID items (`raid:undated:CODE`). Each row: ID, icon, text, owner · priority and a **Set date** button (`data-setdate`) that opens the item's panel in edit mode on the due date; saving is a normal proposal, applied as usual, and the row leaves the card.
- **Quiet nagging:** the count is a "No due date" box in the evidence row after Overdue actions (it scrolls to the card, since it can't open one Work list), and "No date n" in the Evidence column of the Projects table (and Today's portfolio), never red. `projects[].undated` ({actions, raid, total}) carries the same count in the data.
- **Grace:** items raised within `dashboard.undated_grace_days` (default 3; Settings › Work and views › Project page) show dimmer, tagged "new" (muted text, never less readable); they are still listed and counted.
- **Work's "No date" filter** counts every open item without a date, including an assumption or dependency with no response; the card leaves those out by the rule above.

### Trends and flow (BL-91)
- **Measures** (`build_dashboard.flow_data`, in the data as `flow[code]`, per active project; counts only, never about a person): per week, Monday to Sunday and the last one this week so far, **opened** (by `raised`), **closed** and **cancelled** (by `closed`, counted apart; "closed" means Closed) for actions, for risks and issues, and for everything together; **overdue at the end of the week**; **ageing** of the open items by days since raised (0 to 7, 8 to 14, 15 to 30, over 30) with the oldest three; the **open risks and issues by priority**; and **milestone movement** (forecast moves in the period and the net days, per milestone for the top three). `dashboard.flow_weeks` is the period (8; kept between 4 and 26).
- **Overdue over time:** an item is overdue at the end of a day when it was open then and its due date then was earlier. The due date then is rebuilt from `change_log` (the last change on or before the day, else the old value of the first change after it, else today's; an undone apply never happened). A daily snapshot, `system/history/flow-YYYY-MM-DD.json` ({date, projects: {code: {overdue, open}}}, written by every `build_dashboard.build()` and overwritten the same day), is used for a past week's end when there is one; today is always worked out afresh. A file, not a table: no schema change.
- **The Flow card** (`flowCard`, full width under the project cards): five inline-SVG charts drawn to scale (a bar's height is its count times one scale per chart, the tallest filling the plot; every bar carries its number; each chart has a title and an accessible description) in the theme's tokens: accent for opened, amber (`--warn`) for closed, red (`--bad`) for overdue and the oldest bucket. Closed bars are also hatched, so opened and closed differ without relying on colour. The week axis labels each week's Friday (DD-MM). Bars meet 3:1 and text 4.5:1 against the card in every theme and mode. Below the charts: the oldest three open items (each opens its panel), risks and issues opened and closed, and milestone movement. A sentence heads the card: "This week: 3 opened, 0 closed; 2 overdue, up from 1; the oldest open action is 36 days old."
- **The weekly report** gets the same line, "Flow: …", first in Progress This Week (`report.flow_line_for`; `weekly-data` has it as `flow`). Word starts with this line; charts in Word can follow. Not in the client report. Per project first; a portfolio view comes with BL-02.

### Dependencies, part 1: no schema change (BL-90)
- **What it uses:** a RAID item of type Dependency whose milestone (`milestone`, the page's `ms`) is set means "this milestone depends on this item". Nothing is stored for the rest; part 2 (a `links` table, attaching to actions and RAID items and across projects) waits for BL-50, so `schema.sql` is untouched.
- **The date warning** (an early warning, signal `dependency`, worked out in `build_dashboard.early_warnings`, never stored; flagged on the dependency in Work, the panel and the project card, in `D.warnings` with `ms`): an open dependency with a due date attached to a Planned milestone with a forecast (or baseline) date, when the dependency is **due after the milestone's date** ("ABC-M-001 forecast 24-10-2026, but dependency ABC-005 isn't due until 30-10-2026"), or is **overdue while the milestone is within `warnings.dependency.near_days`** (14; "Dependency ABC-003 is overdue (due …) and ABC-M-001 is forecast for …"). Settings › Early warnings switches it and sets the days.
- **The Dependencies card** (`depsCard`, right column after Recent decisions, hidden with none): the project's open dependencies grouped by milestone (nearest date first, then "Not attached"), each with ID, text, owner (an outside party is the owner as written), due date (late style when overdue) and its warning; the heading counts them and how many are at risk. The milestone name opens the milestone's panel, a row the item's.
- **Marks:** a milestone blocked by a dependency with a date warning shows "waiting on ABC-005" (all of them, comma separated; the warning text on hover) and an amber edge (`.msrow.atrisk`) on the project page.
- **Suggestions:** when a Dependency is being created or edited with a due date and no milestone, the panel shows up to three chips under the Milestone field, "Attach to ABC-M-002 (forecast 20-11-2026)?", for the project's Planned milestones forecast after the due date, nearest first, and "None needed". A chip only fills the field (you still save); nothing is attached by itself. "None needed" is remembered in this browser for that item until its text changes. Suggestions for other targets (actions, RAID items, other projects) are part 2.
- **Work:** a **Milestone** filter under "+ Filter" on RAID (Attached or Not attached; `ms` in the address), a built-in RAID view **Unattached dependencies** (open, type Dependency, not attached), and "not attached" after the type under an open unattached dependency's title.

## Work board (BL-103, Sprint 4a)

- **Layout:** a **List | Board** switch in the Work toolbar for Actions, RAID and Decisions (the Decisions board is described below). The board is `#workBoard`, beside the list's `#workWrap`; one is hidden. It uses `rows()` and `passes()`, so filters, search, the chips, "Showing X of Y", saved views and the sort are shared with the list. The layout and grouping are remembered in the browser (`creaite-worklay`, `creaite-workgrp`) and are in the address (`&lay=board&g=due`); a saved view keeps `lay` and `g`. Under 640px the List is the default until a layout is chosen.
- **Group by:** Due, Status, Owner, Milestone (`#wgrp`). Starting grouping per tab from settings `work.board.group` (Actions Due, RAID Owner, Decisions Status; Settings › Work and views › Work board). Due: Overdue, This week (to Sunday), Next week, Later, No date, plus a Closed column when closed items are in view. Status: the tab's statuses, Cancelled only when something is in it. Owner: Me, then people by number of items, then TBC. Milestone: the project's milestones in their order, then No milestone; needs one project in the project filter, else the board asks for one (`#bpickP`).
- **Only the controls for the layout in use (BL-114, sprint-polish):** on the List the toolbar shows **Columns** and the key hint `#whintList` (J K move, click to open, E or double-click a cell to edit, click a heading to sort, drag a column edge); on the Board it shows **Group by**, **Sort** and **Hide empty columns** (`#wboardctl`, on a row of its own so nothing else moves) and the hint `#whintBoard` (J K step through the cards, drag a card or use **Move to…**, Esc, /, F). Search, the filters, saved views, the applied-filter chips and **New …** are shared and stay where they are. A control hidden for the other layout is hidden with the `hidden` attribute and a `display:none` rule that a class's own `display` cannot undo, so it is out of reach of the keyboard and screen readers. The layout switch always shows. `test_work_layout_controls.py` walks Actions, RAID and Decisions in both layouts.
- **Closed:** a Closed column shows items closed in the last `work.board.closed_days` (14) days, with **Show all (n older)** and back.
- **Columns:** the header reads "Overdue · 5", collapses to a strip (remembered, `creaite-boardcoll`), and empty columns can be hidden (`#wbhide`). About 270px wide; the board scrolls sideways. Order within a column follows the Work sort; the Sort menu (`#wbsort`: default, due date, priority, last updated, raised) sets the same sort. No hand-ordering, so nothing new is stored.
- **Cards:** title (two lines), ID, project code, client-visible mark, owner initials (full name on hover), due in words ("6 days late" in red), priority (Urgent in bold), the early-warning flag, and a **⋯ Move to…** button. Click opens the side panel; the card stays highlighted; J and K step through cards, column by column.
- **Moving a card** (`boardDrop` → `boardMove` → `commitRecord`, the same record save as the panel's Save): dragging uses pointer events (mouse or pen; touch uses Move to…). Due asks for a date in a small dialog (`#boardDlg`, pre-filled with this Friday, next Friday or two weeks out, the calendar popup limited to today onwards, a past date refused); No date asks to clear it; Overdue and Closed are not drop targets and say why. Status sets the status (closing fills today, reopening clears it). Owner resolves the name against the register (TBC clears it). Milestone attaches or detaches (only the project's own are offered). Each move is one proposal with Undo, applied by the auto-apply setting; the same checks as the form (`validate`, `norm`) run. Esc, or dropping anywhere else, puts the card back.
- **Looks:** cards and columns use each theme's `--surface`, `--sunk` and `--line` in light and dark. The drop target has a dashed outline and the words "Drop to move here"; the dragged card is dimmed with a dashed outline and a lifted copy follows the pointer; motion stops under reduced-motion.
- **Not built:** work-in-progress limits and hand-ordering. (Swimlanes and the Project, Priority, Workstream and Type columns came with BL-129, sprint-work-views.)

## Decisions board (BL-103, sprint-debug-log-2b)

- **Where:** the List | Board switch is on the Decisions tab too (`layoutOf`, `renderLayoutBar`: no longer special-cased). The Decisions parts of the board code are one marked block, `/* ===== Decisions board (BL-103, sprint-debug-log-2b) ===== */`, which wraps `boardCols`, `boardKeyOf`, `boardTargets`, `cardHtml`, `boardMenu` and `boardDrop`; filters, search, chips, saved views (which now keep a Decisions layout and grouping), "Showing X of Y", the sort chooser, J and K, the panel and the hash (`lay=board&g=status`) are the Actions and RAID ones.
- **Group by** (`DGROUPS`, `groupsOf(t)`): **Status** (the default; setting `work.board.group.decisions`, Settings › Work and views › Work board › Decisions board groups by), **Decision maker** (Me first, then people by number of cards, then No one asked) or **Needed by** (Overdue, This week, Next week, Later, No date, and Decided). A decision has no milestone, so there is no Milestone grouping. Columns: Status shows Pending and Made, and Withdrawn only while something is in it.
- **Cards:** the decision's text, ID, project, who is asked (initials; "Asked of …" or "Made by …" on hover), the needed-by date in words ("3 days late" in red, "In 2 days", "No date needed by"; "Made 06-10" or "Withdrawn" once decided), its status and the early-warning flag; ⋯ **Move to…** only on a pending decision.
- **Moving a card** (one change with Undo, as everywhere on the board; a move is for a pending decision only):
  - **to Made:** opens the panel's Record-the-decision form on the same record (made, today, the same number) and writes nothing until Save; closing it leaves the card in Pending;
  - **to Withdrawn:** asks first (the board dialog, "Withdraw"), then withdraws as the panel does;
  - **to Pending:** refused with "A decision that has been made or withdrawn can't go back to pending; record a new one that replaces it" (Pending is not a drop target); a made or withdrawn decision can't be moved at all;
  - **to a Decision maker column:** reassigns who is asked (a person from the people register, as an owner is), or clears it for No one asked;
  - **to a Needed by column:** asks for a date (never a past one; Overdue and Decided are not drop targets), No date asks before clearing it.

## Settings, calmer, and Health: the server side (BL-320, v1.9.2)

The routes and data the page reads; the page itself is the next section. Notes: `dev/archive/notes/v1.9.2/V192-SETTINGS-SERVER-NOTE.md`.
