- **The catalogue** (`system/scripts/settings_catalogue.py`): for every key in `system/templates/settings-defaults.json` (the one home of the defaults; descriptions `_about` and `note` skipped; `prep.series` and lists such as `sweep.ignore` are one setting each), its group, its tier (`essentials`, `advanced`, or `developer` once there are development-only settings), a label, one line on what it does, and the reason for its default, written out. `ESSENTIAL_ROWS` is the Essentials page: 16 rows in six sections (You, Your day, Saving and review, People I follow, Look and help, Safety), a row covering one key or several (What the sweep reads is the five `sweep.sources`; AI drafts is every `drafting` switch). `dev/tests/test_settings_catalogue.py` fails when a default key has no entry or an entry no default.
- **Changed:** a setting's value (from `settings.json`; a missing key is its default) differs from its default; order is ignored for lists where it means nothing (weekend days, Week ahead priorities, short forms, own domains, never-read folders, dismissed reminders, hidden rail pages). `last_changed` is the last `settings-log.jsonl` line naming the key, a parent or a child. Once that log is over 30 days old, `retire_candidates` counts the resettable settings never changed (counts only).
- **`POST /api/settings/catalogue`** (token): `{settings: [{key, tier, group, label, line, reason, default, value, changed, resettable, fixed_why, last_changed}], essentials: [{section, label, line, keys, changed}], groups, changed, essentials_count, advanced_count, retire_candidates, retire_after_days}`. Read only.
- **`POST /api/settings/reset {key | keys}`** (token): each key back to its default through `settings_catalogue.save`, the same steps as the page's `writeSettings`: the file as it was copied to `system/backups/settings-<YYYY-MM-DD-HHMMSS>.json` (`-2`, `-3` when taken), the whole file written with two-space indents, one compact `{at, key, old, new}` line per key in `settings-log.jsonl`. Answers `{reset, unchanged, catalogue}`. A key already at its default writes nothing. Refused (403, nothing written) for a key that isn't a setting or one only set-up changes (`FIXED`: the folders and OneDrive settings, the port, `live_mode.required`, `assistant.enabled`, `excel.export` and the old people and workstream lists); 400 with no key.
- **`POST /api/health`** (token): `health.health()` runs `doctor.py --read-only --json` in its own process (only when ask creaite's read allow list already permits doctor, as BL-166's set-up steps). `--read-only` checks folder permissions instead of writing test files; `--json` prints `{checks: [{id, name, status, detail}], passed, failed, read_only}`. The answer: `{problems: [{id, title, check, severity, detail, fix: {kind, button, would_run, waits_for, how, built: false}}], fine, fine_names, stopped: [{area, count, span, message}], read_only, checked}`: only warnings and failures, failures first, in plain words (`health.PLAIN`; a check it doesn't know keeps doctor's name), the home folder written `~`; what passed is counted; errors that kept coming back and have stopped are listed as stopped (BL-263). No Fix it runs: each would run a command, change a permission, write outside the vault or touch Microsoft 365, so `built` is false and the page shows `how` until Alan decides each (BL-141).
- **Not changed:** `update.py`'s after-update steps.
- **New default (Alan, 14-10-2026):** `day.close_from` is 17:30 (was off). `dashboard.week_ahead_priorities` stays Urgent only (Alan, 14-10-2026, replacing his earlier yes to the mockup's Urgent and High). An update only adds missing keys (`update.merge_new`), so a vault that already has either keeps its own value; only a new vault, or one without the key, gets the new default.

## Archive folders and Health (BL-09, part 1)

- **One name, `_Archive`:** every archive folder creaite knows of (`paths.ARCHIVE`); `paths.skipped_names()` also skips the old `zz Archive` (`paths.OLD_ARCHIVE`) for one release. `projects/_Archive` (or `<projects root>/_Archive`) is never a project folder.
- **Health lines (doctor checks):** `old_archive_folders` (warn) names the folders still called `zz Archive` in the projects root and each engagement or project folder; `skeleton_archive` (warn) says the project skeleton still holds one (a folder listing; creaite never writes in `_Templates`).
- **Fix it for `old_archive_folders`** (`health_fixes.FIXES["old_archive"]`, `card: True`): `POST /api/health/fix/old_archive` (empty body) runs `propose.py old-archives`, which only prints the spec (a `move` per folder, file block `archive: "rename"`); the live server puts it on the change card (`server_edit_card`, the BL-258 card) and answers `{ok, card: true}`; the page opens ask creaite on that card (`pendingEdit`). **Approve** stages and applies it through `tracker.py apply`; Undo moves the folders back.

## Archive and restore a project (BL-09, part 2)

- **Data:** `project_states` ([{code, name, state: active | closed | archived}], from `main.projects`) and `settings.showArchived` (`dashboard.show_archived`, off). Every other list is built through `tracker.hide_archived`: temporary views named like the tables leave archived projects, their items, their links, labels and updates out, unless Show archived is on. The Excel export does the same.
- **Settings › People and projects › Projects and register:** a **Show archived projects** switch, then one row per project (name, state, **Archive**; an archived one, listed only with the switch on, has **Restore**). Archive posts `POST /api/project/archive {code, action: "archive"}`: with open items the answer is `{ok: false, open: [{table, id, text}]}` and the page asks in a dialog (`#parchDlg`): **Cancel them all and archive** (posts again with `cancel_open: true`) or **Stop**. Otherwise the server runs `propose.py archive|restore` and puts the change on the change card (`server_edit_card`); the page opens it in ask creaite (`pendingEdit`). Approve applies it through `tracker.py apply`; Undo reverses it.
- **Search:** an archived project's items are found, `archived: true`, "Archived" in their meta; opening one with Show archived off says so instead.

## Archiving items and reports (BL-10, BL-03; v1.9.5)

- **Items (BL-10), automatic:** once a day (`archive.py daily`: session start after the daily backup, and the live server's daily backup job (`backup_timer`, BL-323) after the backup, the zip and Tidy up, a minute after start-up then hourly, under the job lock, never during an ask creaite run, never in the development repository) closed or cancelled actions and RAID items, and done or dropped promises, closed `archive.after_months.<table>` months ago or more (6), get the BL-09 `archived` flag through one proposal (`source_run: "archive"`, items `{"op": "archive_item"}`) applied by `tracker.py apply`; tracker checks the rule again, so a proposal can't archive an open item, a decision or a milestone. Undo reverses it and leaves nothing in Review. Editing an archived item un-archives it (logged); the daily run then leaves it for a full period from that edit. The views hide archived rows through `tracker.hide_archived` (the item views already filter `archived = 0`); **Show archived projects** (`dashboard.show_archived`, Settings › People and projects › Projects and register) brings them back, each record carrying `ar: true` and its Work row tagged Archived; search finds them marked Archived, and opens an age-archived item of a live project (`/api/records?aged=1`). Client-visible items follow the same rule.
- **Reports and minutes (BL-03), on a yes:** doctor's `archive_reports` warns once a month while any weekly or client report, minutes or Steerco one-pager is dated `archive.reports_after_months` (12) months ago or more (by its file name; transcripts, agendas, documents and ad hoc reports never). Health's line is the detail's first clause, "Archive 14 reports and minutes older than 12 months", with **Archive them** (`health_fixes.FIXES["archive_reports"]`, `card: True`): `POST /api/health/fix/archive_reports` (token, empty body, no query string) runs `archive.py reports`, which only prints the spec (a `move` per file, file block `archive: "reports"`), and the server puts it on the change card (`server_edit_card`); the page opens it (`pendingEdit`). **Approve** applies it through `tracker.py apply`: each file goes to `<engagement>/_Archive/<its folder>/<file>`; the portfolio's minutes (only those) go to `<portfolio>/_Archive/<minutes folder>/<file>` (Alan, 14-10-2026). After an archive in the current month the line is quiet until the next month. `paths.resolve` maps a stored path whose file has moved into its engagement's `_Archive` there, so links still open; `_Archive` stays never-indexed and on ask creaite's never list. Undo moves the files back and removes the folders the move made.
- **Move back one (BL-331; after v1.9.5):** Files has an **Archived** chip (`#fquick [data-q="archived"]`, shown only while `files_archived` holds any) listing the files BL-03 moved that are still in an `_Archive`, from the apply records (`archive.archived_files`: an apply not undone with a `move` of kind `reports`, and no later one of kind `reports_back`; names, sizes and dates only, nothing in `_Archive` is read). Each row is tagged Archived and has **Move back** (`[data-moveback]`) instead of Open folder; no other row has it. `POST /api/files/move-back {path}` (token; only `path`, the stored path) has `archive.back_spec` build one `move` from `<…>/_Archive/<folder>/<file>` to `<…>/<folder>/<file>`, file block `archive: "reports_back"`, and puts it on the change card (`server_edit_card`, `pendingEdit`); **Approve** applies it through `tracker.py apply`. Refused when the original place is taken (never overwrites) or the file isn't one an archive moved (tracker's `check_move` checks the apply records again). `reach.archive_move_problem`'s `reports_back` is the reversed `reports` shape and nothing wider; `classify()` is unchanged. A CHANGE card from ask creaite with a move back is refused, and `propose.py stage` stages one only from the server's own snapshot in `system/guard/`. Once back the apply's search refresh indexes it; Undo puts it back in `_Archive`. `/api/files` returns `archived` beside `files`.
- **Health › Archiving:** the passing details of `archive_items` ("Archived 12 items on 14-10-2026") and `archive_reports` ("Archived 14 reports and minutes on …"), from `health.health()["archiving"]`, listed under their own heading (`#healthArchiving`).

## Diagnostics (BL-102 part 2, stage A)

- **What the page sends:** its own script errors (`error` and `unhandledrejection`) go to the live server's debug log as `message`, `view`, `source` and `line`, each cut at 300 characters, at most 50 a page load and 20 a minute at the server. The server takes out double-quoted text, long single-quoted text, email addresses and paths first. Nothing about what the user was looking at, no element text, no state.
- **The errors banner (BL-180; replaces the top bar's "2 errors in the last day" link):** `#errBanner`, under the header and under `#liveAlert` (errors last), in the page column so never over ask creaite: the warning icon and words ("3 errors in the last day"; a group is one message with its numbers ignored, so a repeat reads "1 error (30 times)"; after a dismissal "1 new error"), the kinds by area ("2 sweep runs failed, 1 live-server request"), **Investigate** (`go("settings","diag")`), **ask creaite to look** (only with the live server: an ask creaite request to read the log and explain, changing nothing) and Dismiss (×). `/api/diagnostics` also returns `groups` (the last day's, each with `area`, `message`, `count`, `last` and its `times`) and `newest`. Dismiss, Investigate and drawing Diagnostics on Settings store the newest error time seen (`localStorage` `creaite-errors-seen`); only errors after it count. `role="status"`, a fade of 180ms (none under reduced motion). Not shown before the log is read, or with nothing unseen. **Beside ask creaite (fix-peek-140):** one line at every width above 600px (the words give way with "…", the whole text in their `title`; the actions, `.eb-a`, never shrink), so the chat opening or closing never changes its height; with the chat open over the page (BL-173) it ends at the chat's left edge (`padding-right` of the chat's width; not when the page already sits beside the chat), and a click in it doesn't close the chat, so its buttons stay where they were under the pointer.
- **Settings › Diagnostics** (`#diagCard`, under Live server and backups, Preferences tab):
  - **Log level:** info or debug (`#diagLevel`), saved to `logging.level` by `writeSettings` (backed up and logged like every setting);
  - **Log folder:** "Open the folder" (`#diagOpen`) through the open route; `system/logs` is allowed there and nothing wider;
  - **Report:** "Copy diagnostics" (`#diagCopy`) copies the version, the health check, the safe settings and the last 200 log lines; if the browser refuses the clipboard the report is shown in a box to copy by hand;
  - **Latest errors:** the last ten of the seven days, each a line (date, time, area, message) that opens to its traceback; Refresh re-reads.
- **Without the live server** the card says the log is written by the server and its controls are disabled.

## ask creaite reads everything (Sprint 4b, item 1b)

- **Reading, always allowed:** `READ_TOOLS` carries every Microsoft 365 reading tool (`permissions.M365_READ`: calendar, mail and chat search, Teams chats and channels, SharePoint and OneDrive search, people, availability, `get_me`, `get_granted_scopes`, `read_resource`), `Read`/`Grep`/`Glob` (the vault, and the projects root through `--add-dir`) and every creaite script's read commands (minutes `key`/`filename`/`filedate`/`new`/`expected`, report `current-context`, sweep `calendar-window`, search `preview`/`context`, propose people and workstreams lists, locations, prep_page `context`/`latest`, and the earlier ones). The vault's `.claude/settings.json` allows the same Microsoft 365 readers.
- **Writing, still confirmed:** `WRITE_TOOLS` are added only to a run Alan confirmed in the panel, which shows what will change first. The transcript runs use this same list: they have no list of their own.
- **Microsoft 365 actions, off:** every send, delete, modify, create, update, respond, move, upload and rename tool (`permissions.M365_ACTIONS`) is in `DENY_TOOLS` for every run. A Microsoft 365 tool creaite hasn't listed is simply not allowed.
- **Doctor:** "Assistant permissions" accepts a reading rule, and flags an action rule, the whole connector or an unchecked tool as broader than creaite asks for; `permissions.broad_rule` refuses the same when ask creaite is asked to add a rule.
- **Still never read:** `_Archive`, `_Templates` and online-only OneDrive files (the existing never-index rule).

## Transcripts in Calendar import (BL-42 phase 3, BL-104 highlight; Sprint 4b)

- **The only route to a transcript, three calls** (`dev/reference/spike-transcripts-2026-10-08.md`): `outlook_calendar_search` finds the event; `read_resource` on `calendar:///events/<id>` gives `onlineMeeting.joinUrl` and `meetingTranscriptUrl`; `read_resource` on that `meeting-transcript:///…` URL exactly as returned (it carries the occurrence's `start` and `end`) gives `transcripts[].content` as WebVTT. SharePoint and OneDrive search are never a fallback. The chat is `teams:///chats/<thread id>/messages` (the thread id is inside the join URL) and each message in the meeting's window is read in full.
- **Statuses** (`transcripts.classify`): *Transcript available* (at least one transcript for the occurrence), *Not transcribed* (an online meeting in the past with a transcript URL and an empty list), *No transcript* (no online meeting, or no transcript URL), *No access* (a permission or not-found error for an online meeting in the past). A future meeting, or a dropped connection after one retry, has no status yet. The organiser comes from `isOrganizer`, never from addresses.
- **The sweep checks them** (cx-sweep step 8b, `sweep.py transcripts todo|record|show`), not Intake: `todo` lists the meetings in the window that are new or still *Not transcribed* (cleared, private, processed and unfinished ones are left alone; in-person meetings are recorded as *No transcript* without a lookup); the sweep reads each event and its transcript URL and `record` keeps `{status, uri, start, end, count, checked, thread}` in `system/staging/transcripts.json`, keyed by event ID. Nothing else can reach that file: no body, passcode, PIN or transcript text. `inbox_meetings()` gives each row `transcript`, `transcript_checked` and `loaded`.
- **Loading is the live server's job** (`POST /api/transcripts/check` and `/load`, `transcripts.Job`): a headless run makes the calls (the same read-only tools as ask creaite, no write tool); the server reads the run's own `tool_use` and `tool_result` events, takes the transcript from the result (inline, or from the file Claude Code spooled it to, read only from Claude Code's folders), tidies it and writes `inbox/YYYY-MM-DD Title.vtt` and `… - chat.txt` itself. Claude never re-types a transcript. The event ID in the result must match the meeting asked for. Already in the inbox, or already processed (`calendar:<id>`), it isn't loaded again.
- **The files:** the `.vtt` has LF line ends, cues in start-time order, speaker names as given, and a `NOTE` block (meeting, date, start, organiser, attendees, event ID); `minutes.py transcript` reads it as before (it skips the whole NOTE block). The chat holds only messages sent during the meeting, in full, without call, recording, join or leave events, HTML removed, local times. Both start with `Event ID: …`, which `minutes.py key` and `filedate` use, so the meeting is `calendar:<id>` once processed.
- **The page:** buttons **Select with transcripts** and **Load into inbox** (the one **Process selected** button, BL-119, loads then processes; there is no Load and process); they are disabled without the live server, while ask creaite is busy, or with nothing loadable ticked); the Transcript column shows the status, "checked …" and a **Check again** link; a **With transcripts · n** chip and **All**. Rows with an unprocessed transcript get a tint (`--tx-tint`, the accent mixed into the surface, at least 4.5:1 for text in every theme and mode), an accent bar and a **Transcript** badge; muted for the other statuses, quiet when processed; a ticked row looks stronger. Intake's file list tags loaded files **From calendar** and lists a chat as "Goes with its transcript".

## Chat and sweep (BL-108, BL-109, BL-110; sprint-chat-sweep, 2026-10-09)

- **The sweep rebuilds the dashboard (BL-110 part 1):** `sweep.py stage`, `calendar`, `done` and `fail`, and `minutes.py stage` and `refresh`, end with `build_dashboard.rebuild()` (never fails the command; one printed note on error). Builds take turns (a lock file in `system/staging/`) and the page is written to a temporary name and renamed, so a reader never sees half a page.
- **Changes appear at once (BL-304, v1.9.4; `core/instant.js`):** an edit through `save()` with `opt.recs` (`commitRecord`: the peek's form Save, a Work cell, the board, the right-click menu; `itemsMove`: the bulk bar, ask creaite's field card) or through the peek's `pkCommit` (the peek's chips and text, ⌘K) changes the record and redraws (`refreshAll`) before anything is written: measured from the edit to the next animation frame it is on screen in one frame (about 28 to 40 ms in headless Chrome, which is one frame's wait). Each change is an overlay in `INFLIGHT` (`{k, id, f}`) until it is applied (or staged, for the peek); `ovReapply()` lays the peek's staged patches and then every overlay over fresh data after a full reload or a targeted refresh, so a live update never clobbers a change still saving. The row shows `.pkdot.saving` (hollow, `data-st="saving"`) until then, and `.pkdot` (`data-st="staged"`) for the peek's staged changes and, with auto-apply off, for a change waiting for Apply (`shared.PENDING[].ids`). The background work is one queue (`saveQueue`, `SAVEQ`) in the order made, unchanged in what it does: `writeProposal` (the same proposal, `source_run: "dashboard"`), then `/api/apply {hold_peek}` with auto-apply, so `tracker.py` validates, logs and applies as before and the bulk rule is untouched. A later change to the same item waits for the earlier one and takes its `based_on` from the item the earlier one left (`opRebase`): two quick edits are two applies, in order. **Failure:** a refused write or apply (`res.ok` false), or an item held as a conflict (its proposal is still in staging after an apply that succeeded: "changed elsewhere"), removes the page's own proposal from staging, puts the record back (`undoIt`, then the overlays still saving), fetches the item again ("reloaded it") and says `Not saved: <reason>` with **Retry** (`save` again with `retry`, which rebases `based_on` on the item as it now is, so Retry is Alan's choice to keep his value). **Undo:** the first message ("… — saving…") already has Undo: before it is written nothing is written; once applied the apply is undone (`/api/undo`, which returns the proposal to Review as pending, as every undo of an apply does); after it is saved the message becomes "… — saved and applied" with the apply's Undo. A caller without `opt.recs` (milestones, workstreams, the register, health, Current state) still waits for the save and reloads all its data, as before; `opt.wait` makes a record save wait too (ask creaite's field card, which names the apply). The peek still stages, never applies on the spot (BL-176); `pkNow` puts the change on screen and `pkWrite` stages it, or puts it back with Retry.
- **Only what changed is fetched again (BL-304):** `GET /api/records?ids=A,B` (token-checked, read only, item IDs only, at most 200) returns those actions, RAID items and decisions in the page's shape (`build_dashboard.records`, sharing `action_row`, `raid_row`, `decision_row` with `collect`), with what an apply always changes: Review (`review`, `review_focus`), Activity, `peek_staged`, `milestone_names`, `data_at` and `latest_apply`. `recordsRefresh` merges it into `A`, `R` and `DEC` in place (the same objects; an ID in `missing` is removed), redraws, and takes the pulse's fingerprint so the page's own change isn't reloaded again. The live-updates stream's `changed` event carries `ids` ({table: [IDs]}) when the tracker changed: the server reads the change log's rows since its last check (`change_log_ids`; IDs only, never values; none past 100 items). The page (`evOn`, `evFlush`) fetches just those items for a tracker or Review change; the calendar, a table other than actions, RAID and decisions (projects, milestones, workstreams…), an event without IDs (a gap in the stream sends every part and no IDs), a failed fetch and a server restart fall back to the whole of `/api/data` through the pulse, as before; while Alan is typing it waits, as the pulse does. `RECSTAT` counts targeted and full refreshes. Not yet narrower: a refresh still redraws the current view (`refreshAll`), not only the rows that changed, and derived blocks the server builds from the whole tracker (Today's week ahead and day plan, early warnings, the meeting hub) refresh at the next full reload.
- **The open page refreshes itself (BL-110 part 2):** `POST /api/pulse` (token) returns `{v, pending, swept}`; `v` is a hash of file stamps only, `pending` the Review count. The page asks on the health-check interval (`live_mode.health_check_seconds`) and when the tab is shown or focused; a changed `v` calls `reloadData()` and shows "n new item(s) in Review" only when `pending` rose. It never reloads over an edit: with the side panel's form, the Current state editor, a dialog or a focused text field it shows the top-bar chip "New items arrived: refresh" instead, and reloads when the edit ends (checked every 2 seconds, locally) or on a click when nothing is open. A reload Alan's own action caused takes a fresh fingerprint so it doesn't repeat. No polling for a dashboard opened as a file, a hidden tab, or a stopped server. **BL-253:** served, the page follows one change stream, `GET /api/events` (fetch with the token; `hello`, `changed {seq, parts, v}`, `ping`), and the pulse runs only while that stream is down; `files` reloads just `/api/files`, `vip` just `/api/vip`, `tracker`/`review`/`calendar` go through the pulse's check. A tab hidden 10 minutes closes the stream. **BL-269:** once a reply says stale, the page sends no more `/api/` requests until reloaded.
- **Markdown in the chat (BL-109):** `mdChat(text)` draws creaite's replies (live `text` events and History alike) after escaping every character. Blocks: headings (three sizes), paragraphs (a single newline is a line break), nested bullet and numbered lists, quotes, rules, fenced code (monospace, scrolls sideways, **Copy** button), GitHub-style tables (scroll inside the bubble; no separator row means plain text). Inline: bold, italic, strikethrough, code, links. Links are `https:`, `http:` and `mailto:` only, opening in a new tab with `rel="noopener"`; images show their alt text; an item ID in the tracker opens that item; a path in the Files list opens the file. Unfinished fences and tables are closed for display. Rendered bubbles use `white-space: normal`; Alan's bubbles, the greeting, notices and the Files search answer keep their own rendering. Styles use the theme tokens in every theme and mode.
- **Formatted text everywhere (BL-196):** `mdChat(text, {hBase})` is the one renderer for text from a file or a run; `mdView` wraps it in `.mdv`, which shares the chat's styles (`:is(.msg.md,.mdv)`) and its clicks (`mdClick`: a code block's Copy, a file, an item). `hBase` puts the text's headings under the page's (`aria-level` = level + `hBase`). Formatted: the release notes in Settings › System › creaite version › Review (`hBase` 2, in a scrolling box, with **Copy the notes**, which copies `p.notes` as written), a run's `CONFIRM:` text on its card (`confirmCard(…, md)`), and a `.md` file's preview in Intake (`#inPrevMd`, `hBase` 2; other files keep `#inPrevText`). Already formatted: chat replies, Current state, its sources and search answers (`mdHtml`, kept for the editor's round trip through `htmlToMd`), Help (built from `help.md`). Plain on purpose: Alan's bubbles and notices; an update's command output; the Prep invite body (mail text); a transcript or Word file's preview; work item titles, descriptions and latest updates (edited in place, stored as typed); Review rows; the Activity log; the refused command's fold-out (code).
- **Times on bubbles (BL-108):** under each of Alan's bubbles and creaite's replies, a `<time datetime>` on that bubble's side: `14:32` for today, `08-10 14:32` for an earlier day, the whole `08-10-2026 14:32:05` on hover. A reply's time is when it finished (none while it is written). Where a chat spans days, a thin divider reads Today, Yesterday or DD-MM-YYYY before the first bubble of each day. Steps, confirm cards and the greeting have no time. `remember_chat` stores `at` on both messages (Alan's: when sent; creaite's: when the reply started, kept on later updates) and `done_at` on the reply, to the second on the laptop's clock; the run's `done` event carries `done_at` too. A chat saved before this has none per bubble and one divider with its `started` date.

## Select, open and edit in the Work lists (BL-122 to BL-124; sprint-polish)

- **Highlight (BL-122):** `HL` (id → type), with the anchor `HLA` and the cursor `HLC`, is a view setting only: nothing is saved. A click on a row of the Work table, a Work board card or a person's page (`hlRowClick`) highlights that row alone; Cmd/Ctrl-click toggles it (`hlPick`), Shift-click highlights the range from the anchor in the visible order (with Cmd/Ctrl it adds the range). A click on the **ID** (`a.rowid` in the title's second line, the ID column's `td.idc`, a card's `.bid`) opens the sidebar and makes that row the only highlight; so does a double-click anywhere on the row (`hlRowOpen`, which still focuses the cell's field as before) and Enter. A link or control in a row (`a`, `button`, `input`, `[data-proj]`, `[data-person]`, `[role=link]`, early-warning flags) does its own job and highlights nothing. Under `mousedown`, Shift stops the browser selecting text.
- **Not the open record:** `.sel` is still the record open in the sidebar; `.hl` is the highlight (a tint, a 4px edge bar on the first cell and a "✓" before the title, so colour is never the only cue), `.cur` the cursor (a dotted outline). Both can show on a row (`tr.sel.hl` is stronger). With one row highlighted and the sidebar open, a plain click on another row moves only the highlight; the sidebar follows an ID click, a double-click or J/K. Two or more highlighted close the sidebar (not while it is being edited).
- **State:** cleared on a tab switch and when the view leaves Work and the person page; after every render (`hlPrune`) the highlight keeps only rows still in the list, so it survives filters and sorting for the rows that stay in view, and a reload of the data drops rows that no longer exist. Rows carry `aria-selected`; `#workTbl` is `role="grid"` with `aria-multiselectable`.
- **Keyboard (`hlKey`):** J/K move the cursor and replace the highlight with it (the sidebar follows when it is open and not being edited), Shift+J/K extend from the anchor, Space toggles the cursor row, Enter or E open it, Esc clears the highlight and then closes the sidebar. A focused link or button keeps Space and Enter for itself.
- **Review:** `RHL` (the highlighted rows) and the tick boxes (`CHK`) are one selection: a highlighted row that may be ticked is ticked, and ticking highlights. A click highlights (repainted in place by `rvPaint`, so a double-click still lands on the row), Cmd/Ctrl and Shift add, a double-click or Enter opens its detail, Space toggles, Esc clears. **Approve selected** and **Reject selected** act on the ticked rows only; a highlighted item that needs individual approval (`indiv`: minutes, reports, health changes, new decisions, decisions recorded or withdrawn, new risks) is never ticked, so the bulk rule is unchanged, and the message says how many were left out.
- **Edit in the table (BL-123):** an editable value is a `td.ce` with a `.cebtn` (▾, `aria-label` naming the field and the item; opacity 0 until the row is hovered, highlighted, the cursor row or the button focused; tabbable only in the cursor row, the roving tab stop set in `hlPaint`). `CE_FIELDS` lists them: Actions status, owner, due date, priority, workstream; RAID status, owner, type, priority (its cell is `data-f="imp"` and edits impact and likelihood together as two lists), due date, workstream; Decisions status (only while Pending: Made opens the Record form as on the board, Withdrawn asks first), made by, date (only once Made). A click on the value (not on the owner's link) highlights the row (unless a highlighted group of two or more contains it), then `ceOpen` swaps the cell's content for the control that `fieldsFor` defines for the sidebar: a list (`ceSelect`), the people register with TBC for owners, the project's own workstreams, or the calendar input (`#ceDate`, `data-pick`). A list saves on `change` (it is opened with `showPicker`); the date saves on Enter or when the calendar sets it (a plain `Event`, where typing is an `InputEvent`). Esc, clicking away (`focusout`) or Tab leaves it as it was (Tab goes on to the next ▾). The save is `ceApply` → `boardMove` (BL-103's: `ownerFinal`, `norm`, `validate`, `commitRecord`): one proposal, auto-apply as set, Undo in the message, an Activity line with the item's text; a refusal shows its message and the cell is redrawn as it was. Closing fills today's date and reopening clears it, as in the sidebar. A double-click on the row opens the sidebar in its reading view (editing in the sidebar is E or Edit). Not editable here: free text, ID, project, client-visible, and milestone (it has no column; it is changed in the sidebar or by BL-124's bar).
- **Several at once (BL-124):** `#wbulk` (above the list or the board, drawn by `hlBar`) shows when two or more rows are highlighted: the count, **Close** and **Reopen** (not for Decisions), and a list or the date picker for each field in `bkFields()`: Actions status, owner, due date, priority, workstream, milestone; RAID status, owner, due date, impact, likelihood, workstream, milestone; Decisions only **Made by** (a withdrawn decision keeps its maker). A field that doesn't apply to every selected item stays in the bar disabled with a `title` giving the reason (workstream and milestone belong to one project: "These items are in different projects"; "These items are of different kinds"). `batchMove(k, ids, patch, what)` resolves an owner once (`ownerFinal`), checks every item with the sidebar's own rules (`prepMove`: `norm` and `validate`), leaves out any that fail (named in the message: "Left out: ABC-A-009 (Closed can't be before Raised)"), skips items that already hold the value, and saves the rest as **one proposal** with one Undo, so one apply and, in Activity, one line per item with its text. If nothing can take the change it saves nothing ("Nothing changed"). Over 25 items it asks first (`boardAsk`). The highlight clears afterwards. A cell edit of a highlighted row (`ceApply`) sends only the fields that differ from that row to every highlighted row, and says how many ("Owner set to Omar Ali on 3 items"). No delete is offered. Not built: the bar on a person's page (it mixes Actions and RAID); Review's bulk rule is separate (BL-122).
- **Not built here:** the project page's short lists (they are links to an item, not rows of a table) keep opening the item on a click, and the Today lists are unchanged.

## ask creaite's running and finished badge (BL-139; sprint-final)

- **The badge** (`#runBadge`, `role="status"` `aria-live="polite"`, fixed at the bottom right: 20px from each edge, 72px up under 760px; z-index 46, under dialogs): a main button (`#rbMain`, opens the chat) with the icon (`#rbI`), the words (`#rbT`), a label (`#rbL`) and the clock (`#rbE`, `aria-hidden`, so it is not read out every second), and a × (`#rbX`) once the run has ended. State is `RB.state` (`running`, `done`, `stopped`, `failed`; empty is hidden). Words: "ask creaite is working…" with the label (what is running, where known: `RUNLABEL`, which Process selected sets to "Processing 3 meetings", `jobLive` sets to "Loading a transcript" or "Checking a transcript", else `rbGuess(message)`: weekly or client report, one-pager, calendar refresh, sweep, prep, a meeting), the clock "1:24"; then "ask creaite has finished" and "took 1:24", "ask creaite was stopped" and "ask creaite stopped with a problem" ("after 1:24"). The icon is a pulsing disc while running (`mrbp`, removed under reduced motion), ✓, ■ or !, in `--accent`, `--ok`, `--muted` and `--bad`: the state is never colour alone.
- **Every run:** `askStream` (a chat message, Process selected, `jobLive`, a sweep or refresh, a report or document, a reattached run) calls `rbStart` and ends with `askFinished(outcome)` (`stopped` and `error` events, an abort or a failed start set the outcome; a 409 cancels); `runGen` (Generate and Make it executive), `prepPage` and `askClaude` (a search answer) are wrapped. The VIP check the server makes by itself is not shown.
- **It stays:** the finished state has no timer. It goes when × is pressed, the chat is opened (`openChat` calls `rbDismiss`) or the next run starts. A run that ends while the chat is open leaves nothing (the chat showed it). While the chat is open the badge is hidden by CSS (`body:has(#chat.on)`) and returns if the panel is closed during the run. It replaces the 15-second `#askDone` note.
- **The tab:** a run that ends while `document.hidden` puts "✓ ask creaite finished · " in front of the title (`RBMARK`, kept by `vipBadge`) until the tab is seen (`visibilitychange`), and uses a browser notification only if `vip.notify` is true and permission is granted. Nothing leaves the laptop.
- **Motion (BL-136):** it enters as the banners do (`@starting-style`, opacity and a 6px rise with `--d2` and `--out`); its pulse stops under reduced motion (`data-reduced`, Motion: Reduce) and runs under Full. The suite runs with reduced motion. The top-bar dot (`#askBtn.working`) stays.

## ask creaite keeps your place (BL-120; sprint-polish)

- **One rule, one helper:** `chatScroll(force)` replaces the four `scrollTop=1e9` assignments (the reply writer `say`, `sayMd`, `addEl` and the bubble time in `stampMsg`). The chat follows new content only while it is at, or within 12px of, the bottom. The state (`CHFOLLOW`) is kept from the log's `scroll` events, since content added below does not move the view; `chatNear()` measures. Scrolled up, nothing moves the view and **Jump to latest** (`#chJump`) appears over the foot of the log, reading "↓ New activity", or "↓ Latest · still working" while a run is going (`RUN` or `BUSY`).
- **The button** is a real `button` with the classes `btn primary`, so every theme's own primary button supplies its colours (checked at 4.5:1 in all five themes, light and dark, by `test_chat_scroll.py`). It sits in `.chlogbox` (the log's box), centred, 12px above the log's foot. Pressing it, or scrolling back to the bottom, resumes following and hides it.
- **Always the bottom:** Alan's own bubbles (`msg u`) and the first line added to an empty log (a new chat, a chat opened from History, a reattached run, the greeting). Opening the panel scrolls to the bottom only while following.
- **Not changed:** the content, the markdown drawing, the time stamps, Stop, reattaching a run.

## Executive Current state (BL-106)

- **Generate dialog:** gains **Audience** (Internal, or Client-safe: only client-visible items in their client wording; the sources are switched off and the page says so) and **Length** (Short about 40 words, Standard about 150, Fuller about 250). Both are remembered in the browser (`creaite-gen-aud`, `creaite-gen-len`); Internal and Standard are the defaults. The server validates them.
- **The material and the prompt:** `report.py current-context` opens with the writing guide (`executive-style.md`), the audience and length, and the ranked facts sheet (`status_facts.py`); the open lists and files follow as reference. A client-safe context holds nothing internal.
- **Notes under the draft:** after a draft arrives, and on "Check again", `/api/current/check` runs `status_checks.py` and the page shows its notes (headline length and position, item numbers, a date or number, a pending ask, length, sources not in the facts). They never stop a save. "Facts it was written from" shows the sheet.
- **Make it executive:** the "Make it executive" item of **AI ▾** in the editor's action row (`#curAi`, `[data-exec]`; BL-301 D: it was a button of its own). It sends the left pane to `/api/current/rewrite` with the remembered audience and length; the result appears in its own box ("Executive version") with notes on any date or number it lost or added, and replaces the text only when Alan chooses **Use this version**. **Discard** leaves the editor as it was.

- **Meeting prep page and popup (BL-126, sprint-prep-files):**
  - **Work items:** `prep_page.select_items` lists only what is **Urgent**, **overdue** (judged from the meeting's day) or **due within `review.due_soon_days`**; a *Decision needed* when it is due soon or overdue, a *Decision to revisit* when its revisit date has come. Order: overdue (oldest first), then due soonest, then Urgent with no date; `MAX_ITEMS` caps it. The invite ranking only gives the "why"; Claude is given only the listed items. A line under the table says how many other open items are not listed (`ctx.not_listed`) and links to Work for the project; with nothing listed the page says so.
  - **Reporting:** per project, a heading with the health word and icon (✔ On track, ▲ At risk, ✖ Off track; never colour alone) and "as of" the weekly update's date, the Current state's headline, its first five points (bold lead-ins kept), the weekly update in a line or two, and the whole Current state behind a closed "Show the full current state". `prep_page.md_blocks` handles paragraphs, bullets, numbered lists and bold only; everything is escaped. A Portfolio meeting gets one compact block per project with the points behind the disclosure. The page stays one self-contained file with a print style.
  - **Recent reports:** `build_dashboard.recent_reports` (in `D.prep.reports`, by project): the latest weekly report, client report and steering one-pager (at most three, newest first; ad hoc left out; only files that exist and can be read; an online-only file is flagged `online` and not linked). The popup's **Recent reports** section opens a row with `/api/open`. The saved page cannot POST, so its links go to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#openfile=<projects/… path>`: on load (or `hashchange`) the dashboard clears the address, refuses anything outside `projects/`, `inbox/` or `system/staging/drafts/` or with `..`, and otherwise calls `/api/open` once (which checks again, including that the file exists); a refusal says "Can't open that file".

- **Agendas in Files (BL-135, sprint-prep-files):** `paths.project_files` returns the files of `paths.folder(code, "agendas")` as their own kind, **`agendas`** (`sub` is the folder path below Agendas), whether or not documents are walked, and `_excluded_dirs` keeps the folder out of the documents walk, so nothing is listed twice (both layouts: `agendas/` and `02 Meetings/Agendas`). `build_dashboard.files` passes the kind through (the Portfolio keeps only its minutes and transcripts, so it has no Agendas folder). The Files tree shows **agendas** beside minutes and transcripts, with its own count, the folder filter and the All files and project counts include it; Latest reports and This week's meetings are unchanged. Search keeps indexing them: `tracker.STORED_KIND` stores an agenda as a `documents` row, so it stays a "Document · internal" result and the include-documents switch still governs it. Passive: never extracted.

## Settings, calmer, and Health: the page (BL-320, v1.9.2)

Branch `v192-settings-ui`. The page side of the routes above; notes for Team 1 in `dev/archive/notes/v1.9.2/V192-SETTINGS-UI-NOTE.md`.

- **Four places** (`SETCATS`): **Essentials** (opens first), **People and projects**, **Advanced** and **Health**. The sections keep their old category in `cat`; `CATOF` maps general, work, review (the old Health and warnings: thresholds and Early warnings) and capture to Advanced, and system to Health, so a section another module adds (Set-up from today.js, AI drafts from drafts.js) moves without touching that module. Old addresses still resolve (`SETOLD`: prefs → Essentials, general/work/capture → Advanced, system → Health; `#settings/follow` is the People I follow card).
- **Components** (dev/COMPONENTS.md): Essentials, Health and Advanced's "Everything else" are Preact components in `src/components/settings/` (`Essentials.jsx`, `Health.jsx`, `MoreSettings.jsx`, and `SettingRow.jsx`, the row frame with the dot and Reset). Their state is `src/stores/settings.js`: `catalogue`, `query`, `changedOnly`, `health`, `healthBusy`, `healthError`, `healthCount`. A section with `mount` is drawn into its own box (`data-mount`); the old code never writes into it (search, Saved and the dots skip it), and renderSettings unmounts the boxes before it redraws.
- **Essentials:** the catalogue's `essentials` rows in their sections, each with its line and a control per key: text (name; HH:MM for the sweep, the weekly time and Close the day, empty meaning off), tick boxes in a named group (weekend days, the five sweep sources, the Week ahead's priorities), selects (catch up: Start it, Ask first, Don't; weekly day; theme Follow my Mac, Light, Dark; style from `THEMES`; motion), switches (save at once, tell me when they write, the backup) and one switch for every AI draft kind (mixed when some are off). A save goes through `writeSettings` (backup and log), then the page's copies are brought up to date (`setSynced`: `S`, the follow settings, the draft switches, theme and motion, a data reload for the Week ahead, Close the day and the weekend) and the catalogue is reread. With save at once off, the row offers **Apply n waiting**. Without the live server the Essentials say they need it.
- **Advanced:** the older cards under their old names as group headings (You and the look, Work and views, Review and warnings, Capture and alerts), with the Essentials' rows taken out (your name, theme, mode, motion, save at once, the sweep's time and sources, the weekly reminder, the Week ahead, the follow switch and its interval, the daily backup), then **Everything else**: every catalogue setting that is neither an Essential nor on an older card (`covered`: the `SKEY` keys, `warnings.*`, `dashboard.rail.*`, the ignore rules, `vip.notify`, `logging.level`, dismissed reminders), by catalogue group, with a control fitting its default's type (limits kept from the old Advanced card), its default and why, the dot and Reset. A set-up-only setting shows its value and `fixed_why`, with no control. A Developer group appears only when a developer setting exists. The old Advanced card is gone; its settings are here.
- **The dot, Reset and Show changed only:** a dot on any row whose setting differs from its default; on the older cards it is added after each draw (`setMark`, from the row's control: `data-k`, `data-vipk`, `data-drsw`, `data-wk`) with a Reset button (`data-reset`). Reset posts `/api/settings/reset` and redraws from the answer's catalogue. **Show changed only** (under the search box) works like a search across Essentials and Advanced, showing only rows with the dot. A save on an older card rereads the catalogue a moment later.
- **Search** also matches the catalogue's line, reason and old key in Everything else.
- **Health:** checks on opening (once a visit) and on **Check again**; "Checking… (a few seconds)" while it runs. One line per problem: the title, its severity in words (Needs fixing, Worth a look), the detail, and **How to fix** (a disclosure with `fix.how`, and **Copy** when it contains a `system/scripts/run …` command). Where `fix.built` is true (health.py asks `health_fixes.fix_for(check id, detail)`; the fix is `health_fixes.describe(id)`), its `does` line and its button (`fix.button`): `POST /api/health/fix/<id>` with no body and no query string; the answer `{ok, fix, message[, url]}` is said, a `url` (Microsoft 365) is opened, and Health checks again. `standalone` (the icon and Microsoft 365, which no check sees) are listed under "If you need them". A closing block says what every update now does by itself. Stopped errors are listed as stopped; the passes fold into "n checks fine". Below it: the live server, Set-up, Diagnostics (Copy diagnostics), the version and Stop.
- **The rail's count:** `healthCount` is the last check's number of problems, kept in this browser (`creaite-health-count`, with its time) and refreshed by a check a minute after start-up when the last is over a day old. `showHealthCount(n)` in the store is the hook for v192-sidebar's `setRailCount("settings", …)`.
- **No VIP label** on the Essentials or the People I follow card (BL-288).

### Backups in Health and Settings (BL-323, v1.9.3)

- **Doctor's lines, so Health's:** `offsite_backup` (the OneDrive copy: a warning, with the `offsite` **Make it now** fix, from two days without a copy; it now copies the day's zip), `backup_check` (the weekly restore check from `system/backups/backup-state.json`: a warning naming the problem and what to do, `backup.py check`), and `tidy_up` (information only: "Tidy up, 14-10-2026: removed 23 files, 410 MB"). doctor only reads the state; `backup.py` writes it.
- **Health's Backups block:** `health.health()` adds `backups`, the three lines above when they pass; `Health.jsx` shows them under **Backups** with one sentence on what leaves the laptop (the daily zip of the database backup, settings, staging and history; never chats, the engine or the live database). A failing one is in the problems list instead.
- **Settings:** the Essentials row "Daily backup, and a copy in OneDrive" says what the zip holds and what never leaves; the retention values (`backups.daily_days`, `weekly_days`, `copy_days`, `snapshot_days`, `file_change_days`, `releases_kept`, `update_backups_kept`) are Advanced rows under Safety, from the settings catalogue, each with its default and why.
- **Restore from a backup** (`#backupRestore`, Alan, 14-10-2026): **Choose a backup…** posts `/api/backup/list {}` (names, days, where, sizes; one per name, the laptop's copy first: `backup.choices()`); each row's **Preview** posts `/api/backup/preview {zip}` (a dry run: nothing changes) and opens a confirm card (`#backupConfirm`) listing what will be replaced; **Restore** posts `/api/backup/restore {zip}`. The routes are token-checked like every POST, accept a body of exactly `{"zip": name}` with a name from the server's own list (anything else is 400), take no query string, run under the job lock (and not while an ask creaite run is in progress), are logged, and rebuild the dashboard after a restore. ask creaite never holds the page token, and `backup.py restore` stays a record command. cx-setup's "restore creaite from backup" stays.
- **The daily job:** the live server runs the daily backup, zip, OneDrive copy, Tidy up and restore check a minute after start-up and every hour (each step once a day), never while an ask creaite run is in progress and never in the development repository. Every headless run it starts carries `CREAITE_HEADLESS_RUN=1`, so a `session.py start` or `backup` an ask creaite run calls never runs Tidy up.

## Settings page (BL-115; sprint-settings)

*Since BL-320 (v1.9.2) the six categories below are four (Essentials, People and projects, Advanced, Health); see the section above. The rest still describes the older cards, now under Advanced and Health.*

Replaces the Preferences and Data admin tabs with one page. Code is in the `/* ===== Settings page (BL-115, sprint-settings) ===== */` section of `dashboard.html`; the sections' own code (early warnings, ignore rules, VIPs, Diagnostics, versions) stays beside what it draws.

- **Layout:** a search box and the category list on the left, the chosen category's sections on the right (`#setcats`, `#settingsBody`), and the Help card below. Under 700px the list becomes a drop-down (`#setcatsel`) above the settings. Every theme in light and dark.
- **Categories:** General (You, Appearance, Saving), Work and views (Opening views, Work board, Project page, Files), Health and warnings (Review and health, Early warnings), Capture and alerts (Sweep with its ignore rules, VIP alerts, Reminders), People and projects (People, Workstreams, Projects and register), System (Live server and backups, Diagnostics, creaite version, Advanced, and Stop the live server at the bottom).
- **One list:** `SETSECTIONS` is the one place the page is built from; `renderSettings` and the four wrappers it used to have are gone. An entry has `id`, `cat`, `title`, `kw` (search keywords) and `render()` (the card's contents; with `raw` the whole card), and optionally `elid` (the card's element id), `wide` and `after()`. A later feature adds a section by adding an entry. A test checks that every setting the page shows is in exactly one category.
- **Rows:** label with its help text beneath, the control on the right, short labels. **Saved:** a quiet "Saved" beside the row that was just changed, for about 2.5 seconds, with the existing snack. How a setting saves is unchanged: its key, the backup, the log entry and the live server's checks (`writeSettings`).
- **Addresses:** `#settings/<category>` (`general`, `work`, `health`, `capture`, `people`, `system`) opens that category; `#settings/<section>` (`sweep`, `diagnostics`, `live`, `advanced`…) opens the section's category and scrolls to it. `STAB` still works as an alias: setting it to a category, a section, or an old value (`prefs` is General, `admin` is People and projects, `people` and `projects` are People and projects) picks the category. A bare `#settings` opens General (or the last category used); an unknown one opens General. The live dot opens System at Live server and backups; the status line's "n errors" opens System at Diagnostics. Each category is a history entry, so Back returns to the last one.
- **Search:** `#setq` filters across every category as you type. A section matches by its title or category (all its rows show), by its keywords or by a row's label and help text (then only the matching rows show, or all of them when only its keywords matched). Every word typed must be found; case is ignored. Results show where each one lives ("Capture and alerts › Sweep"); Esc clears; no match says so. Advanced opens while a search is on.
- **Advanced:** collapsed, in System: the assistant run timeout, log size and days kept, Calendar import's look-back (`monday` or a number of days), Generate days, and the search options. Values are read from `settings.json` when the section draws; a value outside its range is refused. Never `live_mode.required`, `assistant.enabled` or the permission rules.
- **New row:** General › You › Own email domains (`identity.own_domains`, a comma-separated list, lower-cased).
- **Not changed:** the keys, the live server's routes, and the People, Workstreams, Ignore rules and Diagnostics managers (they moved).

## Work views and Review triage (BL-128 to BL-133; sprint-work-views)

Code is in marked sections of `dashboard.html`: `BL-128` to `BL-132` after the Work board, `BL-133` and `BL-133 stage B` in Review. The designs are `dev/mockups/work-filtering-mockup.html` and `dev/mockups/review-triage-mockup.html`.

- **Display (BL-128):** `#dispBtn` beside the List | Board switch opens `#dispMenu` (role dialog, `D`): Layout; Group by (`#wgrp`: the List has None first; the Board's columns); Sub-group (`#wsub`); Order by (`#wbsort`, every sortable field, with `#wordRev` to reverse; reversing the default order is `SORT={col:"_def",dir:"desc"}`); Properties (the old Columns menu: `#dprop-<key>` and ↑ ↓; Milestone and Last updated are new columns; a card shows the first four, `cardProps`); Show (`#wshowempty` on both layouts; `#wclosed`, the Closed column's window, on the Board only). Reset (`dispReset`) returns the tab's display to its default and keeps the layout. Esc and Done close it and return focus to the button. The menu is always drawn (hidden), so its controls exist for the address and tests. The removed controls: `#wboardctl`, `#colBtn`, `#colMenu`, `#wbhide`. The hint lines (`#whintList`, `#whintBoard`) are built by `workHint(layout)` from `HINTS`.
- **Groups (BL-129):** `GROUPS[t]` (Actions: Due, Status, Owner, Milestone, Project, Priority, Workstream; RAID: Type for Workstream; Decisions: Status, Decision maker, Needed by, Project). `wgGroups(t,g,L,empty)` orders and labels them (Owner: Me, then by count, then TBC or No one asked; Project by name; Priority by rank; Type in the schema's order); `wgKey` places an item; `wgPatch` is what a move into a group changes (Project and RAID priority can't be set, Status on Decisions keeps FR-110's rules). List: `listBody` draws `tr.wgh` header rows (`data-gk`; a ▾ button, a tick, the count and the overdue count) and the rows carry `data-g1`/`data-g2`; collapse is `creaite-listcoll` per tab, grouping and value. Board: `boardCols` takes the new keys; with a sub-group `renderBoard` draws `.blane` lanes of `.bcol[data-col][data-lane]` cells (lane collapse in `BCOLL`). `laneDrop` makes a column-and-lane move one `boardMove` after `boardAsk` names both changes (a date column asks in its own dialog, naming the lane change); Move to… adds a lane section (`data-blto`). The List's grouping is `WLGRP` (`creaite-listgrp`), the sub-groups `WSUB` (`creaite-worksub`, per tab and layout); the address carries `lg` and `sg`.
- **Selection with groups:** a collapsed group's rows aren't drawn, so a Shift range and J/K never reach them, and `hlPrune` drops them from the highlight (a snack and the bar say how many). `#wbulk [data-bq=all]` ("Select all N matching") sets `HLALL`: the highlight then keeps every matching item, collapsed or not, and the bar says "7 selected (3 in collapsed groups)".
- **Filters (BL-130):** `+ Filter` (`#moreBtn`, `F`) is a field picker (`drawPicker`; `#fpq` search, `[data-fpk]` fields, the main five first, then More fields and the date ranges; the chosen field's is/is not, options with counts, "Result: N of M shown"). `NEG` holds the fields set to "is not"; `passes` inverts them; `snapshot`/`applyFilters` store them as `!key`; the address takes `!key=`. Chips with `.fchip.neg` say "not". New fields: `sl` Stale (`isStale`: open, no change for `staleDays`; a decision's last change, else its date), `lu` Last updated and `rd` Raised (last 7, 14, 30 days; not on Decisions, which have Date), `rr` raised between, `ms` Milestone on Actions (and RAID's gains the milestones themselves). Today's and the project page's links into Work are unchanged.
- **Empty state and count (BL-132):** `#whidden` follows `#wcount` (" · 14 closed hidden": the Status filter on the List, the Closed window on the Board). `emptyHtml` names the filters (`activeFilters`), counts each removal (`passes(x, key)`; `q`, `dr`, `rr` too), offers `[data-relax]` Remove, Include closed items (`withClosed`) and Clear all filters. `#wlive` (polite, `role=status`) announces the count or the empty state when it changes; `#wapplied` is no longer a live region.
- **Saved views (BL-131):** `WB[tab]` is the view the current state came from; `syncView` keeps it while it matches (`viewMatches`: a saved view compares its filters and the display it stored; a preset its filters only). A changed view's chip has a dot and words (`.vdot`, "(changed, not saved)"); the applied row offers `#updView` (saved views) and `#saveView` ("Save as new view"); the address carries `vb`. A saved view's `[data-vmenu]` opens Rename (inline `#vrename`), Move earlier, Move later and Delete. `renderViewChips` moves chips that don't fit into More views (`#vmore`, `#vmoreMenu`) and always keeps the view in use in the row; Overdue on Actions starts there. New preset Chase on each tab. Decisions' default view: `defaults.decisions_view`.
- **Review (BL-133 stage A):** the toolbar is a search (`#rvQ`), Kind, Needs, Source and + Filter (`#rvPick`, the same picker layout, with is / is not; Project and Age), Display (`#rvDispMenu`: group by project, source or kind; order as proposed, oldest, kind or due) and Approve all routine with its count. `rvRender` draws the groups (`rvBlocks`; minutes keep their items under them; a group tick `.rgtick`). The bar (`#rvbar`): the count, the split (`rvBulkOK` and `rvWhyNot`, named on hover), Select all, Approve (`#bulkApprove`), Reject (`#bulkReject`, and `#bulkRejectWhy`), and Set project, owner, due date, workstream, milestone (`rvSet`: `editProposal` per file, tagged edited, checked per item by `rvCheck`, one Undo; a project choice offers one learning card, one signal per sender). `rvDecideMany` writes each proposal file once and applies once; `rvSummary` writes `#rvresult` (role status) with Show them and Undo. `rvPreview` (`#rvDlg`) for Approve all routine and any batch over `RV_MAX` (25). Rows carry the stale tag, Open source (`rvSrcLink`) and the cursor moves on after a decision (`rvAdvance`, `review.auto_advance`). Shift+J/K, X (Space), Cmd/Ctrl+A, Cmd/Ctrl+Z. An empty queue shows `rvEmptyHtml`. A reload remaps the selection by file and item (`rvKey`), because review numbers change.
- **Review (BL-133 stage B):** a row's ▾ (`[data-act=rr]`), the bar's ▾ and Shift+R open `#rvReason` (Duplicate of the hint, Not mine to track, Not an action, Other with a text box; keys 1 to 4); `setDecision` writes `reject_reason` and `duplicate_of`. `build_dashboard.duplicate_hint` gives `r.dup`, shown as a tag on the row and in the panel with Reject as duplicate (`[data-rvdup]`). Activity shows rejections that carry a reason (`build_dashboard.rejected_activity`).
- **Rejects, in real life (BL-315, v1.9.2), server side.** The seven reasons (`tracker.REJECT_REASONS`: Not an action, Duplicate, Chatter, Already done, Wrong project, Doesn't need tracking, For another team) or none; a typed reason in any case is stored as listed, and BL-133's own words still pass (one line, 200 characters). Routes (token-checked, through `review.py`, only the proposals change; the page applies after them as after any decision): `POST /api/review/guesses` → `{reasons, guesses: [{n, file, ref, reason, why, of}]}` (creaite's guess per pending row, plain code: Chatter for a thank-you or no-owner scheduling, Duplicate of an open look-alike (BL-221's mark or `extraction.best_repeat`; off with Spot repeats), Already done for a closed look-alike or a close of an item already closed, Doesn't need tracking for an action with no owner); `POST /api/review/reject {rows: [{file, ref}], reason, duplicate_of | guess: true}` → `{rejected: [{file, ref, key}], gone}` (one or many, one reason, each row's own guess, or none; each reject is stamped `rejected_at`); `POST /api/review/rejected {rows, how: "undo" | "reason" | "approve", reason, duplicate_of}` → `{done: [{key, was: {decision, reason, duplicate_of}}], open: [keys]}` (pending or applied rejects, named `<file>#<n>`; `open` lists those approved only on their own, back in Review to be opened); `POST /api/review/rejects {limit}` → `{items: [{key, file, ref, applied, project, table, kind, text, reason, duplicate_of, at}]}` newest first (Activity › What you rejected). An applied reject decided again goes back to its proposal in staging marked `redecide` {fingerprint, apply_id}; the next apply removes (approved) or updates (rejected again) its `rejected` row, logged, so "undo last apply" puts it back. `rejected_activity` lists each reject once (its insert), with its current reason, and leaves out one back in Review. Quality counts each item's latest decision and reason (`quality.REASONS`: the seven, plus BL-133's "Not mine to track").
- **Rejects, in real life (BL-315, v1.9.2), the screens.** One reasons menu, `components/review/RejectMenu.jsx` (Preact; its state in `stores/rejects.js`: `rjReasons`, `rjGuesses`, `rjMenu`, `rjGone`, `rjRecent`), mounted once in `#rjMenuHost` and drawn as `#rvReason` (`role="menu"`, named "Reject n because", "Rejected because" or "Duplicate of which item?"). It opens from the bar's `#bulkReject` ("Reject with a reason" with its R; `#bulkRejectWhy` is gone), a row's ✕ (`[data-act=r]`, `aria-haspopup`) and ▾ (`[data-act=rr]`), R and Shift+R (`rjKey315`: the highlighted rows, else the cursor row; minutes, reports and file changes are rejected as before), the row's menu ("Reject with a reason…", Shift+F10), the peek's `#pkReject` and One at a time's R. Keys: 1 to 7 the seven, G Use each guess (disabled with no guess), 0 No reason, ↑ ↓ Home End, Enter or Space, Esc (focus back to the opener, which carries `aria-expanded`), Tab or a click away closes. A row's ✕ or ▾ acts on the selection when the row is in it (`rjWith`). Duplicate takes the item a "Looks like" or a guess names; otherwise, for one row, it asks: `/api/review/candidates`' look-alikes, an ID typed (`#rjDupId`), or "Don't name it". `rjLoadGuesses` fetches `/api/review/guesses` once per set of pending rows (`rjGuessFor`) and each row shows `.rvguess` "guess: Chatter" (with "(looks like ABC-A-009)" when it names an item). `rjReject` sends `/api/review/reject` (one call per reason and item; `{guess:true}` for G), marks the rows, applies (`applyNow` with auto-apply) and says one line in `#rvresult` and the message, with Undo (`rjUnreject`: `/api/review/rejected {how:"undo"}`, every row back, highlighted). The rejected rows stay as `tr.rvgone[data-gone]` (no `data-n`, so no cursor number), placed by `rjPlace` where they were (under their minutes, or in proposal order), with the chip `.rjchip` "Rejected · Chatter" (`[data-rjchip]`, a button that reopens the menu: `rjRereason`, `/api/review/rejected {how:"reason"}`, with Undo sending each `was` back), **Approve instead** (`[data-rjok]`: `rjApproveInstead`, `{how:"approve"}`, then apply; `open` keys, approved only on their own, go back to Review highlighted and the first proposal opens; Undo undoes that apply and rejects them again with their reasons) and ▾ (`[data-rjrr]`). Right-click or Shift+F10 on one (`cxGone`): Change the reason…, Approve instead, Put back in Review. `go()` leaving Review clears them (`rjLeave`). Activity › Quality › **What you rejected** (`#qRejects`, `components/review/RejectsList.jsx`) reads `/api/review/rejects` when Quality opens (`rjLoadRecent`): each line's text, project, kind and when, the chip, Approve instead and Undo. Without the live server a reject still writes its reason into the proposal (BL-133's `RVREASON`); guesses, changing a reason and Approve instead say they need the server.


## All items (BL-134; sprint-all-items)

- **The tab:** a fourth Work tab, **All items** (`data-t="all"`, `WT==="all"`, address `#work=all…`), after Decisions. Frame 7 of `dev/mockups/work-filtering-mockup.html`.
- **The read model:** `allRows()` copies every action, RAID item and decision into one list (rebuilt on each `renderWork`). Each row carries `k` (its table), `type` (`ALL_TYPES`: Action, Decision, Risk, Issue, Assumption, Dependency; one list, so promises can be a seventh), `st` (the bucket: Open = Open, Delayed, Pending; Closed = Closed, Made; Cancelled = Cancelled, Withdrawn) and `nst` (its own word, which the cell shows). A decision's `o` is its Made by, its `due` its Needed by (a made decision's is its Date), its `raised` its Date, and it has no priority (blank, sorted after Low). `items()` returns it for `all`; the shared filter, group, count, empty-state and saved-view code reads `x.st` and so works unchanged.
- **Default order:** overdue first, then due soonest, undated last (`DEFSORT.all`).
- **Columns** (`COLS.all`): Item, Type (a tag with the word), Owner, Due, Status by default; Priority, Project, Workstream, Source, Last updated and ID in Display › Properties. Each cell's `title` says which tab it is edited on.
- **Filters** (`FDEFS.all`): Status (three buckets), Due, Owner, Project, Priority on the toolbar; Type, Stale, Last updated and Raised under + Filter; Due between and Raised between. Workstream, Response, Milestone, Source and Client-visible are not offered.
- **Display:** List only (the List | Board switch and Display's Layout are hidden; `setLayout('all',…)` does nothing and `lay=board` in an address is ignored). Group by Project, Type, Owner, Due, Status (the buckets), Priority (with No priority); Order by adds Type and Project.
- **Views:** All open (the default), Mine, Due soon, Chase, Overdue (under More views), Closed; saved views in `work.saved_views.all`.
- **Read-only:** no cell editing, no bulk bar, no New button. A click highlights; the ID, a double-click, Enter or E opens the item's own panel (`openRecord` resolves `all` to the row's table with `kOfId`). Rows carry `data-k`.
- **Links in:** the project page's Actions card has an **All items (n open)** link (`data-nav="all:open:CODE"`). Today, ask creaite and search are unchanged.

## People in two tabs (BL-138; sprint-all-items)

- `renderPeople` (in the BL-138 section) draws a tab bar `#ptabs` above `#peopleTbl`: **With work n** (`PTAB="work"`, the default) and **Nothing assigned n** (`PTAB="none"`). `peopleTabs()` splits `PERS()` without hidden people (`isHiddenPerson`): With work has an open action, an open RAID item or a pending decision where they are asked (`dw`), and always You (first); Nothing assigned is everyone else (the register and anyone whose items are all closed).
- **Columns:** With work: name, organisation, role, projects, open actions, open RAID, decisions waiting, overdue (actions and RAID, as before). Nothing assigned: name, organisation, role, projects (from all their items), closed items (closed actions and RAID plus made and withdrawn decisions; a count). "not in register" as before. Empty: "Everyone creaite knows has something assigned." Each tab keeps its own sort (`PSORTT`; With work by overdue, Nothing assigned by name).
- **Address:** `#people` is With work, `#people/none` Nothing assigned (`parseHash`, `hashOf`, `navState`, `go` wrapped). The tab is remembered in this browser (`creaite-peopletab`), so `go("people")` with no tab (the rail, the breadcrumbs) returns to the last one. Left and Right arrows move between the tabs.


## Motion (BL-136; sprint-motion)

Motion explains a change or confirms an action; it never decorates. Design: `dev/mockups/motion-mockup.html`. Code: the
`BL-136 (sprint-motion)` sections of `dashboard.html` (one CSS block at the end of the stylesheet, a helper block after the
settings, and a hooks block at the end of the script).

**House rules**
- **Three durations, three curves**, as tokens on `:root` (every theme inherits them). An exit takes about 75% of its entrance.
- **Only opacity and transform** (and `grid-template-rows` for a collapse). Never width, height, top or left across a list; the
  detail panel's width is the one exception until Stage B replaces it. Lifts and presses use the `translate` and `scale`
  properties, so an element's own `transform` (a centred button) is kept.
- **One-shot, not looping.** The only loops: the spinner, the ask button and the run badge's spark (BL-139) while a run is alive, and (Stage C) the sweep hairline,
  the typing dots and Today's "now" ripple. Nothing loops while the page idles.
- **Never delay the user.** Animation runs beside the state change. Nothing waits for `transitionend` or `animationend`; any
  clean-up is a timeout that cannot hang. Remove every BL-136 section and the dashboard behaves exactly as before.
- **No motion on bulk or live updates:** more than about 12 rows (or more than one chip) changing, a re-render, another tab,
  a saved view that changes several chips, a live refresh: all swap at once.
- **Reduced motion means no movement.** One `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` rule (and the same rule under
  `data-motion="reduce"`) collapses every transition and animation to 0s; `reduced()` does the same for scripted effects
  (`anim()` runs for 1ms, `countUp` shows the final number, `mScroll` scrolls instantly). Colour may still change.
- **Colour tokens only**, so all five themes, light and dark, animate in their own colours. Colour is never the only cue.

| Token | Value | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `--d1` | 120ms × `--k` | press, hover, tick, small colour changes |
| `--d2` | 200ms × `--k` | menus, chips, tints, the focus ring, the theme fade |
| `--d3` | 320ms × `--k` | panels, collapse, reorder, the sliding indicator, the switch |
| `--d4` | 520ms × `--k` | a status colour change |
| `--out` | cubic-bezier(.2,.7,.2,1) | arrivals |
| `--inout` | cubic-bezier(.65,0,.35,1) | moves and toggles |
| `--in` | cubic-bezier(.4,0,1,1) | exits |
| `--k` | 1 | a multiplier for tests and the console's "slow" check (`document.documentElement.style.setProperty('--k',3)`); never a setting |

**The Motion setting** (Settings › General › Appearance, `dashboard.motion`: `System`, `Reduce` or `Full`; default `System`).
It is applied as `data-motion` on `<html>` with the theme (`applyMotion()`), and `data-reduced` marks the effective state for
the few rules that keep things still rather than merely instant. Full overrides the system for creaite's own motion; the
spinner, essential state, still follows the system. Tests and Help screenshots use Reduce.

**Helpers:** `reduced()`, `anim(el, keyframes, opts)` (Web Animations; `opts.pseudo` for a pseudo-element), `countUp`, `flip`,
`slideDown` (opacity, transform and clip-path, no height), `mScroll(el, opts)` (named so as not to replace `window.scrollTo`),
`themeFade()`, `copied(btn)` and `mSay(text)` (the polite live region `#mlive`).

**Stage A (built):** press, hover and focus on `.btn`, `.mini`, `.iconbtn`, `.vchip`, `.fsel` and `.chipbtn`, with a 3px
`--accent-soft` ring on focused inputs (`:where()`, so an input's own shadow wins; the focus outline is unchanged). The sliding
indicator (`#wtabs`, `#wlay`, `#setcats`) exists only while it moves: `mSlide` copies the selected tab's own colour from the
theme, glides from the old tab, then removes itself, so it can never sit in the wrong place after a resize or a count arriving.
A newly highlighted row's tick draws in (`.mtick`, keyed by row id so a re-render draws nothing); the change-several bar and
Review's bar rise with `@starting-style` and leave at once. One filter chip added scales in; one removed leaves a text-less
copy fading where it was (outside the chip row) while the others close up. The Settings switch slides by transform with a small
overshoot; a setting being written shows a spinner, and only a save that worked shows the drawn tick with "Saved". The ask
buttons sweep once on hover. Copy buttons swap to the tick and "Copied" for 1.4s and say it in `#mlive`. Changing the theme or
mode (Settings, the moon button, Cmd+K) cross-fades colours for about 300ms (`.themefade`); never on load, and not
when code calls `applyTheme()` itself (so the contrast tests measure settled colours).

**Stage B (built):** menus and popovers (200ms in from 4px above at 97%, 150ms out, `@starting-style` and
`allow-discrete`), dialogs (97% to 100% with a 4px lift and the backdrop, 320ms in, 200ms out), the detail panel's content
(320ms from the right, sections at 40ms steps, at most 8; the width snaps), the toast (rises 320ms, an Undo timer line, leaves
200ms, one at a time), a decided Review row tinting in place, sort (FLIP, 320ms, up to 30 rows; never the Board), List group
collapse (rows glide, the chevron turns) and `<details>` easing open, the Board drag's lift and target wash (the move itself
instant), Review cleared (one circle and tick, once per clearing), row hover and the J/K cross-fade (instant for a held key or
a range), the skeleton (only after 150ms), the page change (the new page rises 4px over 200ms), scroll-linked header shadow,
progress line and Prep reveal (CSS timelines, `@supports`), the landing pulse (`landOn`, from Today, search, Show them and
Open links; a collapsed group opens first) and banners (after load only).

**Stage C (built):** the new-since-last-visit dot (Work with All items and a person's tables, Review, Today's Needs
attention; fades after 2s on screen or when opened), count-up (700ms, once per page per load), progress bars and sparkline
bars (staggered, once), the changed-value flash (more than 12: one line, said by `#mlive`), a health change's ring, Today's
"now" ripple (paused while the tab is hidden, as is every animation), the run hairline, ask creaite's typing dots and word
fade (live replies of up to about 400 words), Today's staggered first paint (under a second), the spotlight (one card,
pointer devices), the ask input's turning border (only while focused) and the empty-state icon's two floats.

**ask creaite's approval card (BL-141):** the card rises in (320ms), its changes follow at 40ms steps (at most 8), and
"Approved" shows the drawn tick only when the changes were made. The What ask creaite changed list rises in once; a row just
undone flashes.

## The UX baseline (BL-146 to BL-157; sprint-ux)
- **Every text box grows with what is typed (BL-146).** One helper, `autoGrow(el,max)`, made from BL-145's `chFit` (the chat box now calls it with its own maximum; there is no second copy). Every `textarea`, present and future, starts at its own minimum (CSS `min-height` or `rows`) and grows with its content up to 60% of the window, then scrolls inside with the cursor in view; it shrinks back as text is removed. It is re-measured on input and paste, whenever code sets `.value` (the `HTMLTextAreaElement.prototype` setter: a record opened, a draft restored, Clear), when a textarea is added to the page or shown or changes width (a `MutationObserver` and one `ResizeObserver`), and on a window, theme, density or font change. The drag handle stays where a box had one; a height dragged by hand becomes that box's minimum. Height never animates (BL-136). Not changed: the Current state editor (rich text), BL-145's rules for the chat box.

## The UX baseline (BL-146 to BL-157; sprint-ux)
- **Dates typed in plain words (BL-147).** One parser, `parseWhen(text,today,due)`, behind every typed date field (`isDateField`: `[data-date]`, `placeholder="DD-MM-YYYY"`, `#qcDate`, `#bdDate`, `[data-und]`; read-only fields stay picker only). It takes `today`, `tomorrow`, `yesterday`; a weekday (`fri`: the next one, never today; `next fri`: the one after); `+3d`, `+2w`, `+1m` (also `3 days`, `in 2 weeks`); `next week` (Monday), `end of week` (Friday; today on a Friday), `end of month`; a day and month (`15/10`, `15-10`, `15 oct`, `oct 15`: this year, or next year when a due field would be in the past; due fields are `due`, `fc`, Review's and the change-several bar's due date, the move-date dialog, the No due date card); and DD-MM-YYYY (also `/` or `.`, a two-digit year, or YYYY-MM-DD). While a date field has focus `#dateHint` (fixed, under the field, `role=status`) says what it understood ("Friday 15-10-2026"), or in words with examples that it didn't, or that the day is outside the field's `data-min`/`data-max`. On Enter, a change or leaving the field (capturing listeners registered before every other date handler) the field is set to DD-MM-YYYY, so every existing check and save reads DD-MM-YYYY as before. The old "Dates are DD-MM-YYYY" messages now say "Not a date creaite knows. Try “fri”, “+3d”, “15-10” or 15-10-2026." Quick capture's date is typed too (no longer read-only; Process refuses a future day); its calendar button opens the popup. In a typed field ↓ opens the calendar, and in a picker field that can be typed in, Space types (it no longer opens the calendar).

## The UX baseline (BL-146 to BL-157; sprint-ux)
- **ask creaite's conveniences (BL-148).** (1) **Copy** on every reply: `.msgcopy` (an icon, `aria-label` "Copy the reply") is added to each `.msg.a` by a `MutationObserver` on `#chLog` (so streamed and loaded replies get it), sits on the bubble's bottom-right edge, and is shown on hover and on focus (`:focus-within`); it copies the reply's plain text (`innerText`, without its buttons). (2) **↑** in an empty box recalls the last message Alan sent in the chat on screen, ↑ again goes back, ↓ forward and past the newest empties the box; a box with typed text is left alone, and the suggestions keep the arrows while their list is open. (3) **Edit** (`.msgedit`) on Alan's last message only puts its text back in the box with the cursor at the end; sending continues the chat (no branching). (4) **Try again** (`.retry`) after a stopped or failed run (Stop, an error, the server gone, a run that couldn't start) sends the same message again. (5) The unsent draft, its text and attached images' names, is kept per chat in `localStorage` (`creaite-ask-drafts`, the ten most recent chats), saved as typed and on leaving the page, restored after a reload or on returning to that chat (with a note asking to attach the images again: they can't be kept), and cleared on send. It never reaches the server.

## The UX baseline (BL-146 to BL-157; sprint-ux)
